## [2026-09-06] build | `claims_lint.py` — the code-side sibling of wiki_lint §9
- Code: **`tools/claims_lint.py`** (new). Run: `python tools/claims_lint.py`.
- **The gap it closes:** wiki_lint §9 catches wiki phrases that go false when CODE ships. Nothing caught code phrases that go false when a BACKTEST ships — and a docstring cannot fail a test. Three such claims were found by hand this week (HT caption quoting the retracted +2.5%, `smallcap_discovery`'s "UNMEASURED … until then", `gamma_exposure` still calling itself a vol-regime input after B-44r retired it). All were prose written BEFORE a measurement and never revisited AFTER it.
- **Two sections:** promissory claims ("until then", "needs a backtest", "has not been through") and hardcoded figures (p-values, % edges, pp deltas) to be checked against [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] by a human.
- **Parses with `ast`+`tokenize`, does not grep.** It sees only string constants and comments, so `st.number_input(..., step=0.5)` and slider bounds never appear — ~40 lines of noise removed without one exclusion pattern.
- **The first run was the design input.** Bare "unmeasured"/"unvalidated" matched ~40 lines, every one CORRECT — this repo uses them as honest state labels. Dropped both: what dates itself is the promise of future work, not the admission of present ignorance. A lint that is mostly right is one you learn to skim, which is the exact failure being prevented. Now 11 + 28.
- **Suppression names its evidence.** `# claims-lint: checked B-NNx` — there is no bare silencer, because "I looked at it once" is the state that produced the bug. Valid anywhere inside an implicitly-concatenated caption.
- **Reports, does not fix, exit always 0.** Same principle as wiki_lint's `sources: N`: a number rewritten confidently by a script that cannot read the backlog is worse than the stale one. Also reconfigures stdout to UTF-8 — it quotes the prose it scans, and cp1252 killed it on a warning glyph on the first run, the same way it killed a finished 42-scanner `opportunity_daily` run.

## [2026-09-06] docs | Hidden Treasures caption carries B-34r4's number, not B-34's
- Code: `dashboard_app` (Hidden Treasures caption), commit `d6c9dd7`. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r4).
- The page still advertised **"+2.5% @60d (p=0.22)"** — the exact figure B-34r4 retracted as a drifting-promoter-window artefact. The one place a reader actually meets this lane was quoting the bug.
- Now reads **−0.12% @60d (p=0.94) / +0.20% @120d (p=0.94)**, cites **B-34r4** rather than B-34, and states that the old number was the artefact — so a reader who remembers +2.5% sees where it went instead of assuming a typo.
- Small change, but it is the difference between a desk that measured a null and a desk that *shows* one. The correction was only in the wiki until now.

## [2026-09-05] build | B-34r5 - the red-flag gate becomes a mark; the Hidden Treasures thread closes
- Code: `dashboard_app` (veto default OFF, per-card flag marker). Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]].
- **Measured on the live scoring path, not the backtest:** ranking WITHOUT the veto, the first governance-flagged name lands at rank **128 of 249** (Smallcap-250) and **60 of 250** (Microcap-250). The page's "Show top N" maxes at 100, so the gate could reach **at most one visible name, usually none**.
- **Structural, not luck:** the score already costs -15 for promoter selling and -20 for pledge, so a flagged name cannot climb into the top ranks. The veto was re-applying as a hard cut a judgement the ranking had already made - which is also why B-34r2's two veto arms were byte-identical and B-34r3's fix measured near zero.
- **Shipped:** toggle defaults off; flagged names are MARKED on their card with the specific flag rather than removed. Strictly more information than a veto - name, score and reason at the point of use. Verified: Microcap-250 returns 100 hits with INOXGREEN marked at #60 for equity dilution.
- **The whole thread, in order:** owner asked whether a value-trap gate existed -> there was no gate of any kind (the caption's "pledge veto" was a -20 penalty) -> added FR-4 as a hard gate, which vetoed 40% -> demoted FCF to a penalty -> found the veto's window was drifting (B-34r3) -> found the SCORE's window drifted too and **the lane's entire measured edge was that artefact** (B-34r4: +0.91%/+1.47% -> -0.12%/+0.20%) -> made FCF testable point-in-time and measured it (p=0.80, and it lowers the 120d hit rate) -> and finally found the surviving gate cannot reach the visible list at all.
- **What Hidden Treasures is, after all that:** a coverage lens on small/micro-caps the large-cap funnel structurally cannot see. Every claim beyond coverage has now been measured to zero.

## [2026-09-05] measure | B-34r2 closed - FCF veto tested point-in-time; governance veto found redundant
- Code: `--fcf-veto` in `hidden_treasures_backtest`. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r2 measured, **B-34r5** opened).
- **The FCF question is answered, and the demotion stands - now for a measured reason.** Point-in-time FCF veto is the best arm on mean edge (+0.69% @120d vs +0.20% no-veto) and it is still nothing: **p=0.80**. It also LOWERS the 120d hit rate to 52.8% against a 53.6% baseline - edge up while hit rate falls means a few big winners, not more winners. No case for restoring the hard veto; none for dropping the penalty.
- **Both veto arms returned byte-identical numbers, which I checked rather than published.** It is correct: **zero** of the FCF-eligible top-25 carry a governance flag in ANY of the 7 quarters. The score already penalises the same signals (-15 promoter selling, -20 pledge, +10 steady/adding), so a governance-flagged name cannot reach the top 25 anyway and the veto only removes names far below the cut. That also explains B-34r3's near-zero effect.
- **Consequence worth stating plainly:** the governance veto does not improve the shortlist and cannot, on this evidence. It still earns its place as a display of what was excluded and as protection in a thin universe, but describing it as a quality gate on the visible list would be wrong. Filed as **B-34r5**: should it be a gate at all, or just a display?
- Full arm table (bounded score, Smallcap-250, top 25, 6 quarters): no veto -0.12%/+0.20%; governance +0.05%/+0.33%; FCF +0.22%/+0.69%; both +0.22%/+0.69%. Nothing approaches significance.

## [2026-09-05] measure | B-34r4 - Hidden Treasures' edge WAS the bug; plus fcf_years/shares_years for point-in-time
- Code: bounded promoter window in `smallcap_discovery.score_from_cache` + `hidden_treasures_backtest`; `fundamentals_deep._period_ends` exposing **`fcf_years` / `shares_years`**. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r4 measured, B-34 headline marked superseded).
- **The score had the same unbounded window as the veto.** A cumulative promoter drop measured oldest->newest over an ever-lengthening cache made names look progressively worse as history accumulated, so the ranking was partly sorting on an artefact. The institutional term never had this - it already divides by (len-1) into a pp/quarter rate.
- **Bounding it removes the lane's edge entirely.** Same universe/window, one session: unbounded score + no veto = +0.91% @60d / +1.47% @120d. **Bounded score + no veto = -0.12% / +0.20%** (p 0.94), hit rate 56.0% against a 56.5% baseline - at or below a random pick. With the veto: +0.05% / +0.33%. Every positive number this lane ever showed traces to that one defect.
- **What survives is coverage**, which is what B-34 always claimed as the primary value: small/micro-caps the large-cap funnel structurally cannot see. What does not survive is any claim that ranking them by institutional conviction beats the same universe at random. B-34's "+2.51% / +6.29% WEAK" carries a superseded callout - measured on the same defect.
- **Also shipped, and it unblocks the FCF question:** `fundamentals_deep` was calling `.tolist()` on statement rows and throwing away the frame's column dates, so `fcf_history` was five numbers with no idea which years they covered. `_period_ends()` keeps them as `fcf_years` / `shares_years`, aligned by position. That is what makes an as-of evaluation possible at all - no new source, no scraping, no ToS question. Owner had offered trendlyne.com; declined because the labels were already in the payload being discarded, and because scraping today's page still would not give point-in-time history.
- Still open: actually running the FCF-veto test now that the labels exist (needs a filing-lag assumption on top of fiscal year end).

## [2026-09-05] fix+measure | B-34r3 - bounded the promoter window, and it retracted B-34r2's conclusion
- Code: `red_flags._PROMOTER_WINDOW_Q = 4` (both promoter paths), matching bound in `hidden_treasures_backtest`. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r3 shipped, **B-34r4** opened, B-34r2 marked superseded).
- **Fixed at the source, not in the lane.** Both promoter checks compared oldest-vs-newest across the whole cached series, so the flaw was the module's and `dashboard_app`'s Analyzer inherited it too. Detail strings now name the span actually compared, so a verdict cannot be read without knowing its window.
- **Veto rate is stationary where it can be:** the tail went 22/24/26/28% -> 20/21/20/19%. The residual ramp in the first two quarters is NOT the same defect - those quarters hold only 2-3 quarters of history, so the window is short, and a 2pp drop is HARDER to reach in 2 quarters than in 4. That bias runs toward not flagging on thin history, the opposite of the original defect.
- **The re-measurement retracted the earlier conclusion.** With the bounded window the 120d edge is **+1.46%** against **+1.47%** for no veto at all - indistinguishable. B-34r2's headline +2.15% was substantially an artefact of a gate that tightened as the cache deepened. Corrected verdict: the governance veto is **NEUTRAL** - it does not hurt and does not measurably help. Keeping it is now a governance-risk argument, not an edge argument, and the earlier "directionally positive" recommendation is withdrawn.
- **Deliberately out of scope (B-34r4):** the SCORE's promoter term (-15/+10) still uses the unbounded delta and has the same drift. Changing it moves the ranking and would invalidate B-34's baseline - a re-measurement, not a bug fix.

## [2026-09-05] build | B-34r2 resolved - negative FCF demoted from veto to a 12-point penalty
- Code: `smallcap_discovery.RF_DEMOTED` + UI copy. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]].
- **Vetoed 100 -> 72, kept 149 -> 177.** Veto causes are now promoter 51 / pledge 28 / dilution 5; FCF is gone from the list. 51 kept names carry the FCF flag as a penalty instead. **SAILIFE**, the highest-scoring name in the universe, is back in at 64.9 (76.9 less 12) having been removed entirely.
- **Why FCF and not the others:** it caused the majority of exclusions, it is the one critical flag B-34r2 could NOT backtest (no year labels on `fcf_history`, so no as-of evaluation without hindsight), this lane has no valuation input so it is not strictly a value screen, and it was removing JSWCEMENT and GRSE on what is plausibly a capex cycle. The two governance vetoes stay - they measured harmless and directionally positive AND were testable point-in-time.
- **Two implementation traps, both hit and fixed:** (1) the demotion must be computed from the flag LIST, not `counts["critical_flagged"]`, which still includes `negative_fcf` - gating on the count would have silently ignored the demotion; (2) demotion must apply to the CRITICAL grade only, because `red_flags` scores a single negative-FCF year as WATCH and charging that the full 12 is harsher than the source module's own reading. Caught on SUNTV (1-year dip): 50.9 -> 56.9 once it took the watch 6.
- **Honest status:** this is a judgement made because the measurement was impossible, not a measurement. Whether FCF *should* veto is still unanswered and needs a point-in-time fundamentals store.

## [2026-09-05] measure | B-34r2 - the Hidden Treasures veto, backtested as far as the data allows
- Code: `hidden_treasures_backtest.py --gov-veto`. Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r2 half-measured, **B-34r3** opened).
- **Result: WEAK, same class as B-34 itself.** Smallcap-250, top 25, 6 quarters. No veto: +0.91% @60d (p=0.54) / +1.47% @120d (p=0.54). Governance veto: **+1.21% @60d (p=0.42) / +2.15% @120d (p=0.39)**, hit rate 59.3->60.7% and 58.4->60.0%, Q+/Q 3/6->4/6 at 60d. Every metric moves the right way; nothing is significant. It does not hurt and cannot be shown to help.
- **Only the smaller half was testable.** FCF and dilution cause **64 of the 100** live vetoes, but `fcf_history` / `shares_history` arrive from yfinance as bare value lists with NO year labels, so they cannot be truncated to "as known at Q". Applying today's FCF streak to a 2024 entry vetoes a pick for something that had not happened yet. **The headline question - should negative FCF veto - is NOT answerable with current data**; it needs a point-in-time fundamentals store.
- **The run exposed a flaw in the veto itself, which the LIVE page shares.** The veto rate is not stationary: 3% at 31-DEC-2024 rising to 28% at 30-JUN-2026, because `_check_promoter_holding_nse` compares oldest->newest across however many quarters it is handed. A 2pp drop over 8 quarters is much weaker evidence than over 2 and the check cannot tell them apart, so the gate quietly tightens as the ownership cache deepens. Filed as **B-34r3**: bound the window (fixed trailing 4 quarters, or per-quarter like the score's `rate` term) before reading anything into the veto rate.
- **Also found, not fixed:** `--enrich` has never done anything. The eval loop calls `_score_at_quarter(sym, q_date, enrich=False)` hardcoded, so the parameter is accepted and dropped - B-34's published +2.51% was measured on the ownership score alone regardless of the flag.
- **Recommendation pending B-34r3:** keep the two governance vetoes (harmless, directionally positive, real rationale); demote negative FCF to a penalty - it drives most exclusions, is untestable here, and removes JSWCEMENT and GRSE on what is plausibly a capex cycle.
- Both arms share an identical baseline (+4.61 / +6.84) and n(top)=150, confirming the veto touched only the picks and the basket filled every quarter.

## [2026-09-05] build | Hidden Treasures had NO gate - FR-4 red flags wired in as the first hard one
- Code: `smallcap_discovery.py` (+`_red_flags_for`, veto in `discover`), `dashboard_app.py` (toggle + vetoed-list UI). Updated: [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-34r closed, **B-34r2** opened).
- **The question was "is there a value-trap gate?" and the honest answer was "there is no gate".** Every factor was a soft score nudge - including the one the page called a *pledge veto*, which in code was **-20 points**. A name with 40% pledge and promoters distributing scored ~27 and stayed in the universe.
- **Fix was wiring, not writing.** `red_flags.py` (FR-4) already existed and was imported by three other modules but not this lane. It now runs at **zero extra network cost**: same `fundamentals_deep` payload the quality overlay already fetches, promoter/pledge fed from `ownership_cache`, `sessionid=None` so the screener.in path stays dormant.
- **The obvious gate would have emptied the list.** `no_critical_flags()` returns False for every symbol by design - auditor/related-party/contingent-liability items live in filing prose nothing here parses and are permanently NOT_CHECKED. The real gate is `counts["critical_flagged"]`: a critical check that ran AND failed. Recorded in the code comment so the next reader does not repeat it.
- **Measured, Smallcap-250: 100 of 249 vetoed (40%)** - 59 negative FCF, 51 promoter stake reduction, 28 pledge, 5 dilution, 37 tripping more than one. **SAILIFE, the top-scoring name in the universe at 76.9, is vetoed** on two years of negative FCF; so are JSWCEMENT and GRSE.
- **I do not think the default is settled, so it is a toggle and every exclusion is listed.** Negative FCF is the biggest cause and is often a reinvesting small-cap's business model; this lane has no valuation input at all, so "value trap" may be the wrong frame - its real failure mode is an ownership trap. Filed as **B-34r2**: decide it by re-running `hidden_treasures_backtest.py` with and without the veto, not by opinion. Same experiment shape as B-34/B-49/B-50.
- Also corrected the page caption ("pledge veto" -> "pledge penalty") and put B-34's measured result (+2.5% @60d, p=0.22 - coverage, not edge) on the page instead of only in the backlog.

## [2026-09-05] measure | B-44r - the convention was the suspect; it is now eliminated, and GEX retires
- Built: `tools/gamma_magnitude_backtest.py`. Updated: [[concepts/Gamma-Exposure-GEX]], [[analyses/Open-Backlog]].
- **The backlog was wrong about the cost.** It said B-44r "needs a fresh 480-session pass over the raw chains". It does not: the quantity the both-sides-short convention needs is `gross_gex_cr` - the sum of absolute gamma notional - which `compute_gex` has persisted since day one. Zero downloads.
- **Pre-registered, then falsified.** Hypothesis: dealers short gamma on both sides, so the sign is constant and only MAGNITUDE predicts, bigger book -> HIGHER forward vol. Result: high gross gamma goes with **LOWER** forward vol, both indices, every horizon. NIFTY -2.47 pp (p_rot 0.042), BANKNIFTY -4.27 pp (p_rot 0.004) on the primary 5d test. The expected-null directional control behaved (p_rot 0.77 / 0.57), so this is the market, not a broken harness.
- **Then most of it turned out mechanical.** Black-Scholes gamma carries 1/sigma, so high vol MAKES low gamma, and vol clusters: corr(gross, trailing vol) = -0.44 / -0.24 against corr(trailing, forward) = +0.43 / +0.49, whose product is most of the raw -0.29 / -0.17. This confounder should have been pre-registered and was not - it was added post-hoc and is labelled as such in the module docstring.
- **The stratified test is what closed it.** Controls halve NIFTY to insignificance (p_rot 0.029-0.067); BANKNIFTY survives them, including residualisation against 1/vol (the artefact's own shape). But split by trailing-vol tercile, the effect lives ONLY in the high-vol third (-9.25 pp, p_rot 0.010) and **flips sign** in the low-vol third (+1.23 pp, p_rot 0.44). A predictor that inverts depending on which half of the vol distribution the day sits in is describing the vol regime, not forecasting it.
- **Clause triggered.** B-44r was written with "if it also nulls, GEX gets retired as a research instrument that prints a daily description and nothing more". Both conventions have now been tested - US sign (B-44) and both-sides-short magnitude (B-44r) - and neither carries forward vol information on NSE indices. Retired.
- **What it settles:** the convention is no longer a live excuse for the B-44 null. The remaining gap is per-strike dealer inventory - data this repo does not have, rather than another assumption about it.
- 14 existing gamma tests still pass; `gamma_regime_backtest.py` untouched and still runs.

## [2026-09-05] ingest | GEX primer (unattributed) - corroboration, one new claim, and a PoC probe
- Source: pasted video summary, no transcript retained -> [[sources/YT-GEX-Dealer-Hedging-Volatility-Primer]]
- Pages created: [[sources/YT-GEX-Dealer-Hedging-Volatility-Primer]], [[analyses/GEX-Strategy-Mapping-PoC-Probe]]
- Pages updated: [[concepts/Gamma-Exposure-GEX]] (sources 1 -> 2), [[wiki/index.md]]
- **The PoC already exists and already failed.** The ask was "ingest and probe for a PoC"; `tools/gamma_exposure.py` + `gamma_regime_backtest.py` + 14 tests shipped 2026-08-29 under B-44, and 480 sessions of NIFTY/BANKNIFTY returned a clean null. Ingested as corroboration against that record rather than as new ground.
- **What the source actually adds:** strategy selection by regime - premium selling in positive gamma, long options in negative gamma. Everything else restates the existing concept page. Filed with a `[!contradiction]` callout because both prescriptions are downstream of the forward-vol split B-44 measured at +0.28 / +0.24 / **-0.40** pp (p_rot 0.84 / 0.83 / 0.67) - a strategy split that exists to harvest a vol spread has nothing to harvest if the spread is absent.
- **Key insight from the probe:** the cheap decisive test is **RV-IV by regime**, not a strategy backtest. Premium-selling-vs-long-options is economically one number - the variance risk premium - and testing it directly skips strike selection, rolls, spreads and assignment. It is blocked on data, not code: `compute_gex` inverts per-strike IV but never persists it, and `find cache -name "*bhav*"` returns nothing, so backfilling `atm_iv` over 480 sessions means ~480 NSE downloads. Cache the zips during that rebuild and every later question about this data is free.
- **Also recorded:** the regime label is a thin residual - net/gross -9.5% NIFTY / -6.0% BANKNIFTY on 2026-09-04, the small difference of +81,542 Cr calls against -98,600 Cr puts. Under the naive convention a modest positioning error flips the sign, which is why B-44r sits upstream of any strategy work.
- No code written, no backlog item promoted.

## [2026-09-04] build | Market regime + Today's verdict merged into one compact card
- Owner: both blocks were eating most of the first screen on every page. They are now **one card**, and the merge is defensible rather than just tighter: the regime is the CONTEXT and the verdict is the DECISION taken in it. The old code already carried the rule that makes it safe - *"two coloured surfaces stacked would read as two verdicts, and only one of these is a decision"* - so one card carrying the verdict's border tone keeps exactly one decision on screen, with the regime as a labelled strip inside it.
- **Compaction is layout only - nothing was dropped.** Same verdict word, instruction, position size, max trades, regime label, playbook sentence, vol-vs-baseline, distance-from-50-SMA, and all three flag blocks including NOT CHECKED (which exists precisely so a partial verdict is distinguishable from a complete one). Most of the height came back from moving Caution / Favorable / Not-checked out of stacked full-width lists into three columns; headline figures went 30px -> 24px. Measured card height **213px** for what used to be two cards plus the gap between them.
- **A tweak I made and then reverted, recorded because the reasoning was wrong.** The group popovers render 62px tall and I read that as label wrapping caused by the new sign-in column, so I rebalanced the ribbon widths. It is not wrapping - Streamlit stacks its `expand_more` glyph under the popover label, and the 62px predates every change in this session. The ratios are back to what they were and the comment now says what the height actually is. A fix whose comment states a false cause is worse than no fix.
- Verified live: one card, no traceback, sign-in popover still present, and the hero now reaches the first screen.

## [2026-09-04] build | Sign-in made optional and moved to the ribbon; Home gets a "where the action is today" card map
- Owner supplied a screenshot of a commercial NSE screener's landing page and asked for two things: sign-in optional and moved to the top-right of the ribbon, and a Home layout like the reference.
- **Sign-in.** The hard gate is gone - it used to render a full-page login and `st.stop()`, so nothing at all rendered until you signed in. It is now a popover at the far right of the nav ribbon; the dashboard renders regardless. The credential check (`.streamlit/secrets.toml`) is unchanged and `logged_in` is still set, but **signing in currently unlocks nothing** - the gate was all-or-nothing, so removing it leaves the flag with no consumer. Kept rather than deleted so a future per-page gate has something to read, and the module comment says plainly that it is not protecting anything today.
- **Security note recorded, not buried:** the Streamlit server binds 0.0.0.0 and prints an external URL, so with the gate off anything reachable at that port is browsable by whoever can reach it. That is a firewall/binding question, not an app question, but it changed materially with this commit.
- **Home layout follows the reference's SHAPE, not its data volume, and the reason is the no-fetch-on-load rule.** The commercial page can afford nine live lenses because it serves precomputed server-side lists. Every card here reads **stored state only** - the persisted `daily_opportunities` run and the offline NSE constituent file for company names (reusing `peer_comparison._constituents`). A card with no stored data says so rather than being padded.
- **Six cards, not nine, and that is deliberate.** A RISK-OFF regime caps the stored shortlist at five names, so nine "top 3" cards off one table would be the same five names rearranged. Each card answers a different question from a different column: today's shortlist (rank), sectors in the shortlist (groupby), strongest momentum, highest M1 rating, most confluence, and which scanners actually fired (tallied across the `scanners` strings).
- Also added the reference's call-to-action row under the hero (Open screener dashboard / View top movers / Scanner Hub) and its "No sign-in required to browse - for research and educational purposes only" line, which is now literally true.
- **Verified live without signing in** (the point of the change): no gate, ribbon popover present with the form, the map renders off the 2026-09-04 stored run (WELENT / WINDLAS / INOXINDIA, Industrials 2 names avg momentum 65, Stage 2 Uptrend fired on 5 of 5), and a card's View button routes to Opportunity Hub. One layout bug found and fixed in the pass: at Home's card width the View label wrapped onto two lines, so the header split went 3:1 -> 1.9:1.15.

## [2026-09-04] build | Trending Stocks folded into Home as a lazy section; the page is gone
- Owner asked to move the Trending Stocks section into Home. Two facts from the code shaped it: **one of its four lenses was already on Home** (`_darvas_window("home_vs", compact=True)`, moved up earlier because the lens was "buried three clicks deep"), and **Home has a stated no-fetch-on-load rule** (the watchlist block says so explicitly).
- Those two together decided the design. The remaining lenses are the most expensive fetches in the app - Price Movers pulls the whole-market bhavcopy plus live quotes, and the 52W/ATH pair downloads history for a few hundred names (the spinner says minutes). Mounting them eagerly would have put a minutes-long wait on the landing page, so the section is **lazy behind a Load button**, with a Hide to collapse it again.
- **Chosen scope: move and delete.** The page body became `render_trending_stocks(key_prefix, include_volume=True)`; "Trending Stocks" is removed from `PAGE_GROUPS["Market Intel"]` (4 pages -> 3) and from `PAGE_BLURBS`. Home mounts it with `include_volume=False` so the Darvas window is not rendered twice on one screen - the "menu of a menu" mistake Home was cleaned up to avoid. The cause-attributed spike scan travels with the section regardless, since it answers a different question and has no other home.
- **Two bugs the move created, both caught before commit.** (1) Streamlit runs the script top to bottom, and the Home block sits ~1,700 lines ABOVE where the page block lived - calling the new function from Home would have raised `NameError` on every load. The whole block (function + its three `@st.cache_data` helpers) was moved above Home. (2) A session open across the change would still name the removed page in `session_state` and match no router branch, rendering a blank screen; a `_VALID_PAGES` guard now falls back to Home.
- **Not verified in the UI.** The dashboard was restarted to load the edited module and the Browser-pane tab never got authenticated (server log shows no session connected), so the rendering was confirmed by parse + eight static assertions rather than on screen: call ordering, nav removal, blurb removal, stale-page guard, lazy mount, optional volume tab. Recorded as unverified rather than implied.

## [2026-09-04] verify | FP-1..FP-5 driven in the live app - one presentation bug found and fixed
- Owner signed in (credentials are theirs to enter; the login form is never filled by the agent) and the whole peer-comparison feature was exercised end-to-end in the running dashboard rather than headlessly.
- **Confirmed live on the Stock Analyzer (TITAN):** the six donuts now read Returns **66** - the derived-ROE change reaching the UI; **Compare peers** builds the 10-name Luxury Goods group; the FP-3 card renders the leader line and the per-axis rank chips (Valuation 10/10, Growth 7/10, Health 5/10, Returns 5/10, Capital Allocation 8/10, Earnings Consistency 6/10); the **Technicals** toggle appends RS 6m / RSI / ADX / 52w / Stage / Pivot with the correct group header; the radar draws two traces, and three once a peer is overlaid; the **vs anchor** drill opens with the gap column.
- **Bug found only because it was driven in the app.** The head-to-head printed the raw stored values where the table printed formatted ones: 52w as `3.2` against the table's `-3.2%`, and RS as `23` against `+23`. One number, two presentations, inside one feature - exactly what this surface exists to prevent. Fixed by extracting `peer_comparison.tech_text()` (plus `_tech_tone()`), which the table cell and the drill now both call; re-verified in the live app afterwards (`RS 6m +23 | +54`, `52w -3.2% | -7.7%`).
- **Worth recording about the method:** a Streamlit page holds its imported modules in memory, so editing `peer_comparison.py` under a running server changes nothing - the fix needed a server restart, which drops the session and costs another sign-in. Verify-then-fix loops on this app are expensive; batch the fixes.
- **FP-5 exercised in the Opportunity Hub** by building the live funnel (77 candidates across the leading industries). The expander renders the identical component under a different anchor, and the run happened to hit the **guard** path rather than the happy path: *"No peer group for ACE. Only 2 tagged peer(s) in Farm & Heavy Construction Machinery - below the 4-name floor."* Better evidence than a success would have been: the MIN_PEERS floor fires in production on a real small-cap, and says why.

## [2026-09-04] build | ROE derived from the filed statements - 13.5% -> 100% coverage, and the Returns axis stops running on two inputs
- The calibration pass had flagged ROE as uncalibratable: yfinance `.info` carries it for only 27 of 200 NSE names. **It turned out `fundamentals_deep` was already computing `roe` and `roe_prev` from the balance sheet (net income / shareholders' equity) and returning them - `fundamental_score.raw_metrics` was simply reading `.info` instead.** Same story for `debt_to_equity`. The fix was a preference order, not a new data layer.
- **Coverage: ROE 13.5% -> 100% (200/200), D/E 90.5% -> 99.5%.** Where both sources exist they agree closely: median |diff| **1.9pp**, max 8.0pp over the overlap (worst case NIVABUPA 4.1 derived vs 12.1 from `.info`, a timing difference - ours is latest filed FY, point-in-time equity, not average equity). So this is a coverage fix rather than a change of opinion.
- **Unit trap worth recording:** `.info`'s debtToEquity is a PERCENT (195.0), the derived one is already a RATIO (1.95). Preferring one over the other without the conversion would have scaled every leverage score by 100. The fallback keeps the /100 only on the `.info` branch.
- **Recalibrated the whole curve set from one fresh 200-name run** rather than pasting the new ROE curve into the old table - a curve table mixing two measurement dates is not a snapshot of anything. Measured NSE median ROE **13.5%**, p75 18.1%, p90 27.9%.
- **The one band intuition got right.** The old ROE ladder (12% -> 55, 18% -> 80) sits almost exactly on the measured p50/p75. Every other hand-set band was wrong, several badly; this one was not, and that is worth recording as honestly as the failures.
- **Result:** Returns now uses **2.90 of 3 inputs** on average (was ~2.0), median 57, 73 distinct scores over the sample. ITC coverage 16/17 -> 17/17. TITAN Returns 52 -> 66 with ROE 32.3% against ROCE 17.2% - which is the leverage story its Health axis (D/E 1.95x, STRETCHED 40) tells from the other side. Only **PEG** (11.5%) still resists calibration and keeps Lynch's absolute band.

## [2026-09-04] build | FP-2 to FP-5 shipped: peer radar overlay, within-group rank, technical columns, second surface
- Completed the [[analyses/Analyzer-Radar-Peers-Roadmap]] plan in one pass at the owner's request (the one-phase-per-live-session rule was set aside deliberately).
- **FP-2 radar** - `radar_figure()` draws peer median (dotted) + anchor, with an optional third trace picked from a selectbox; capped at three by construction because radars degrade past ~4 series. Caption states **shape, not area**: polygon area scales as the square of the values and depends on axis order, so the donuts and table stay the precise readout.
- **FP-3 rank** - `rank_report()` gives the anchor's **rank** per axis ("3rd of 10"), never a percentile, because in a group of 6-12 names a percentile is a weak statistic in a precise costume. The leader line averages the **five business axes and excludes Valuation** - leading a peer group must not mean being the cheapest. The star on the group's best cell is the ONLY peer-relative mark in the table and the group header row says so. **All tied-best rows are starred**; starring whichever sorted first would invent a ranking between identical scores (three jewellers tie at Growth 100).
- **FP-4 technicals** - RS 6m / RSI / ADX / 52w / Stage / Pivot behind a toggle, because it is a second full network pass (one price history per peer) on top of the statements. Stage carries the mockup's richer labels (`stage_label()`: Continuation / Stage 2 (Early) / Failed Reclaim / Basing / Topping), written as an explicit stated heuristic over `_infer_stage`. Pivot distance reuses `scanner_templates._darvas_box`. The "vs anchor" head-to-head reuses rows already scored, so it cannot disagree with the table it was opened from, and shows **no gap column on technical rows** - a 12-point RSI difference is not comparable to a 12-point axis difference.
- **FP-5 second surface** - the same `render_peer_comparison()` component drills from the Opportunity Hub's leading-industry step (the Hub goes sector->industry->constituents; peer comparison fixes one constituent and turns that group side-on). The deep-dive "Peers tab" was already satisfied - the analyzer has no tab shell since the nav router replaced `st.tabs`, and the peer section lives in that flow.
- **The bug verification caught, and it is the one this roadmap predicted.** `stock_council._relative_strength` returns `rs_pct` as **6-month (130-bar) outperformance vs the Nifty in percentage points** - signed and unbounded, e.g. NIACL +62, MFSL -10. The 2026-07 mockup showed "RS 100 = Best RS", a within-group percentile: a different quantity. Shipping ours under an "RS percentile" heading would have been exactly the absolute-vs-peer-relative mixing the roadmap flagged in July. Column renamed "RS 6m", signed, thresholds at +/-10pp.
- **Not verified in the live app:** the Streamlit page sits behind a login, so the head-to-head panel and the Opportunity Hub expander are compile-checked only. Everything renderable without Streamlit (table, ranks, radar, technicals) was rendered standalone and eyeballed - which is exactly why the markup lives in `peer_comparison.py` rather than in the page.

## [2026-09-04] build | All six fundamental axes calibrated against the measured NSE distribution
- Generalised the Growth fix to the other five axes, which had never been checked. `tools/calibrate_bands.py` (renamed from the growth-only script) samples 200 NIFTY-500 names through a new shared `fundamental_score.raw_metrics()` - the same extractor the scorers use, so the measurement can never drift from what is actually scored. Twelve metrics moved onto measured percentile curves (p10->20, p25->35, p50->55, p75->80, p90->95).
- **Health turned out worse than Growth had been.** Median NSE debt/equity is **0.18x** against a band that gave 100 at 0.25x, and median debtor days **47.5** against a band that gave 80 at 60 - two of the axis's three inputs were handing the median Indian company a perfect or near-perfect score, and only the `min_drag` aggregation was hiding it. Margin CV: median 0.19 vs a band paying 70 at 0.30. P/E was roughly right by accident (median 35.7x, band gave 55 at 40x).
- **Four facts about this market the calibration established.** NSE is **expensive** (median P/E 35.7x, p90 92.5x - US-calibrated bands would mark the whole market overvalued). NSE is **unlevered** (median D/E 0.18x, a quarter under 0.05x) - so leverage is an outlier signal here, and TITAN's 1.95x now reads STRETCHED 40 where it read SOLID 57. **ROCE is falling for most of the market** (median trend -0.69pp YoY), so flat ROCE is above-median. **A quarter of listed India runs negative FCF margin** (p25 -5.6%).
- **Two coverage findings, about our data not the market:** yfinance `.info` carries **PEG for 11.5%** and **ROE for 13.5%** of NSE names. Both failed the 30-observation floor, so both keep absolute externally-argued bands (Lynch's PEG<1) rather than a curve fitted to whichever names yfinance happens to populate. Consequence: Valuation runs on P/E+P/B alone for ~88% of names and Returns on ROCE+net margin. **ROE is worth deriving from the statements** - we already compute ROCE that way at 90.5% coverage.
- **Verified:** every metric's curve puts the sample median at 55 with ~65 distinct scores; ITC Health 100->86, Returns 100->91; RELIANCE Valuation 88 CHEAP (P/E 24 against a market median of 36). In the peer groups every axis now spreads - the jewellers' Health median is 28 (gold-financing working capital, correctly penalised) against the insurers' 74.5.
- The refactor was verified behaviour-neutral before any number changed: `raw_metrics()` extraction reproduced ITC and TITAN's six axes exactly, then the curves were swapped in.

## [2026-09-04] build | Growth band saturation fixed - the axis now scores off the measured NSE distribution
- Follow-on from FP-1: the peer table showed nearly every peer scoring Growth 90-100. Measured the actual distribution over a 150-name stride sample of the NIFTY-500 ([[analyses/Analyzer-Radar-Peers-Roadmap]], `tools/calibrate_growth_bands.py`, raw values cached): revenue CAGR 3Y median **12.8%**, profit CAGR median **16.4%**, quarterly revenue YoY median **18.6%**. The old shared band table topped out at 25%, which is the **81st percentile** of revenue CAGR but only the **65th** of YoY - so it handed a perfect 100 to a fifth of the market on one input and over a third on another, and read STRONG for **39% of listed India**.
- **Re-banding alone did not fix it.** Percentile-anchored four-rung tables put the axis median at **47** (SLOWING), because averaging three step scores drags the middle down - the rungs below the median sit much further from it than the rungs above. Steps also collapsed 150 companies onto a handful of values, which is its own kind of non-measurement.
- **The fix is a per-metric distribution CURVE**, interpolated between percentile anchors (p10->20, p25->35, p50->55, p75->80, p90->95, clamped). Per-metric because the three inputs are not interchangeable: quarterly YoY runs hotter than a 3Y CAGR and profit compounds faster than revenue, so one shared ladder was scoring the same company's growth well twice. Growth is now the only axis in `fundamental_score.py` scored this way, and the docstring says why.
- **Measured after:** median **55** (MODERATE - what median should mean), STRONG 20%, WEAK 16%, 3% at 100, **65 distinct scores** over the sample. NIACL's insurer peer column went from pinned to a **27-90** spread. The jewellers still sit at 59-100 - correctly, that sector is genuinely in the top decile - so the axis now separates the two cases instead of flattering both. Spot-checks: ITC 25 WEAK (rev CAGR 3.6%, YoY -11%), TITAN 76 STRONG (29% CAGR, 40% YoY), previously 93.
- **The transferable lesson:** the saturation was invisible on any single stock's donut and obvious the moment ten companies sat in a column. A comparison surface is a calibration instrument, not just a feature. The other five axes have never been checked this way.

## [2026-09-04] analysis+build | Analyzer radar + peer comparison re-audit, and FP-1 shipped
- Owner asked whether the radar / peer-comparison roadmaps existed and for a fresh one. Both did ([[analyses/Radar-Chart-Analyzer-Fit]] RC-1..RC-4, [[analyses/Peer-Comparison-Roadmap]] PC-1..PC-4, both 2026-07-25). Re-audited against the code and **RC-1 is now the wrong first step**: its deliverable already ships. `render_fundamental_signals` draws the six banded axes as donuts on the Stock Analyzer, `fundamental_score.score_all` is the shared scorer, and `fundamentals_deep` made the screener.in CAGR/ROCE dependency dead. A solo single-stock radar would redraw what is on screen. Merged plan filed as [[analyses/Analyzer-Radar-Peers-Roadmap]] (FP-1..FP-5); the two old pages carry supersede notes.
- **Design finding, from current practice rather than the 2026-07 video:** radar area scales as the SQUARE of the values, circular comparison is harder than linear, and radars degrade past ~10 axes / ~4 series. Our six 0-100 axes fit the narrow band where they do work - but only as a COMPARISON. Hence the rule: a radar for one stock is decoration; a radar for a stock against its peer group is information. Peer table first (FP-1), radar demoted to an anchor-vs-peer-median overlay (FP-2).
- **FP-1 built and measured the same day.** `tools/peer_comparison.py` + `render_peer_comparison`, behind a **Compare peers** button (it is ~48 network calls; it never runs on the default Analyze path). TITAN -> Luxury Goods, 9 jewellers. RELIANCE -> Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing, 8 names. NIACL -> 12 insurers.
- **Three things the build forced.** (1) yfinance's fine industry is TOO fine for NSE - NIACL's "Insurance - Diversified" holds one other listed name, IRCTC's "Travel Services" three - so the 4-name floor refused nearly every financial. Rather than lower the floor, the ladder widens one rung to the industry FAMILY (the text before the separator, which yfinance uses hierarchically) and labels the group `Widened`. IRCTC still correctly gets nothing. (2) Peer resolution is two-stage: the offline NSE constituent files narrow by macro sector (their `industry` field is really the macro sector - "Financial Services" is 121 names), and only that pool pays the network tag, disk-cached 30 days. (3) **pandas re-introduced the exact dishonesty the scorer bans** - a column of ints and `None` becomes float64 with `NaN`, `NaN is None` is False, and an unscored axis rendered as the literal text `nan`. The no-neutral-50 rule has to be defended at the render boundary too.
- **New calibration debt surfaced by the peer view:** Growth saturates. Nearly every peer in both groups scored 90-100 because the band tops out at 25% CAGR. An axis where everyone scores full marks discriminates nothing - invisible on a single stock's donut, obvious the moment ten companies sit in a column.

## [2026-08-31] analysis | Contra strategy roadmap - and the supplied list's two best-rated methods are the two we already falsified
- Owner asked for a contra roadmap: add the top contra strategies known from the web, fold in three named screens (Sales > 2x mcap, P/E < 20, order book > mcap), against a supplied 7-strategy list from another AI. Written as [[analyses/Contra-Strategy-Roadmap]].
- **The finding that matters most: the supplied list rates as its two strongest exactly the two methods this repo has already measured to a NEGATIVE.** It calls liquidity-exhaustion/POC "extremely powerful" and astro-contra "the highest-accuracy contra method in astro-quant trading". Our own data: [[analyses/SVP-Signal-Backtest-Design]] found **5 of 7 factor families sign-INVERTED**, `corr(score, 20d fwd) = -0.041`, stop-and-reverse median **-80%**, beat buy&hold **0/8** - and the `trap` factor carrying the engine's largest weight attributes at **-0.51**. The SBC daily-vedha study is recorded as a clean negative. Its "Confluence Model" then stacks both of those as Layers 1 and 2: **confluence between two negative signals is the same error twice with more conviction attached.**
- **Scored all seven against what is actually built:** sentiment-extreme = best-supported *and* the biggest unrealised asset (inputs computed, nothing gated - already B-32); value dislocation = B-49 shipped; mean reversion = built, and RSI 60-40 measured no edge as tuned, so no more oscillator variants; event-driven = built, and PEAD is the one anomaly with real Indian evidence; macro-cycle = lowest ROI (a few turning points per decade is not a sample).
- **The three named screens, assessed rather than accepted.** *Sales > 2x mcap* is P/S < 0.5 - real (Fisher) but **sector-conditional to the point of being sector selection in disguise**: thin-margin distribution/commodity names pass permanently, software never does. Fix is sector-relative ranking or a margin floor, both one-line additions to B-49's CRITERIA. *P/E < 20* is already `max_pe`, a fine guard and a weak signal - and it excludes loss-makers, which for contra is a real cost (B-50 exists partly for that). *Order book > mcap* is **the strongest of the three and the only one genuinely blocked** - it is in investor presentations and concalls, i.e. **PDFs**, the same data shape as the buyback price (verified: 0 of 28 buyback filings carried a price in their announcement text). One PDF fact-extractor unlocks both, so they are sequenced together.
- **Owner's buyback framing accepted and split.** They said a buyback is good news only when EPS-accretive with growth intact, and left the rest subjective - correct, and it separates cleanly: **computable** = size / shares outstanding -> EPS accretion, premium to CMP, route, promoter participation, size/mcap; **subjective** = whether growth is intact. Encode only the first. Supporting evidence is contra-specific: Ikenberry/Lakonishok/Vermaelen (1995, JFE) +12.1% 4-year abnormal return overall, **+45.3% for value stocks, ZERO for glamour** - the signal lives exactly where B-49/B-50 already look. Caveat carried: India's **1 Oct 2024** change makes the whole consideration deemed dividend at slab rates with no cost deduction, so *tendering for the premium* is largely destroyed for taxable residents while *holding on the signal* is intact.
- **Four omissions named, chief of them Piotroski F-Score.** Nine binary accounting tests; Piotroski (2000) found high-book-to-market names scoring 8-9 beat the market by **7.5%/yr** while 0-1 lagged by **8.3%** - i.e. it works *specifically inside the value universe* by discarding the losers. That is exactly **the value-trap guard B-49 does not have**; `grep -i piotroski tools/` returns nothing and every input is already computed by `fundamentals_deep`. Also: insider/promoter buying (free data already flowing, currently scored for institutions only), long-term reversal F-Score-gated, and forced selling / index deletion.
- **Roadmap sequenced with Phase 0 as the blocker: one shared event-study harness.** B-34, B-49 and B-50 all owe the *same* experiment and it should be built once - signal entry, N-day hold, matched control, **exact rotation p-value** (Welch is invalid on overlapping forward windows; it already produced one false 0.031 that read 0.32 under rotation). Then Phase 1 F-Score, Phase 2 sentiment-as-gate, Phase 3 buyback (B-52), Phase 4 PDF fact extractor -> order book (B-53), Phase 5 reversal (B-54). Deprioritised with reasons: macro cycle, astro-contra, POC confluence.
- **Recorded so it is not re-litigated:** the list's implicit model is *more layers -> more conviction*. This repo's results say the opposite - 43 indicators, 9 of 41 scanners hidden for redundancy, three of four new indicators null. **The binding constraint is not more strategies, it is Phase 0.**

## [2026-08-31] ship | B-51 volume-anomaly screen with cause attribution - and the exchange's own notices were making it circular
- Owner asked whether a "volume is the story" card (5 names at 20-60x their 1-year average volume, each with a trigger) could be probed from free data or the existing OI/deal section. It can, and most pieces were already in the repo. Built `tools/volume_anomaly.py` + 21 offline tests.
- **Reproduced the card first.** Turnover landed within ~7% on all five names; the residual is fully explained by the card's header saying **NSE/BSE** while the bhavcopy is NSE only.
- **The finding that decided the architecture: yfinance is not reliable for the LATEST session on smaller NSE names.** On 2026-08-28 it had **no bar at all** for MASTEK or OAL and silently served the previous day - turning MASTEK's 43.9x volume day into **0.9x**. Not an error, a plausible-looking normal reading, which is worse. The NSE bhavcopy has all five plus the whole market (**2,629 EQ rows**) in one free file, with volume, traded value, close **and prev_close** - so the multiple, the turnover and the 1-day return all come from it. yfinance is used only for the 1-year baseline, and only for the shortlist that already cleared the turnover filter.
- **Refactor rather than duplicate:** `screener_pipeline.fetch_bhavcopy_top_volume` already did all the column sniffing and EQ filtering and then discarded everything but the symbol list. Added `fetch_bhavcopy_frame()` and made the old function a thin wrapper over it, so the two cannot drift.
- **Cause taxonomy:** CORPORATE_ACTION / PROP_CHURN / DEAL_DRIVEN / UNEXPLAINED, from `nse_announcements` (per symbol) plus `india_market_intel`'s bulk/block feeds. **The Market Intel page's standing objection - "the whole-market announcement feed is very high volume" - does not apply to this shape:** announcements are fetched only for names that already cleared the volume filter, i.e. **tens of calls after the funnel, not thousands before it**.
- **The bug the first LIVE run found, and it is the one that makes the module work: NSE files a "Spurt in Volume" notice BECAUSE volume spiked.** Counting it as the cause is circular. It had labelled **7 of 9** spikes CORPORATE_ACTION, starving UNEXPLAINED - the only bucket worth attention. Added a `REACTIVE_SUBJECTS` exclusion (spurt in volume/price, price movement, clarification, surveillance, ASM/GSM, newspaper publication) checked **before** the material-keyword test - the order matters, because a clarification reply quotes the company and routinely contains material words. Distribution moved from `UNEXPLAINED=2 / CORPORATE_ACTION=9` to `UNEXPLAINED=5 / CORPORATE_ACTION=5`. No unit test could have caught this; it needed real filings.
- **A correction to my own pitch, recorded in the code.** I had told the owner prop churn explained MASTEK's spike. Measured: the visible round-trip is 528,244 of 5,449,060 shares - **9.7%**, below the 15% headline threshold. It is also a **lower bound**, because NSE only requires a bulk deal to be reported above **0.5% of listed shares**, so smaller desks never appear at all. Churn share is now printed on every row regardless of verdict, and a test pins MASTEK to DEAL_DRIVEN specifically so the threshold cannot be quietly re-tuned to fit the example that motivated it.
- **Live result** (2026-08-28, top 300 by turnover, >=8x): **13 spikes** - FMGOETZE 60.2x (Record Date + Dividend filings), MASTEK 56.8x, JGCHEM 26.2x (**PROP_CHURN, 19%** - HRTI + Junomoneta), OMAXE 21.1x (**PROP_CHURN, 23%** - Microcurves + Junomoneta), and 5 UNEXPLAINED including CGCL at Rs 748 Cr turnover. **19 names skipped for short history** rather than scored against a meaningless average.
- **Wired as a SCREEN, not a trigger** - no entry, stop or target, never a confluence vote, same discipline as the Renko Flip screen. A volume spike is a question; the module's job is to hand over the answer next to it.
- **Unvalidated + one hard operational limit:** no forward-return study, so nothing claims an UNEXPLAINED spike precedes anything. And **the NSE bulk/block endpoints carry only the current day** - no history - so a back-dated run finds the volume but not the deals. Daily capture into an archive is the obvious follow-on and is deliberately not built.

## [2026-08-31] ship | B-50 contra margin-inflection screen - and two bugs that only a live run could find
- Owner asked whether the OPM-jump / PEAD / EPS-acceleration shape on an OCCL Ltd "deep value" card could be caught systematically as a **contra** strategy. Built `tools/contra_inflection.py` + `test_contra_inflection.py`; concept page [[concepts/Margin-Inflection-Contra]].
- **The design point, and it is the whole module: B-49 screens on LEVEL, B-50 screens on CHANGE, and they select disjoint sets.** OCCL fails B-49 outright (ROCE 11.6%, ROE 11.1%) yet its Jun-2026 quarter shows **OPM 25.6% vs 16.0%** a year earlier with **PAT +206%** - reproduced from free data to the decimal (the card says +206%, the statements say 206.33%). Run the two cards through both screens and they swap: **AGI Greenpac = 9/10 on value, NO_INFLECTION on contra; OCCL = fails value, INFLECTING on contra.**
- **The signal only exists at quarterly grain.** OCCL's four annual OPMs read 12.0 / 11.1 / - / 15.5 - flat to declining. The quarterly series reads **16.0 -> 10.8 -> 8.5 -> 12.2 -> 25.6**. So `fundamentals_deep._quarterly_history` gained `ebit`, `opm` and `opm_yoy_base`; it had carried net margin only, and net margin moves on one-off gains that OPM does not.
- **Output is a verdict LABEL with reasons, not a score:** INFLECTING / MARGIN_LED / RECOVERING / SINGLE_QUARTER / UNVERIFIED / ONE_OFF_SUSPECT / NO_INFLECTION / INSUFFICIENT_DATA. Four guards, each tested and reported separately: low base (two independent tests - was the year-ago base below the trailing median, AND is the latest print above *every* prior quarter), one-off gains, profit-without-cash (`cfo_pat_ratio`), and persistence.
- **Bug 1, caught by the test suite: `operating_led` demanded EBIT keep PACE with PAT.** That flags ordinary deleveraging and tax-rate changes as accounting one-offs - and the AGI card's own pitch, "debt cut by over half", is precisely that shape. The test is now **participation, not parity** (`EBIT_PARTICIPATION = 0.25`): the red flag is EBIT *not moving at all* while PAT multiplies, not EBIT moving more slowly.
- **Bug 2, caught only by running it on real names, and it is the important one: an unevaluated guard was being read as a PASSED guard.** **ITI Limited** took the top INFLECTING slot on OPM 72.7% vs 3.9% - with all four prior quarters **loss-making** (-25, -54, -64, -4 Cr) and revenue **down 40%**. Because the base was negative, `pat_yoy`, `operating_led` and `cfo_pat_ratio` were all `None`, so every check silently no-opped and **silence read as approval**. Fixed with an explicit evidence gate, a new `UNVERIFIED` verdict and a `loss_in_base` flag. **Same failure mode as B-49's `.info` ROE hole** - worth treating as a standing hazard in any screen built on optional fields.
- **Also split MARGIN_LED out of INFLECTING.** Margin up on flat or shrinking revenue is cost-cutting or mix; it can be a fine outcome and it is not a rising star. Bayer CropScience cleared every other test with revenue **down 4.2%** and now labels as MARGIN_LED rather than sharing a bucket with a business that is actually growing.
- **Measured on 120 free small/micro-caps:** 120/120 derived. 11 INFLECTING, 1 MARGIN_LED, 1 RECOVERING, 19 SINGLE_QUARTER, 22 ONE_OFF_SUSPECT, 1 UNVERIFIED, 64 NO_INFLECTION. **The guards reject 42 names a naive "profit up X%" filter would have handed over** - that rejection is the module's actual output.
- **Grounding and its limit, stated in the module:** [[concepts/PEAD]] is documented in Indian equities 2002-2017 and survives controls for beta, size, book-to-market, illiquidity and idiosyncratic volatility. That supports *prices under-react to earnings surprises*; it does **not** support this filter set finding multibaggers. Separately, yfinance returns ~5-6 quarters and `_year_ago_index` matches a year-ago quarter for the newest one or two only, so **persistence is quarter-on-quarter and seasonality-exposed** - reported as a count, never a pass/fail, because the data cannot support more.
- **Owed, and deliberately not shipped without it:** a forward event study - enter at the first INFLECTING print, hold N days, matched non-inflecting control, exact rotation p-value. Same experiment shape as B-34 and B-49 owe; run all three together. Not scheduled, not on any dashboard until then.

## [2026-08-31] fix | scanner_daily_runner resurrected - and the dashboard no longer GENERATES Python
- Found during the Opportunity/Scanner architecture crosscheck: `tools/scanner_daily_runner.py`, whose own docstring claimed it was "executed by the Windows Scheduled Task TradingBramha-DailyScanner", **has never run once**. Four independent reasons, any one of them fatal:
  1. **It does not parse.** `py_compile` -> `IndentationError: expected an indented block after 'for' statement on line 21`. The `for` body and the `with open(...)` body were both un-indented.
  2. **It imports three modules that do not exist** in this repo - `data_fetcher`, `bhavcopy_fetcher`, `dashboard_app_helpers`.
  3. **Its default universe was always empty.** `bh.fetch_top_n(300) if "Bhavcopy" in UNIVERSE_MODE else []` - and the panel's other option, "Screener results (today's pipeline)", reads **Streamlit session state**, which a headless scheduled task does not have. That branch would have scanned `[]` and reported success.
  4. **Nothing reads its output.** `cache/scanner_daily_*.json` has no consumer anywhere in `tools/`, and no such file has ever existed.
- **Root cause, and the actual fix: it was GENERATED.** `dashboard_app.py` wrote the whole module from an f-string template every time the Daily Scanner Scheduler panel was used. Generated Python is invisible to `import`, `py_compile`, linting and tests - which is exactly how a module that cannot parse sat in the repo unnoticed. The runner is now a **checked-in module**, and the panel writes **only the .bat**, passing the universe choice as an argument. That removes the whole class of bug rather than re-indenting one instance of it.
- **The rewrite reuses instead of re-implementing.** It imports `opportunity_daily._FetchRouter`, so the two nightly paths cannot drift again, and that buys two things the template got wrong: **one download per symbol per run** (the template refetched every symbol for every scanner), and **`needs_full_history` routing** so ATH / IPO-base breakout are not handed a 400-bar frame on which `scan_ath_breakout` reports a ~1.6-year high as an ALL-TIME high. It also passes a Nifty benchmark so RS is excess return over the index rather than an absolute trailing return.
- **Failure is now loud.** An empty universe raises with a readable reason and exits non-zero, so the `.bat` logs `***** FAILED *****` instead of a task that "succeeds" having scanned nothing. `--universe screener` runs `sp.run_pipeline()` itself rather than expecting a Streamlit session. The snapshot gained a `meta` block (universe mode, size, days, scanner count, timestamp) so a stale file can be told from a fresh one without reading the filename.
- **Verified end to end, not just compiled:** `--universe bhavcopy --top-n 40` over 3 scanners -> **22 hits** (Pullback Entry 16, Darvas Box 5, ATH Breakout 1), snapshot written and re-read. The log line "Fetched **80** distinct symbol-frames for 3 scanners" is the routing working as designed, not a regression: 40 frames in the default cache plus 40 in the full-history cache that ATH Breakout alone draws on.
- Also ASCII'd the em-dash in the generated `.bat` log banner - cmd reads a `.bat` in the OEM codepage, so a UTF-8 dash lands in `scanner_daily.log` as mojibake.
- **Still open, deliberately not fixed here:** (a) **nothing consumes the snapshot** - the producer works, the consumer does not exist, so this remains a write-only artefact until something reads it; (b) the repo has **two scheduling mechanisms** - `scheduler_core`'s JobSpec registry (which knows `opportunity_daily` but not this) and the Scanner Hub's direct `schtasks` call. Rewiring one onto the other is a separate decision from making the runner work.

## [2026-08-31] ship | B-49 free quality-value screen - and the card that prompted it uses the shortest window three times
- Owner showed a "Deep Value Opportunity" card for AGI Greenpac (P/E 13.6, P/S 1.81, OPM 22%, ROE 15.8, ROCE 19.6, D/E 0.10, rev +14%, PAT +12%) and asked whether similar candidates could be found from **freely available data**. Tested before answering, then built `tools/value_screen.py`.
- **Free data reproduces the card.** 6 of 8 figures land within rounding on the login-free path (P/E 14.13 vs 13.6 and P/S 1.85 vs 1.81 both explained by the price moving 775 -> 791; ROCE 19.4 vs 19.6; D/E 0.10 exact). The two misses are **definitional, not availability**.
- **Finding 1, and it changed the design: `.info` returns `returnOnEquity: None` for AGI Greenpac.** A screen keyed on yfinance `.info` alone would have **silently dropped its own worked example**. Metrics now come from the **statements** path (`fundamentals_deep.derive_fundamentals`, which computes ROE/ROCE itself) with `.info` supplying only the multiples; criteria are three-state **PASS / FAIL / UNKNOWN** and a missing figure is never counted as a fail. An under-reported name is visibly different from a rejected one.
- **Finding 2: the card uses the shortest available window three times.** "OPM 22%" is labelled *latest qtr* against 19.3% for the full year. "+14% revenue / +12% profit" are the `.info` **quarterly** figures - from the annual statements the same company grows **5.4% YoY and 5.7% over three years**. The screen therefore tests the **3Y CAGR** and *reports* the 1Y beside it (`REV3Y | REV1Y | PAT3Y | PAT1Y`), with a note when 1Y exceeds the CAGR by >= 6pp. Flagged, never screened on: a genuine inflection and a flattered one look identical from outside.
- **Finding 3, a free saving: `fetch_statements` was already calling `.info`** and keeping four fields. Extended to carry `market_cap / pe / ps / price / sector / industry`, so the screen costs **one** yfinance round trip per symbol rather than two. P/S falls back to market-cap / revenue when yfinance's own is missing, tagged `ps_source: calc` so a derived ratio is never passed off as the vendor's.
- **Closes a real gap rather than adding a parallel one:** `multibagger_candidates.py` runs almost this exact query (`OPM>10 AND ROCE>15 AND D/E<1 AND sales growth 3Y>15`) but is **screener.in cookie-gated**; `smallcap_discovery.py` is free but scores **ownership only, no fundamentals**. B-49 is the free fundamentals half, and reuses both sides - universe from `smallcap_universe`, governance flags from `ownership_cache` as **flags never votes**, the same convention `RISK_SCANNERS` follows.
- **Verified end to end:** 80 free small-caps scanned, **80/80 derived** (full coverage on the free path), 1 hit under strict defaults (`max_fails=0` across 10 criteria). The growth-gap note fired on that hit - 1Y revenue 11.2pp above its 3Y CAGR. 27 tests in `test_value_screen.py`, all passing, **none touching the network** (`screen` takes an injected `derive`); one test asserts the AGI card's own figures clear the default screen, because a screen that rejects the example it was built from is misconfigured.
- **Bug caught in the CLI:** argparse runs help text through %-formatting, so a criterion label like `"OPM %"` is a dangling format spec and raised `ValueError: badly formed help string`. Escaped.
- **Stated in the module, the CLI footer and the backlog:** ranking is *passes -> fewest unknowns -> ROCE*, which is **not a conviction score and not backtested**. "Cheap with good ROCE" is among the most crowded screens that exist. Deliberately **not** registered in `scheduler_core` and **not** wired to any dashboard page - both are cheap, and both should wait on the forward hit-rate study against Smallcap-250 that **B-34 already owes**.

## [2026-08-30] ship | Indicator dropdown rebuilt TradingView-style - a family rail, and families split 6 -> 8
- Owner: "can we build indicator drop down like TV, lets make indicator family clearer". Shipped in `chart_dashboard.html`: the 220px single-column popover becomes a **520px two-column browser** - a family rail on the left, the matching list on the right. The rail is the actual fix; **43 rows in one scrolling column read as a wall no matter how many headings** are in it.
- **Families 6 -> 8, by splitting the group that had stopped meaning anything.** "Pattern Recognition" held **12 of the 43 entries** and mixed three unrelated ideas, so it split into **Price Patterns** (Elliott, VCP, Rounding Bottom, Falling Wedge, Harmonic), **Smart Money & Structure** (FVG, Order Blocks, Structure Chain, Trendlines) and **Levels & Projection** (Fib, Gann, Projection). "Bands & Channels" -> "Trend & Bands". **Grouping is display-only - the stored `key` is untouched**, so saved pane configs and localStorage survive the regroup with no migration.
- **Eight nouns is not much better than 43 rows**, so each family carries a live count in the rail and a one-line `GROUP_BLURB` under its header saying what the family is for. Blurbs hide while searching, where the query is the context and they would only sit between matches.
- **New `PANE` tag on the 10 sub-pane oscillators** (`rsi, macd, obv, rs, prte, adxdi, evmacd, roc, stoch, hm`). Each one ticked opens a pane BELOW the chart and shortens the price panel - a squeeze the code already had a comment about. The cost is now visible **before** the click rather than discovered after it.
- **"On this chart" pseudo-family** with a live count, so the active set can be seen and un-ticked without hunting eight families. Un-ticking while that view is up drops the row immediately (the change handler re-runs the filter), or the list would keep showing something no longer on the chart. Ticked rows are highlighted in every view via `:has(.ind-chk:checked)`.
- **Search deliberately WIDENS scope.** Typing while a family is selected resets to All and re-highlights the All chip - otherwise searching "hilega" inside Volume returns "no indicators match" for something that exists one family over. The rail highlight can never contradict what the list is showing.
- **Mobile is a different layout, not a squeezed one:** a 148px side rail inside a 375px bottom sheet would leave the list unusable, so the same buttons become a horizontally scrolling chip strip (rail 43px tall, scrollWidth 1137 vs client 349). Verified at 375x812: no page overflow.
- **Bug caught in verification, not by eye:** the mobile sheet body ran **4px past the viewport** (`bodyBottom` 816 vs `vh` 812), silently clipping the last rows behind the panel's `overflow:hidden`. The height calc now subtracts the search row *and* the panel's own top padding; re-measured at 811 vs 812.
- **Second bug caught by the registry check:** `fib` and `gann` carry `group:` on a **continuation line**, so the per-line regroup silently missed them and left both in Price Patterns. Found by asserting the rendered panel's per-family counts, not by looking at the screenshot. The search placeholder now reads its count from `INDICATOR_DEFS.length` rather than a literal that goes stale on the next indicator added.
- Verified through **localhost:5001** - the path the browser actually loads, not a side port - 43 rows rendered, every key present, no orphan groups, zero console errors.

## [2026-08-30] analysis+ingest | Indicator coverage gap vs "what top traders use" - and a partial ingest with the refusals recorded
- Owner asked which indicators/oscillators top traders use, what is NOT implemented, to search the web, do a gap analysis, and "ingest if worthy".
- **The web evidence is bad and the analysis says so up front.** Every 2026-dated "most used indicators" page returned by search is unsourced SEO content - no named survey, no sample, no methodology - and several are broker/prop-firm affiliate pages. They agree because they copy each other. `quantifiedstrategies.com`, the one claiming backtests, is behind a bot wall. Only the *composition* folklore (trend + momentum + volume, 2-4 tools, confluence) was carried, labelled as a popularity prior. The prop-desk tooling literature (NinjaTrader Order Flow+, Tradovate, ATAS, GoCharting) was the single useful strand.
- **Measured inventory, not remembered:** 44 selectable `INDICATOR_DEFS` in `chart_dashboard.html` across 6 groups, 44 `scan_*` in `scanner_templates.py`. Conventional retail coverage is **already unusually complete** and carries several things the listicles do not (session volume profile, order blocks, structure-break chains, K-means adaptive SuperTrend, RS vs benchmark).
- **Tier 1 gap - wiki page exists, no code:** [[concepts/CCI]], [[concepts/KST]], [[concepts/True-Strength-Index-TSI]], [[concepts/CPR-Central-Pivot-Range]] all have concept pages and **zero implementation** (`grep cpr chart_dashboard.html` -> 0). Worse, two series are already computed and simply not exposed: **MFI** exists as `_mfi_series` / `scan_mfi_oversold` but cannot be plotted, and **ATR** is computed 32x (Keltner, PMax, Renko) but is not selectable. That is wiring, not maths.
- **Tier 2:** **Ichimoku** is the largest single named absence. Parabolic SAR is absent but `pmax`/`stai` already occupy the trailing-stop-flip role - adding it risks another zero-unique entry of exactly the kind [[analyses/Scanner-Overlap-Audit]] just trimmed. Vortex/RVI/MAMA/Awesome/TTM-Squeeze judged low priority: momentum restatements on top of eight existing oscillators.
- **Tier 3 - the gap that actually matters, and it is a DATA gap:** footprint, **cumulative volume delta** and Market-Profile **TPO** are what prop tooling converges on and what [[entities/Chris-Kmer]] describes using. The repo has `volprofile` + `svp`, i.e. the volume-at-price half, and **none** of the aggressor half. CVD needs tick or bid/ask-classified trades; `data_source.py` serves OHLCV. A bar-level delta proxy would render a plausible chart of something that is not delta, so B-48 is written as a **feasibility spike whose deliverable is a note, not an indicator**. TPO has no such blocker.
- **Proposed:** B-45 expose ATR + MFI (do first, pure wiring), B-46 Ichimoku, B-47 TPO pane, B-48 CVD data spike.
- **Ingest verdict: PARTIAL, deliberately.** The supplied text is an **AI summary of an unnamed video** - no speaker, channel, URL or date - filed at the wiki's lowest provenance tier as [[sources/AI-Summary-Indicator-Taxonomy]]. **One** idea promoted: [[concepts/Indicator-Selection-By-Regime]]. Declined with reasons recorded on the source page: the six-family taxonomy (already covered page-by-page), the lagging-vs-leading section (**self-refuting** - it concedes leading indicators are also derived from past data, dissolving its own distinction), the over/under-rated nominations (unevidenced), the settings cheat-sheet, and the risk/mindset sections (duplicate better-sourced pages).
- **Why that one idea survived:** "regime gates which indicator family may signal" is *testable* and applies to all 44 indicators at once rather than adding a 45th. Its usual missing half is supplied here - it needs a regime classifier, and three already exist in this codebase, one of which (`stai`'s K-means cluster assignment, a volatility-regime label) is **computed and then discarded**. Marked UNVALIDATED with the two-arm test named against the shared `event_edge` scorer.
- **Headline recorded so it is not re-litigated:** this tool is not short of indicators. It carries 44, just hid 9 of 41 scanners for redundancy, and measured three of its four newest indicators to **no edge**. Adding Vortex or RVI would add rows, not information.

## [2026-08-30] build | 6-hour timeframe added to the chart tool - and measured to be a NO-OP on NSE cash
- Added `6h` end to end: `data_source.TF_SECONDS`/`TIMEFRAMES`, the yfinance interval map (fetch 1h, resample), the Hyperliquid path (it has 1h/4h/8h/12h but **no 6h**, so 6h folds from 1h rather than silently serving 4h under a 6h label), and the client `TIMEFRAMES`/`TF_BARS`/`INTRADAY_TFS`. The tf dropdown is generated from `TIMEFRAMES`, so the UI picked it up with no markup change.
- **Measured before shipping, and the result matters: on NSE cash a 6h chart is byte-identical to the 4h chart.** yfinance returns NSE 1h bars localized to Asia/Kolkata at 09:15-15:15. 4h buckets at 08:00/12:00 IST and 6h at 06:00/12:00 - *different* boundaries that both split the session at the same place, so both yield the same 2 bars/day with the same OHLC (verified on RELIANCE: 60 bars each, identical). The NSE session is **6h15m**, so no bucket wider than ~3h divides it cleanly, and "one bar per NSE session" is simply the **1d** chart. Recorded in the resample block so nobody re-derives it.
- **Where 6h is genuinely useful:** 24h markets - crypto gives a clean 00/06/12/18 UTC grid, 4 bars/day, plainly distinct from 4h's 6 bars/day - and the longer MCX session (09:00-23:30). That is also the timeframe [[sources/YT-Devendra-Renko-Intelligence-Commodities]] reaches for, so the addition serves that ingest's commodity half.
- **Hyperliquid fold verified by invariant, not by eye:** every folded 6h bar equals the aggregate of its six 1h candles (open=first, high=max, low=min, close=last, volume=sum) - **0 violations**; spacing is exactly 21600s and buckets land on 00/06/12/18 UTC, i.e. the same clock grid pandas produces on the yfinance side. Fold count also corrected for: 200 six-hour bars need 1,200 one-hour candles, so requesting `bars` of 1h would have returned a sixth of the asked-for history.
- **Completed the per-timeframe tuning tables rather than half-adding the timeframe.** Seven tables in `chart_server.py` and `projection.py` key off timeframe with `.get(tf, default)` - no crash, but 6h would have silently inherited defaults that in several cases equal the **1w** tuning. Added explicit 6h entries interpolated between 4h and 1d (pivot_len 13, prd 16, max_age 35, MAX_MOVE_PCT 38, HORIZON_BARS 16).
- **SVP needed no change at all** - the measured grain-picker from this morning's fix handles 6h on its own: NSE-like 6h (2 bars/day) steps up to weekly grain, crypto 6h (4 bars/day) stays daily. That is the payoff of deriving the grain from the bars instead of a hardcoded timeframe table.
- **Half-shipped state to be aware of:** `chart_dashboard.html` is read from disk per request so the 6h option appears immediately, but `data_source` is imported at server start - a running server returns a clean `400 unknown timeframe '6h'` until restarted. The validator behaving exactly as designed (an honest 400 rather than daily bars wearing a 6h label), but it does mean **the chart server must be restarted** before 6h works.

- The full-listing-history re-run landed and changed the answer, including for scanners that were not the reason I ran it. **Three scanners disagree between the 1500-day and full-history samples** - High Tight Flag (0 unique -> 1), Structural Breakout (0 -> 2), Rounding Bottom (1 -> 0). That disagreement is the signature of a sampling artefact, not a property of the scanner.

Both are now visible again. Rounding Bottom stays visible for the same reason.
- **ATH Breakout and IPO-Base Breakout are now hidden**, on their first *valid* measurement. The full-history run confirms the artefacts I predicted: **ATH fell 67 -> 45 hits** (fake ATHs from reading a 261-bar slice max as an all-time high) and **IPO-Base 14 -> 6** (every stock looked freshly listed because the scanner gates on `len(df)`). Both then measure zero-unique in both samples.

Hub core stays 32 of 41; invariant re-verified - none has >0 unique in either sample.
