## [2026-08-29] ship | Scanner Hub trimmed 41 -> 32 by measurement: nine covered scanners HIDDEN (not deleted)
- Owner: "drop the zero-unique scanners from the Hub list", then "hide them, don't delete". Shipped as `opportunity_hub.COVERED_SCANNERS` + `core_scanners()`, with the Hub card grid defaulting to the **32-scanner core** and a **"Show 9 covered scanners"** checkbox restoring the rest. They stay registered, runnable, backtestable and active in the confluence pipeline.
- **Hidden (all with ZERO unique cells):** 52-Week High Leaders (401 hits, 95% contained in Momentum Continuation), RSI Mid-Band Momentum (269), RSI 60-40 Setup (91), Base Formation (89, 99% in Stage 2), Triple-RS 52W Cross (70, 99% in 52W High Leaders), Structural Breakout (58), Consolidation Breakout 6M-10Y (17, 100% in Pullback Entry), High Tight Flag (11), Minervini VCP valchak (3).
- **Invariant verified after the change:** every hidden scanner has zero unique cells, so their **1,009 combined hits are all still flagged** by something visible. B-35's condition holds by measurement.
- **Why hidden rather than deleted, recorded so it is not re-litigated:** the audit licenses exactly one claim - removing them from the DEFAULT VIEW loses no hit another scanner already flags. It does **not** show they are worthless; a covered scanner can be the more precise expression of a setup, and coverage says nothing about edge. Deletion would assert something unmeasured. The change reduces what must be **read**, not what the system can do.
- **A correction to my own audit.** I had called the ATH Breakout / IPO-Base Breakout counts "floors". That was wrong: they were **invalid**. The replay feeds `df.iloc[:t+1]`, which only means "everything known at t" if the frame starts at the listing - on 1500-day frames **ATH read a 261-bar slice max as an all-time high** (firing on fake ATHs) and **IPO-Base gates on `len(df)` so every stock looked freshly listed** (firing on decades-old companies). Neither error has a predictable direction. Both scanners are therefore **NOT hidden** pending a `--days 0` re-run; `scanner_overlap.py` now documents full history as required for `needs_full_history` scanners and caches those frames separately so the runs cannot mix.
- Edge case closed: the currently-selected scanner stays visible even when covered, so a saved session never renders a caption with no matching card. Small-n entries (n=17/11/3) are flagged in the list rather than presented as equally solid.

## [2026-08-29] fix | The two dead scanners repaired - two different bugs, neither fixed by loosening a threshold
- Both fired **0 times in 2,496 cells**. Instrumented every gate and counted survivors before touching either, which gave two different diagnoses. Written up in [[analyses/Scanner-Overlap-Audit]].
- **EMA-50 Coil - two undocumented gates contradicted the scanner's own premise.** Funnel: 2,496 -> 1,546 above EMA200 -> 749 ADX>=25 -> 409 ADX **rising** -> 375 +DI>-DI -> **1** within 2% of EMA-50. The port carried "ADX rising" and "+DI > -DI", neither in the documented source (Ashok Daniel's StockEdge screen). Both select a stock whose trend is *already accelerating* - i.e. **extended away from its 50-EMA** - while this scanner exists to catch price *resting on* that mean. Of the 375 cells clearing ADX-rising + DI, exactly **one** was also at the EMA-50. Removed the two undocumented gates, restoring the source. Kept `close > EMA200` deliberately: dropping it would lift hits 5 -> 8, which is fire-rate chasing, not correctness. **Now 5 hits (0.20%), 1 unique.** Sample: BEL 2025-01-30, 1.9% below EMA50, +4.3% thrust on 2.9x volume.
- **Minervini VCP (valchak) - the ratio test was applied to the wrong list.** Funnel: 756 trend-template -> 111 geometry -> 61 first-depth -> 59 final-depth -> **0** survived "every step tightens <=0.9x". `detect_vcp_geometry` returns RAW pivot-to-pivot contractions and real bases oscillate: measured depths like `[14.2, 10.2, 8.0, 5.3, 10.5, 5.7]` are clearly contracting overall, but one widening leg fails a strict all-pairs test and essentially every real base has one. valchak's script never tests a raw list - it only *counts* a contraction when it is tighter than the last, so the ratio test belongs to a **tightening run**. Fixed to take the longest run ending at the most recent contraction and apply the depth ranges to that run; also removed the duplicate range checks on the raw list (same bug one layer up) and made the reported footprint describe the run (it printed six legs next to "2 contractions"). **Now 3 hits (0.12%).** Sample: JUBLINGREA 2024-08-07, 15%->5% contractions, close 661.33 > pivot 616.19 on 4.8x volume, 7.3% extended.
- **Honest read:** EMA-50 Coil now earns its slot (1 unique cell). **Minervini VCP still contributes ZERO unique cells** - all 3 hits are also flagged by Stage 2 / Momentum Continuation / 52W High Leaders / ADX Confirmation / Early Reversal, and that containment is **structural** (its Trend Template gate tests essentially what Stage 2 Uptrend tests). Fixing it made it work; whether to keep it is still the B-35 question, and at n=3 the unique measure is noisy.
- **Regression checked:** re-ran the full audit - **37 of 40 scanners have identical hit counts**; only the two repaired scanners and the new Renko screen moved.

## [2026-08-29] measure+build | Scanner overlap audit (B-35 invariant) + Renko Flip screen wired
- Owner asked which Scanner Hub scanners overlap by method, before wiring a Renko FLIP scanner. Names cannot answer that, so `tools/scanner_overlap.py` measures it: every registered scanner replayed point-in-time over **110 NIFTY-500 names x 2,496 (symbol, as-of) cells**, comparing hit SETS via Jaccard, **asymmetric containment** and **unique cells** (the B-35 invariant). Full write-up: [[analyses/Scanner-Overlap-Audit]].
- **Static pass first:** the 1y/5y/10y/6m breakout functions exist but are **not registered** - already folded into "Consolidation Breakout (6M-10Y)", correctly (a 10y breakout clears the 1y and 6m highs by construction). The one true duplicate is **"Zero-Debt Quality PEAD" = "PEAD Drift"** (identical `scan_pead`), already excluded from the pipeline but still listed twice in the Hub.
- **Result: 12 bullish scanners have ZERO unique cells** - removable without breaking the invariant. **Two never fired at all** (Minervini VCP valchak, EMA-50 Coil): **dead, not redundant** - a bug report, not a merge candidate, and it should be triaged before any UI decision. The trend/momentum quartet is one detector under four names (52-Week High Leaders is **95% contained** in Momentum Continuation: 401 hits, 0 unique).
- **What earns its slot:** highest unique contribution goes to HM Buy (125), Near Pivot (68), Order Block Retest (43), Candlestick (32), %R Bottoming (26), Harmonic (19), Falling Wedge (7). Notably **both `MEASURED_EDGE_SCANNERS` members sit in this group** - the two things measured as having edge are also the two seeing something nothing else sees.
- **Caveats recorded rather than buried:** overlap is NOT edge (clones can both be worthless); a zero-unique scanner can still add *precision*; ATH/IPO-base are `needs_full_history` and were under-fed, so their counts are floors.
- **Renko Flip screen shipped** per the owner's explicit decision - "screen not trigger, no BUY implication". `scan_renko_flip` + `_renko_flips` in `scanner_templates.py`, a **port of the shipped `renko.js`** (deliberately NOT reusing `_atr_series`: that seeds from the first TR while renko.js seeds from the SMA of the first n, and brick size feeds a recursive grid where a hair's difference at an epoch boundary diverges permanently). `tools/test_renko_parity.py` pins it: **935 flips across 5 symbols, bar-for-bar identical**, plus final brick/direction/grid-level agreement at a tolerance set to the JS `toFixed(4)` rounding bound (the only observed gap, 4.5e-5, was that rounding - verified before loosening).
- **"Screen, not trigger" enforced structurally, not by wording:** no entry/stop/target keys; added to `PIPELINE_EXCLUDED` so it can never become a confluence vote; not in any scoring family; and NOT in `RISK_SCANNERS` either, because a DOWN flip is neutral information rather than a bearish call. Verified all six constraints hold.
- **Freshness window tuned on measurement, not taste:** fire rate is 4.3 / 8.9 / 13.7 / 17.5 / 24.3% of cells at `max_bars_ago` 0/1/2/3/5. Default set to **2** (13.7%) - catches a Friday flip scanned on Monday without becoming a firehose; the table is in the docstring so it can be re-tuned on evidence.
- **The new screen has the largest unique contribution in the registry: 146 cells (24%) no other scanner flags** - what a *price*-sampled detector buys when everything else is time-sampled. Explicitly not a claim it is profitable: B-43 measured the flip at no forward edge, and the scanner says so in its own description.

## [2026-08-29] ux | SVP card rewritten in plain English - jargon inline, teaching behind a ?, caveat cut to one line
- Owner's complaint: the card is accurate but "way too technical", and the honest measured caveat had grown into a paragraph of statistics sitting on a chart overlay - which is how a caveat gets skipped. Goal was plainer wording **without losing the core point**.
- **Translation lives in the RENDERER, not the engine.** The engine's factor `name`/`detail` strings are also written into the backtest CSVs, so rewording them would silently break comparability with the 2026-08-29 measurement. `chart_dashboard.html` now maps name -> plain label and de-jargons the detail string while **keeping every number**.
- **What changed on the face of the card:** "POC 3935.56 · VA ..." -> "Busiest price 3,935.56 · fair value ..." (with thousands separators, which the levels lacked); "Critical POCs" -> "Key levels (newest first)"; "Target X · invalidate Y" -> "next structure level X · this read breaks if price passes Y" (it is a structure level, not a forecast, and the old label invited the opposite reading); "agree 40%" -> "40% signals conflict - weak, mixed structure"; factor lines now read e.g. "**Buyers defending** - pushed back from 3,934.74 5 times and held" instead of "POC trap (support) - 6x rejection of B-level ... -> reversal-UP". Added a green note when the short- and long-run busiest prices coincide ("a level both agree on") - that agreement *is* the structural read and was previously invisible.
- **The BUY/SELL badge is now MUTED rather than colour-coded** green/red. The word stays - hiding a reading is not the same as qualifying it - but after measuring it at no forward edge, giving it the same visual weight as the structural facts had the card shouting its weakest claim.
- **Caveat cut to one line** ("Read the structure, not the BUY/SELL word - the direction call was measured and has no edge"), with the full evidence moved into a **? guide** that also explains, in plain terms, what POC / fair value / the D-P-B-I shapes / stacked levels / agreement% actually mean. Collapsed by default; the ? toggle is event-isolated so it does not also collapse the body.
- **Three defects caught in verification** (all fixed): the sequence factor rendered "selling pressure building: selling pressure building" because the plain rewrite of the state duplicated the note it prefixes; prices embedded in factor details were ungrouped while the lines above them were grouped; and the guide claimed the direction word was "greyed out" before it actually was. Also capped `.svp-info` to the pane height with `overflow-y:auto` - with the guide open the card grew past the top of the chart and clipped its own header (open 372px vs pane 388px now, and it scrolls).
- Verified live on SHILCTECH.NS 1d through the normal load path.

## [2026-08-29] fix | The SVP fix was correct but never shipped - service worker served indicator JS cache-first
- Owner reported Session VP *still* broken after the grain fix. It was not a second engine bug: `tools/sw.js` cached every static asset **cache-first** under a hardcoded cache name (`tb-charts-v1`) that was never bumped. **Navigations were network-first, so HTML edits shipped, but the indicator modules never did** - the installed client kept serving the pre-fix `session_volume_profile.js` out of CacheStorage, which ignores HTTP semantics, so the `Cache-Control: no-cache` Flask already sends on those routes did nothing.
- **The screenshot carried the fingerprint:** the card showed the NEW caveat text (HTML shipped) alongside the OLD behaviour ("none computable" rather than the new `note` string). New HTML + old JS is only possible if code assets are pinned.
- **My verification error, recorded so it is not repeated:** I had "verified in-browser" by injecting the module from a second server on another port - which bypasses the service worker entirely. That tested every layer except the broken one. **Verify through the same path the user loads.**
- **Fix:** `sw.js` now splits by asset kind - **network-first for code** (`.js`, `.webmanifest`, navigations) with cache as the offline fallback, **cache-first only for immutable binaries** (png/ico/svg/woff). `CACHE_VERSION` -> `v2`; the existing activate handler drops stale caches. The newer modules (percent_r_trend_exhaustion, adx_di, rsi_divergence, evwma_vwap_macd, renko) were also missing from the precache SHELL list and are now included. A forgotten version bump can no longer freeze a code fix - at worst it costs one round trip.
- **Verified end-to-end on the owner's own symbol** (GANESHBE.NS 4h, median **2 bars/day** - exactly the NSE-4h case): service worker registration active and controlling, `tb-charts-v1` gone, `tb-charts-v2` present, the **cached** module contains the fix, and the card now reads `Sessions (weekly): P B P D P B - now B (selling/liquidation) - POC 121.57 - VA 120.00-125.25` with the profile histograms drawing. Previously: 0 sessions, blank profile.

## [2026-08-29] fix+measure | SVP: 4h plotting bug fixed, and the confluence signal finally scored (clean negative)
- **BUG (reported): Session VP drew nothing and the card rendered half-empty** - "Sessions:" blank, no current shape, no POC/VA line, but a Composite POC present. **Root cause:** `segment()` grouped every non-1d/1w timeframe by **calendar day**, but an NSE cash session is ~6.25h, so a **4h** chart has only TWO bars per day, every segment failed the `< 3 bars` profile guard, and `sessions` came back empty. The composite still computed (it profiles all retained bars), which is exactly the card the owner pasted. Crypto was unaffected (24h day = six 4h bars), which is why it survived review.
- **Fix:** grouping grain is now **measured from the bars** rather than read off a timeframe table - median bars per calendar day, stepping day -> week -> month until a period holds >= 3 bars. Same "detect from bar statistics, not hardcoded lists" idiom as `isLikelyLiquidETF`. Verified live: RELIANCE.NS 4h went **0 sessions -> 12** (grain `week`, sequence "B B B B D D"); 1h/30m/15m NSE and BTC/ETH stayed on daily grain; 1d stays weekly, 1w stays monthly. Also: the card now **names the grain** ("Sessions (weekly)") because "session" means a different period per timeframe, and prints an explicit reason instead of a blank when nothing is computable.
- **Confirmed the fix does not invalidate the staged Phase-1 signals:** re-ran the runner on RELIANCE and diffed - **0 differing rows** on the overlap (1d mapped to weekly grain before and after).
- **BACKTEST (the other thing flagged as pending): shipped `tools/svp_backtest.py`** - Phase 2 of the pre-registered design, scoring the signals staged 2026-08-23. Reuses the shared harness (`event_edge`/`strategy_equity` with an injected signal source) so SVP, the EVWMA cross and the Renko flip are directly comparable, and imports the rotation test from `gamma_regime_backtest` rather than copying it.
- **RESULT: clean negative, and worse than "no edge" - the priors are largely SIGN-FLIPPED.** Transitions: turns-BUY **negative at every horizon**; stop-and-reverse median **-80%**, beat buy&hold **0/8**. Standing state: BUY days **underperform** the baseline at every horizon (-0.12 to -0.68pp) and SELL days **outperform** it - backwards, but at rotation **p=0.209**, so not significant. Weights: score deciles **non-monotonic**, `corr(score, 20d fwd) = -0.041`, and BUY-high confidence does *worse* than BUY-low. **Phase-A attribution: 5 of 7 factor families are inverted** (cluster -0.94, va -0.71, sequence -0.67, trap -0.51, regime -0.50; only sweep +0.39 and exhaustion +0.12 point the right way) - which explains the composite, since the headline `trap` factor carries the engine's largest weight (1.5).
- **Explicitly NOT concluding "flip the signs".** The inversion is not significant, and five heavily-correlated factors firing the same wrong way is more plausibly **one common cause** (a mean-reversion tendency on NSE dailies) than five independent errors. Sign-flipping a p=0.21 in-sample result is the overfit the design pre-registered against.
- **Acted on the pre-registered decision rule** ("~1.0x with p>0.1 is a clean negative - keep the priors, mark the engine descriptive/unvalidated, stop"): the chart caveat no longer claims "every weight is unbacktested" (that had become **false**) - it now states the measured result and says to read the **structure** and ignore the direction. **Phase 3 (fitted weights) CANCELLED, not deferred** - its gate was "only if Phase A shows real per-factor signal".
- **Protocol deviations recorded on the design page rather than glossed:** mean forward returns instead of the locked triple-barrier labels; pooled exact rotation instead of the locked 5000-draw block rotation of the panel; no per-instrument p-values. Verdict is unchanged by them, but a promotion could not have been claimed from this run.

## [2026-08-29] ingest | YT long-form interview - retail->pro trader on the amygdala, the benevolent super-ego, and playing the players
- Source: [[sources/YT-Pro-Trader-Amygdala-Options-Philosophy]] (raw in `.raw/transcripts/`). Requested as a **philosophical** ingest, and it is the most substantive psychology source the vault holds. Guest is never named on camera (host sign-off auto-captions as "Emry"); "Andy Kger" is almost certainly **Andy Krieger**. Both identifications are **inferred, not stated**, so **no entity page was created** rather than fabricate a name.
- **New pages:** [[concepts/Trading-Neurobehavioral-Loop]], [[concepts/Constrained-Participants]].
- **The central philosophical claim:** *willpower is the wrong prescription.* "If you don't know how to swim and I throw you in a pool, is discipline going to stop you from drowning?" Bad decisions are produced by a threat system firing before deliberation, which rewards urgency where trading pays patience; cutting a winner is **threat diffusion** and "the market's overbought" is written afterwards; **dopamine rewards anticipation**, so taking the action reinforces the loop. Overtrading falls straight out of this - and the only large lever you hold is *whether to take the trade at all*. The fix is environmental: pre-commit everything decidable, add deliberate friction (his ~15-second "safety catch" visualisation before he is *permitted* to look for trades), and reduce what rides on any one trade.
- **Benevolent super-ego** (3 years of Freudian analysis, 4-5 sessions/week, one usable output): post-trade, ask only *did you follow your process / would you take this trade again* - no rush to congratulate on a win either. **This is now the vault's THIRD independent arrival at process-over-outcome scoring**: Kmer's good-loss/bad-loss, this, and the Brexit verdict *"a bad trade with a good outcome"* (great P&L, no plan - "what conversation would we be having if it had gone the other way?"). Convergence across independent desks is a better warrant than any single source's authority, and that is recorded as the reason to trust it.
- **Claim checked exactly, not quoted:** "you could have a 55% win rate and still lose seven trades in a row." Bernoulli run probability: at 55% WR the chance of a >=7-loss streak is **17.9% / 33.4% / 64.4%** over 100 / 200 / 500 trades. So a genuinely profitable trader taking ~200 trades a year faces a **one-in-three chance of that streak each year**. Not rhetoric - it is the arithmetic behind the 0.5% risk default already on [[concepts/Capital-Management-Methods]].
- **Folded into existing pages rather than duplicated:** [[concepts/Trading-Psychology-Champion]] (benevolent super-ego, safety catch, heart-rate-as-signal, the **linear-progress fallacy** - the brain craves gym-style progress and trading is the polar opposite - plus a **recorded disagreement**: he rejects hiding P&L as "a synthetic buffer"), [[concepts/Capital-Management-Methods]] (trade from stability not scarcity + the streak table), [[concepts/Prop-Trading-Funded-Model]] (sim-firm interests **diametrically opposed** to clients; counterparty risk = "they just might not pay you"; rules-incentivise-behaviour; "just because you play Call of Duty doesn't make you a real soldier").
- **Market-structure half:** options as a *psychological* instrument (spot is binary and the constraint is on you; options are 3-D because time enters, so "price just has to generally do something"), the *"we sell fire insurance to people whose house is already on fire"* vol-selling edge, gamma scalping for futures traders, OpEx strike battlegrounds, and FOMC liquidity withdrawal ("not manipulation, just how the plumbing works"). Noted precisely: **this vault's measured GEX null (B-44) does NOT refute his OpEx claim** - his is strike-level and intraday, ours was a daily regime read, and the two must not be conflated.
- **Caveats recorded on the concept page, not buried:** "amygdala hijack" is a popularisation (Goleman), Freudian structural theory is a framework rather than a finding, and none of the prescriptions depend on the model being literally true - they stand on behavioural evidence. Plus survivorship: one route, told after it worked, including a quit-your-job leap he explicitly tells you not to copy.

## [2026-08-29] build+measure | B-43 Renko engine - shipped on the candles, and the flip came back NULL
- **Shipped:** `tools/renko.js` (`window.RENKO`, pure compute), the `renko` chart overlay in `chart_dashboard.html`, `tools/renko_backtest.js` + `tools/renko_backtest.py`. Route added to `chart_server.py`.
- **Three decisions that made it measurable rather than decorative:** (1) **close-based** bricks, not high/low - a brick completed by a close is fillable at the next open, one completed intrabar by a wick completes at a price nobody can prove they would have got; (2) **causal ATR-epoch brick sizing** - the brick is re-derived only every 20 bars from ATR over bars *strictly before* that point, because the obvious `brick = ATR(5)` rebuilds the grid from the latest ATR and **repaints all of history** (what most platforms do: fine to look at, fatal to measure), while a single fixed brick sized for RELIANCE at Rs 232 is noise at Rs 1,316; (3) **structure on candles, never instead of them** - `pane.bars` untouched, the same rule Heikin Ashi follows, so every other indicator still sees real OHLC.
- **Engine invariant verified in-browser on live data:** across 470 bars the grid never sat more than `reversal x brick` against the trend without flipping - **zero violations**, once each bar is checked against its own epoch's brick rather than the final one (my first check used the final brick and produced a false alarm).
- **Harness reuse instead of a second copy:** `evwma_vwap_macd_backtest.event_edge`/`.strategy_equity` now take an injected `signals_fn` (default unchanged), so Renko scores through the *same* scorer on the *same* 23-name basket and ~19y - the two findings are directly comparable rather than two differently-built numbers.
- **RESULT: clean null.** flip_up (LONG) edge **-0.06 / -0.08 / -0.04 / -0.77%** at 5/10/20/60d (p 0.62/0.60/0.85/0.07) - **negative at every horizon**. flip_dn +0.11/+0.05/-0.19/-1.00 (60d p=0.01*), which is **not** a finding: 8 cells tested (~0.4 expected under 0.05 by chance), at the longest horizon where overlapping windows inflate Welch hardest - the exact artefact B-44 measured. Equity: stop-and-reverse median **-39%**, beat buy&hold **0/23**; long-only +267% vs +827%, 1/23. At 20bps: -56% / +223%.
- **The finding worth carrying forward:** Renko *did* deliver noise reduction - on the same names it fires **~40% less often** than the EVWMA cross (RELIANCE 202 flips vs 320, TITAN 202 vs 328). It removed signals without improving the survivors. **Fewer decisions of the same quality is an ergonomic benefit, not an edge** - and several open items (B-35 family-lens UI, and the noise-reduction thrust generally) rest on exactly that conflation.
- **Chart feature kept** and deliberately labelled **FLIP up / FLIP down**, never BUY/SELL: a two-colour staircase over the candles, flip arrows, and one labelled price line carrying live state (brick size in points and as a % of price, plus bricks travelled). Structure and state, which is what the measurement supports.
- **MCX/commodities sub-item stays gated** - unchanged owner scope call, not assumed in scope.

## [2026-08-29] build+measure | B-44 gamma-regime (GEX) engine - shipped, tested, and the claim came back NULL
- **Shipped:** `tools/gamma_exposure.py` (naive dealer GEX from the `IDO` bhavcopy rows - per-strike IV inverted from settlement prices by bisection, net/gross GEX, gamma flip, call/put walls, coverage), `tools/gamma_regime_backtest.py`, `tools/test_gamma_exposure.py` (**14 tests, all passing**). Cheap exactly as predicted: `nse_oi_momentum._download_bhavcopy` already pulls the file, and `UndrlygPric` on the same row means spot needs no second source.
- **A test caught a real bug before it could bias anything.** The IV inversion rejected prices at/below **undiscounted** intrinsic - but a deep-ITM *European* put legitimately trades below K-S, since exercise waits for expiry. It was silently dropping exactly those rows, thinning the PUT side and biasing the net regime label **positive**. Fixed to the European lower bound `K*exp(-rT) - S`. Invisible in the output; only the round-trip identity test found it.
- **Built the full 480-session history** (2024-09-19 .. 2026-08-28 - all the UDiFF-format bhavcopy that exists), coverage 87.5% NIFTY / 89.7% BANKNIFTY.
- **RESULT: clean null.** NIFTY forward realised vol +0.28/+0.24/**-0.40** pp at 5/10/20d (p_rot 0.84/0.83/0.67); next-day absolute move +0.03 (0.69); net/gross **tercile** split also null (0.58-0.95); 20-day-high breakout follow-through -0.10 pp over 49 events (0.84). BANKNIFTY has the right sign at every vol horizon (+0.77/+1.02/+1.59) but p_rot 0.75/0.68/0.58 - a hint, not a result. **The "cautiously positive" prior I recorded on this item was wrong.**
- **Nothing wired to a dashboard or to `REGIME_GATE`.** The backlog item said test before shipping UI; the test failed, so the tool stays a research instrument that prints a daily *description* of dealer positioning with no demonstrated forward information.
- **Methodology finding that outlives the null and applies repo-wide.** The first pass used Welch, like every other backtest here, and reported the **pre-registered expected-null** directional test at **p=0.031**. Artefact: daily sampling against a 20-day forward window means consecutive observations share 19/20 of their data. Replaced with an **exact circular-rotation p** (every shift of the label series enumerated, preserving the autocorrelation of both series) - same test reads **0.32**. Both columns now print side by side. Any repo backtest sampling daily against multi-day horizons has this exposure; `stride=5` mitigates it, rotation measures it.
- **Residual B-44r:** is the naive convention backwards for NSE? It hard-codes a US folk model (customers buy puts / sell calls). Indian index options plausibly mirror it - retail buys cheap weekly OTM on both sides, prop/institutions write - which would make dealers short gamma on *both* sides and the 70%/85% positive-day count an artifact of the assumption. Recorded as a *different measurement*, not a re-run: sign becomes uninformative, so the test moves to gamma **magnitude**. One pre-specified alternative, not licence to fish.

## [2026-08-29] ingest | YT interview - Chris Kmer, Robbins World Cup (monthly) champion: order flow + GEX process
- Source: [[sources/YT-Chris-Kmer-World-Cup-Order-Flow]] (raw transcript in `.raw/transcripts/`). Prop-firm channel with a mid-roll sponsor read; championship claim is **one month** of a percentage-ranked contest division - recorded as process, explicitly not as evidence of edge.
- **New pages:** [[entities/Chris-Kmer]], [[concepts/Gamma-Exposure-GEX]], [[strategies/Forced-Participation-Reversal]].
- **The organising idea** is not a setup: **forced participation** - trade where one side has committed size and is failing to be rewarded, because trapped participants must transact against themselves to exit. He says the entry model is "the final 5-10%". Four gates: environment (HTF structure + gamma regime) -> location (value area AND 0.705/0.786/0.886 fib, which must agree) -> confirmation (footprint volume+delta: effort -> no result -> dominance shift, **two** failed attempts, 400% imbalance) -> a hard participation floor (20,000 contracts/5-min MNQ, 90-min window, 0-2 trades/day).
- **Arithmetic checked, as with the Renko ingest.** The stated 60-65% WR / 1.5R / PF 1.8 triple **cannot all hold**: 1.5R at 62.5% implies PF ~2.5, not 1.8; PF 1.8 implies either 54.5% WR at 1.5R or a realised average win of ~1.1R. Benign reconciliation - he trails and moves to break-even constantly, so 1.5R is the *target*. **PF 1.8 is the load-bearing number** (+0.3R/trade). And the 100% month then implies **4-8% of account risked per trade** (`(1+0.3r)^30 = 2`), i.e. 8-16x the vault's 0.5% default in [[concepts/Capital-Management-Methods]] - the contest return is substantially a *sizing* artifact.
- **Unit error corrected** in [[concepts/Prop-Trading-Funded-Model]]: "drawdown 2,000, target 3,000, that's literally a 1.5R, so just take 1.5R trades" conflates a **per-trade** R with a **per-account** ratio. Reaching +3,000 before -2,000 is gambler's ruin (per-trade risk vs buffer, win rate, sequencing), not a payoff-ratio guarantee. The underlying point - capped downside changes optimal aggression in an *evaluation* - survives; the sizing does not transfer to a personal account.
- **Folded into existing pages rather than duplicated:** [[concepts/Order-Flow]] (**absorption != reversal** - it happens constantly; only the dominance shift after it is tradeable, and order flow should *reduce* trade count, not raise it), [[concepts/Trading-Psychology-Champion]] (A/B/C-game sessions scored on execution not P&L; **back-end optimisation** - you don't control how many A+ setups appear, you do control the unnecessary losses; a winning trade taken out-of-process is still a **bad trade**; "a rule without an action attached is not a rule"), [[concepts/Premium-Discount-Zones]] (the deeper 0.705/0.786/0.886 band + the must-be-**outside**-value-area agreement rule, and 0.886 as hard invalidation).
- **Backlog: B-44** - gamma-regime read for NIFTY/BANKNIFTY. Cheap because `nse_oi_momentum` **already downloads** the whole F&O bhavcopy and discards the `STO`/`IDO` option rows `pcr.py` later reclaimed; strike + OI is the raw material for a naive GEX, **no new network call**. Scope limit stated up front: bhavcopy is EOD, so this is a **daily** regime series, not the intraday tool the source uses. Prior recorded as **cautiously positive** - the most favourable in the backlog, because GEX rests on a documented hedging obligation rather than a noticed pattern - with the naive-GEX positioning assumption named as the first suspect if NSE data shows nothing.
- **Explicitly not built:** footprint/delta order flow (needs tick data the repo has never carried) and the intraday futures lane (already an out-of-scope decision). Filed as knowledge.

## [2026-08-29] ingest | YT podcast (Devendra, part 2) - "Renko Intelligence on Candles" + commodities
- Source: [[sources/YT-Devendra-Renko-Intelligence-Commodities]] (recorded 2026-08-24; raw transcript filed in `.raw/transcripts/`). Guest's surname is never stated and the host is named as both "Mamta" and "Mukta" - recorded as-is rather than guessed. The *earlier* podcast it references (candle construction + Renko calculation) is NOT in this vault.
- **New page [[concepts/Renko-Charts]]** - first Renko coverage in the vault. Cross-linked from [[concepts/Point-and-Figure-Charts]] (sibling chart type) and [[concepts/SuperTrend]].
- **The one transferable idea, reconstructed:** brick = `ATR(5)` on a **session-length** bar / (execution bars per session) = the average range of one execution bar. "Session-length" is just one bar per trading day: ~6h NSE, ~14h MCX.
- **Both of the source's worked examples fail their own arithmetic** and the correction is filed: Nifty `100/6/2` = **8.33**, not the "53" printed (that number is contamination from the gold example, and 8.33 is separately mislabelled a 1-minute brick in Ch 7); gold `1500/28` = **53.6** (a 14h session holds 28 half-hour blocks, not the stated 30). The "breaking above 268.2" level is an unusable fragment. Rule survives, numbers don't.
- **Three claims corrected on the concept page:** Renko does not "remove lag" (it converts time-lag into price-lag = one brick, and a 1-second chart changes nothing); Renko bricks are **not fillable bars** (no usable timestamp, last brick repaints, one brick spans many real bars) so any test must run on the underlying OHLC - the classic fake-equity-curve trap; and brick size must be derived, not re-optimised per symbol.
- **Contradiction with the vault, backed by our own numbers:** the "market is a zero-sum game" framing is false for equities - the every-bar baseline measured in [[concepts/EVWMA-VWAP-MACD]] is +0.31/+0.72/+1.54/**+5.05%** at 5/10/20/60d over ~19y. Also flagged: "10% of capital per trade" never distinguishes allocation from risk (vault default is 0.5% risk / 20% allocation cap in [[concepts/Capital-Management-Methods]]).
- **Backlog: B-43** (Renko / time-independent chart engine - `grep -ri renko tools/` returns nothing; pure module + candle-overlay plug, same shape as `evmacd`/`prte`/`stai`; prior recorded as neutral-to-skeptical). Commodity/MCX coverage recorded as a **gated sub-item**, an owner scope call, not assumed in scope.
- Follow-up lead: ingest **Prashant Shah**'s published Renko/P&F work - the checkable version of this material, without the webinar funnel.

Stop-and-reverse median **-29%** vs buy&hold **+827%**, beats buy&hold 1/23; long-only +314%, also 1/23. At 20bps/flip: -64% / +204%.

## [2026-08-27] build | Heikin Ashi candles in the chart tool (TradingView-style)
- Added a per-pane chart-type dropdown (Candles / Heikin Ashi) next to the timeframe selector in `chart_dashboard.html`. `heikinAshiBars()` transform + `applyPriceStyle()` feed the candle series raw bars or their HA transform per `pane.chartType`; wired into load, live-tick, and the toggle; persisted to `tb_pane_cfg` localStorage.
- **Design decision:** HA is DISPLAY-ONLY — `pane.bars` stays real OHLC, so all indicators (RSI/VWAP/patterns + the new B-40 VWAP scanner) keep computing from true prices, never the smoothed series. Live ticks recompute the HA series (a raw `series.update()` would break the HA chain, since each HA bar depends on the prior one).
- Verified in-browser: select renders in all 4 panes, HA math correct vs hand-computed values, toggle fires cleanly (no console errors), chartType persists. Hollow/Line left as extensible follow-ons (they need a series-type swap, not just a data transform).

## [2026-08-27] build | B-40 — VWAP-as-signal scanner shipped ("VWAP Reclaim/Support")
- `scan_vwap_reclaim` + `_rolling_vwap` + `_anchored_vwap` in `scanner_templates.py`, registered as "VWAP Reclaim/Support" (Entry, scanner #40 of 40). Fills the trend+pullback stack's "pull back to a mean" leg, which was only an EMA before.
- Fires on a **reclaim** (close crosses back above a rising VWAP on ≥20d-avg volume) OR a **pullback-hold** (a bar tags VWAP within 1% and bounces). With-trend only (close > EMA-50), VWAP rising over 10 bars, not extended (≤6% above the mean). Emits `time_stop=15`.
- **Design correction (kept in the docstring):** first cut anchored VWAP to the 120-bar low (Brian-Shannon style) — but that mean sits far below price in a trend, so the not-extended gate always rejected and it never fired (0/8, even a hand-built pullback). Switched the *event* engine to a **rolling 20-day VWAP** (price actually oscillates around it); kept `_anchored_vwap` for the catalyst-anchor follow-up.
- Verified: synthetic pullback fires (score 80), downtrend rejected, real-data selective (TITAN pullback-hold, 1/8 large-caps), `py_compile` clean.
- **Open:** anchored-VWAP-from-catalyst + chart click-to-anchor is B-4/B-12; scanner is unmeasured until `backtest_scanner` is run on it (the one measurable claim).

## [2026-08-26] build | UI concurrency — per-session serial job queue (queue, don't cancel)
- **Problem:** Streamlit reruns (interrupts) the single script thread on ANY click, so a long inline task (funnel `od.run`, ~minutes; sentiment `compute_sentiment`, ~25s) is CANCELLED when the user clicks another button. Owner wants the second action QUEUED and run after the in-progress one.
- **Shipped:** `ui_jobs.py` — a per-session `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1)` serial queue. `submit`/`submit_unique` return instantly (rerun completes, UI stays live); a submit while one runs waits its turn, never cancels it. Attaches the script's `ScriptRunContext` to the worker so cached fetchers work off-thread. `render_panel()` (top) shows running/queued jobs; `autorefresh()` (bottom) polls via sleep+rerun so the full page still renders and stays clickable. `take_result(label)` consumes a finished job's output once. **Standalone self-test proves the serial guarantee** (B queued mid-A runs strictly after A).
- **Wired** both long tasks in `dashboard_app.py` (funnel run + inline sentiment refresh) to the queue with a global status panel. Core logic unit-proven; the in-app behaviour is behind the login (not clicked through). App boots clean.

## [2026-08-26] measure | B-33 floor precision — tightened on 55 names + kept-vs-tail test
- Re-ran `regime_backtest.py --calibrate` on **55 names / ~3,000 signal bars**, adding a direct **kept-vs-tail** separation test and a finer threshold grid. (Caught + fixed a shadowing bug: the significance flag was named `sig`, clobbering the `sig` dict.)
- **RISK-ON:** a rating floor genuinely SORTS — kept-vs-tail separation significant at 40 and again 65–75 (kept +0.4–0.5%, sub-floor tail negative). Floor 50 is in-band; ~65 is the cleanest cut.
- **MIXED — correction:** the earlier 15-name "+1.2% edge" was a **small-sample artifact**. On 458 bars the kept edge is ~0 and NO floor significantly sorts. Downgraded the gate note to "coin-flip; mild floor only," removed the false +1.2% claim.
- **RISK-OFF:** now **significant at nearly every threshold** (−1 to −2.2%, p≤0.05), and higher-rated names do significantly WORSE than the tail (kept<tail p=0.02 @≥70) — the bull-trap finding is statistically pinned. Floors kept (RISK-ON/MIXED/UNKNOWN 50, RISK-OFF None); the calibration comment + MIXED note corrected in `opportunity_daily.REGIME_GATE`. Filed on [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-33).

Shows the Nifty vs-200-SMA verdict on the right. Composition unit-checked for all four regimes; dashboard compiles.

## [2026-08-26] measure+build | B-33 gate floors CALIBRATED — rating floor 50, RISK-OFF near-zero longs
- **`regime_backtest.py --calibrate`** — sweeps a rating threshold per regime and compares the KEPT set's fwd edge (@20d) to the dropped TAIL's, vs a regime-matched baseline. 15 names.
- **Result:** RISK-ON — kept edge mildly +, tail turns − above rating 50 → **floor ≈50** (edges small, not significant — rating barely separates in a rising tape). MIXED — kept edge **solidly +1.2% to +2.0%** at every threshold → bullish signals genuinely work; floor 50 fine. RISK-OFF — kept edge **NEGATIVE at every threshold and WORSE at higher rating** (≥70 → −2.5%, ≥80 → −3.1%): the best-looking bullish setups below the 200 SMA are the worst bull traps.
- **Encoded** in `opportunity_daily.REGIME_GATE`: added a calibrated `min_rating` (RISK-ON/MIXED/UNKNOWN = 50; **RISK-OFF = None** because a rating floor selects *worse* names there) and cut RISK-OFF cap 10→**5** (near-zero longs). `_apply_regime_gate` now takes `rating_key` (stored `overall_rating` / live `overall`); both paths + unit test pass. Floors are now data-set, not judgment. Filed on [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-33).

- **Built on the proven premise:** `opportunity_daily.REGIME_GATE` + `_apply_regime_gate(rows, regime, top_n, score_key)`. A regime-dependent **score floor + name cap** replaces the flat `top_n=100` cut: RISK-ON floor 45 / no cap; MIXED 55 / 40; RISK-OFF **65 / 10** (hard); UNKNOWN cautious. So the list shrinks by itself with conviction (B-33) and shrinks HARD in RISK-OFF where bullish signals were measured to lose (B-32).
- **Wired in two places:** `opportunity_daily.run()` (stored/scheduled shortlist, with a `_say` log line + `regime_gate` in the result) and the dashboard **live** Opportunity-Hub pipeline (gates on `rank_score`, shows a throttle callout). Unit-tested the gate across all four regimes; both files compile.

## [2026-08-26] measure | B-32 RISK-OFF premise PROVEN — bullish scanners pick bull traps below the 200 SMA
- **Shipped:** `regime_backtest.py` — buckets forward returns by the point-in-time Nifty regime (mirrors `opportunity_daily._market_context`) and reports per-regime **edge over a regime-matched baseline** (isolates signal quality from market beta). scipy-optional. 30 names, ~7,400 bars.
- **Result:** composite bullish-scanner edge +0.22 @20d in RISK-ON → **−1.78 @20d in RISK-OFF (p<0.005)**; all four scanners flip significantly negative in RISK-OFF (stage2 −2.87, momentum −3.13, 52w_high −2.69). **The premise B-32 needed is proven.**
- **Sharper than expected:** RISK-OFF *baseline* was POSITIVE (+1.89 @20d — random below-200 bars bounced), so this is NOT market beta. Bullish signals below the 200 SMA underperform a *random* below-200 name by 1.3–3% — they actively pick **bull traps**. Justifies throttling (or inverting) bullish output in RISK-OFF far more than "bad tape" would. B-33's conviction gate should be harshest in RISK-OFF. Filed on [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] (B-32). **Throttle now earned — build next.**

## [2026-08-26] measure | ADX/DI on small-caps — regime-dependent, one counterintuitive result
- Ran `adx_di_backtest.py` on 54 NSE small/micro-caps (12,842 baseline bars) vs the earlier 20 large-caps. **Bare bull cross now significantly LOSES** (−0.93% @10d p=0.02, −2.17% @60d) — never a buy. **Confirmed up-state edge is STRONGER in small-caps** (+1.82% @60d, p<0.005 vs +1.20% large-cap) — the ADX Trend Confirmation scanner earns most here, at the ~60d position horizon. **Counterintuitive:** the bearish −DI/ADX "downside" state precedes a short-term BOUNCE in small-caps (+0.51% @5d, p<0.005) — a contrarian oversold tell, NOT a short trigger. Regime-comparison table filed on [[concepts/ADX]].

## [2026-08-26] build | ADX/DI rule backtest registered (adx_di_backtest.py) + refined finding
- **Shipped:** `adx_di_backtest.py` — permanent, re-runnable point-in-time harness for the ADX/DI rule variants (bare bull/bear cross, confirmed bull state, bearish state), reusing `_adx_di_series` + scipy-optional Welch. `py adx_di_backtest.py --live`. Synthetic machinery check + live mode.
- **Refined result** (20 liquid names, 5,576 baseline bars): bare bull cross = noise; **confirmed up-state (+DI>−DI, ADX≥25 rising) flat short-term but +1.20% @60d, p=0.05** — a mild longer-horizon trend-persistence edge (so it's a position-horizon confirmation, not a trigger). **Bearish −DI/ADX state ≈ zero forward edge** — the downside read is a context flag, not a predictive short. Filed on [[concepts/ADX]].

## [2026-08-26] measure | ADX/DI as a standalone buy signal — cross is negative-edge, state is neutral
- **Question:** can you get an actionable buy/sell signal from an ADX+DI subchart? **Measured it** (repo `_adx_di_series`, point-in-time, 20 liquid names, 27,860 baseline bars, ~6y).
- **Bare bullish +DI/−DI cross (n=1042): NEGATIVE edge**, significant at 60d (−1.01%, p=0.04) — you buy the lag. **DI-cross + ADX>25-rising on the same bar fires ~never (n=6)** — the textbook combined trigger barely exists. **Confirmed DI+ADX *state* (n=1091): ~neutral** — flat to +0.86% @60d (p=0.10, not significant).
- **Conclusion:** ADX/DI is a regime/context filter + confirmation, **not a standalone entry** — now measured, matching the concept page's prior stance and how `scan` already uses it. Filed the evidence table on [[concepts/ADX]]. Echoes the [[strategies/TechnoFunda-Delete-Funnel]] result: edge lives in longer-horizon strength, not triggers.

## [2026-08-26] measure | B-38 price-legs backtest — F7 carries the edge, the legs don't stack
- **First measured result.** 20 liquid names, ~4,700 baseline bars, ~6y. **F7 relative strength is the only price leg with a significant edge** (+0.44% @20d p=0.04, +1.23% @60d p<0.005). **F6 200-DMA ≈ zero** (these names are almost always above it → barely a filter); **F8 accumulation mildly negative** (−0.29% @20d). **The F6∧F7∧F8 combo does NOT beat F7 alone** (+0.02 vs +0.44 @20d) — stacking dilutes momentum's edge and cuts the sample.
- **Answer to the price half of B-38's claim: no, the deletes don't stack — momentum does the work.** Caveat: favourable RS regime + liquid universe where F6 rarely excludes anything; broader/small-cap/bear-inclusive set could separate F6/F8. Fundamental legs still untested. Actionable: revisit the F8 footprint (not predictive here); F7 earns its place.
- Filed on [[strategies/TechnoFunda-Delete-Funnel]] (Backtest section). This is the payoff of the `funnel_backtest` infra — a real, honest, mildly-negative-for-the-thesis finding, in the repo's measurement-first spirit.

## [2026-08-09] ingest | Path of the Planets Pine — declination had zero occurrences repo-wide, so the module got built with the ingest
- **Source:** [[sources/BlueprintResearch-Path-of-the-Planets-Pine]] — Pine v6, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. Geo **and** heliocentric longitudes plus **declinations for all ten bodies**, with **per-planet, per-series toggles as the entire UI** (~30 booleans across three groups) and **future projection to 500 bars**. The user's note — *"we should have option for selection of planet"* — is exactly that toggle grid.
- **Notable and worth crediting: it makes no market claim at all.** It plots positions and stops. That is the line this repo holds, and it is rare enough in astro material that the usual caveat did not need adding on ingest.
- ### The audit that decided to build rather than document
  Before writing anything, checked what we actually had. **Declination: zero occurrences repo-wide** — despite [[concepts/Out-of-Bounds-Planets]] existing since 2026-07-08 as an *entirely declination-based* concept whose own Examples section read *"worth checking once the local engine can compute declination."* **Heliocentric: zero.** **Outer planets:** present only as *orbital-period constants* in `financial_astrology_methods.py` (Uranus 84.01y / Neptune 164.79y / Pluto 247.94y), never as positions. **Planetary longitude:** either parsed from an AstroHora CSV (`astrohora_loader.py`) or computed for the **Moon only**, for nakshatra.
- **Verified `ephem` supplies the whole data layer** — all ten bodies, geocentric longitude, declination, heliocentric lon/lat — before committing to build. Same finding as the panchang work: no vendor, no API, no rate limit.
- > [!key-insight] Documenting an indicator we could not compute would have been the FIFTH documented-but-not-coded gap this week
  > After the order-block selection rule, volume-profile HVN/LVN, the evening-star gap requirement and the pmax WXY labels. Having just named that pattern in the EW ingest, adding to it would have been absurd — so **[planet_positions.py](tools/planet_positions.py) shipped with the source page.** Per-planet selection is the API, not an afterthought: `PLANETS` is the selection surface and every entry point takes `bodies=`, so a UI renders one checkbox per row and passes the subset through.
- **What it provides:** `position()` · `positions(when, bodies)` · `path(body, start, end, step_days, field)` for the time series the Pine draws · `out_of_bounds(when, bodies)`. Ten bodies with symbol and the source's colour palette for UI parity. **Selection is strict** — an unknown name raises rather than silently returning nothing, because "typo'd planet" and "no data for that planet" must not look identical.
- **Cross-module consistency asserted, not assumed.** It reuses `panchang._lahiri_ayanamsa` and `_to_ephem` rather than re-deriving them, so the two cannot drift on ayanamsa. Verified: Moon sidereal longitude matches `moon_nakshatra()` to **1e-9** (63.374290 both), and retrogrades match `panchang.retrogrades()` **exactly** on the five classical planets. 8/8 checks.
- **[[concepts/Out-of-Bounds-Planets]] is computable for the first time.** Live 2026-08-09: **Moon +26.84°, Mars +23.65° — both OOB.** Mars only marginally, which is itself a design lesson recorded on the page: a body within ~0.2° of the limit flickers, so an event study needs **hysteresis or a minimum-duration rule**, not a bare threshold. Also caught a **threshold discrepancy** — the page used 23.4344°, the module uses **23.4392911°** (J2000 mean obliquity, i.e. the Sun's own maximum, which is what makes the limit mean anything). Cannot change a verdict except exactly on the boundary, but flagged for reconciliation to one constant.
- **Definitional note recorded so nobody "fixes" it:** the source is **tropical**, all our panchang work is **sidereal** (Lahiri). Sign labels differ by ~24° — about a whole sign — by definition, not by error.
- **Still not built** (stated on the page rather than implied): OOB **transition-day events** — the module answers *"is it OOB now"*, not *"when did the window start"*, which is what `reversal_calendar` would need. And nothing here is measured: this repo's only completed astro measurements are **nulls** ([[analyses/FA-2-Rahu-Kaal-Measured]]), so an ephemeris overlay is context, not edge.

## [2026-08-09] ingest | SweeGlu EW chart mirror-compared against our own engines — and our two engines disagree with each other first
- **Source:** [[sources/SweeGlu-EW-NIFTY-Chart3-2026-08-05]] — NIFTY 50 **3D**, Deepak Kumar, labelled **(y) of (b) of (x) of (B or X) of (iv) of Major (3)**: six nested degrees, all corrective below Major (3), plus an **explicit alternate** ("B or X"). Projects a rally into **26,773–27,864** (100%/123.6% fib) by Oct–Nov 2026, **then a decline to (X) ≈ 22,300**; support shelf **22,879–23,078**. Full comparison: [[analyses/EW-Engine-vs-SweeGlu-NIFTY]]. `overview.md` untouched — a third party's projection is not our thesis.
- **Both runnable engines were actually run**, not described: `ew_module.py` on NIFTY daily, and `elliott_wave.js` — the chart tool's own engine — via node on the same 3D bars the source uses.
- > [!warning] The headline is internal: our two engines return different structures
  > `ew_module.py`: **W2** of an impulse, W1 21,744→26,373, Wave-3 targets **31,097 (45%) · 35,726 (30%) · 28,236 (15%) · 40,356 (10%)**, note *"watch for W3 launch above 26,373"*. `elliott_wave.js`: an **expanded-flat correction, complete**, C targets 23,954/24,168/24,516 **already exceeded**, invalidation 23,606. Those are not two views of one structure. **The dashboard shows the Python chip beside the JS overlay, so one page can display both** — the `detect_bullish_rsi_divergence` split again. And the Python engine contradicts *itself*: `wave_pos: W2` printed beside `owl_signal: "W3-past"`.
- ### The gap asymmetry, concretely
  **Not one of our Wave-3 targets falls inside their 26,773–27,864 band.** Nearest is 28,236 — the *15%-weighted minimum* — sitting ~370 pts **above** their ceiling; our primary is **3,233 pts / 11.6% above their upper bound**. Not a disagreement about magnitude: two models whose output ranges barely intersect. Direction agrees near-term and diverges after — ours is up *because a Wave 3 is starting*, theirs is up *because a corrective (b) is ending*.
- **One genuine convergence, and it is the useful part: 23,606.** Both our engines land on that **identical** pivot by different logic (Python's W2 low; JS's wave-B low / invalidation), and SweeGlu's shelf sits 500–700 pts below it. Three methods, one neighbourhood — the level all three agree decides something.
- **Their bear case cannot disprove our bull case.** Their terminal (X) ≈22,300 is *above* our stop of 21,744, so NIFTY could complete their whole projected decline while our count merely re-labels. **Waiting for one to be proved wrong will not resolve them.**
- **Our own engine says its NIFTY count is weak** — on 3D the JS engine found 18 impulses, **median score 40, max 59**, with **14 of 18 flagged diagonal** (W4 overlapping W1), and its best structure is a *bearish* 5-wave from Jan 2026. That is a reason to distrust our 31,097, not only their 27,864. **And the disagreement is not a resolution artefact:** `ew_module.py` returns the identical count on 1D/2D/3D.
- ### A correction I had to make mid-analysis, and it produced the better finding
  I first wrote that our tooling had **zero** corrective-degree vocabulary. Checking [[concepts/Elliott-Wave]] revealed a **third** engine — `pmax.pine`'s v2 9-position cycle, which *alternates* impulse and corrective cycles with prime marks. So the "impulse-only" claim was wrong and I fixed it. **But its corrective branch emits `(a)(ax)(ay)(b)(c)(az)(ax)(d)(e)` — an ABCDE triangle, not a WXY combination** (`f_waveLabel` line 196). A triangle is one pattern; WXY is two or three joined by X waves, which is exactly what SweeGlu draws. Conclusion survives, reason corrected.
- > [!contradiction] The wiki documents WXY support for pmax that the code has never had
  > [[concepts/Elliott-Wave]] line 72 **and** [[sources/PMax-Optimization-Pine]] both state pmax's corrective cycles are `W, w1, x1, X, Y, y1, z1, XX, Z` — WXY vocabulary. The code emits ABCDE. **That string appears nowhere in any source file — only in those two markdown pages.** Presumably the upstream TradingView script had it and our port simplified. **Fourth documented-but-not-coded gap this week**, after the order-block selection rule, volume-profile HVN/LVN, and the evening-star gap. The pattern is now worth naming: **this repo's pages describe upstream sources more faithfully than they describe its own code.**
- **Bug found:** `ew_module.analyze_ew` raises `TypeError` on **weekly** bars — the timeframe a position-degree count would most naturally use.
- **Standing caveat:** no EW output here has ever been scored — [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] has never run on it, and the SweeGlu chart carries no track record. Two unvalidated models that disagree, plus a third that disagrees with both. **Open:** reconcile or declare one engine canonical; reconcile `wave_pos` vs `owl_signal`; fix weekly; score the dated band later. WXY support is a real gap but **should not be built before EW output is measured at all** — the same ordering argument as FA-2 before FA-3.

- > [!note] Reclassifying pushed "missing required sections" UP, 76 → 82, and that is correct
  > Those five pages were previously typed `indicator` / `chart-type`, which matched no schema, so **they were never checked**. Giving them `category: concept` subjected them to the concept schema for the first time and surfaced real gaps (`Common Mistakes`, `Examples in This Wiki`). A number getting worse because a check finally applies is not a regression.
- ### The lint flagged its own log entry, which is a fair catch
  The previous entry *quoted* the bad bare-name links as examples, and the checker matched them — reporting 8 phantom dead links in `log.md`. **Prose about a link is not a link.** Fixed properly rather than by rewording: the checker now strips fenced blocks and inline code before extracting links, which also cleared the long-standing `[[wiki-relative-link]]` false positive on the kepano source page.
- **Promoted the checker to [tools/wiki_lint.py](tools/wiki_lint.py)** — `python tools/wiki_lint.py`. It had been a throwaway scratch script run three times in one day, which is its own smell. The docstring records the two things it deliberately does *not* do: it reports `sources: N` mismatches without fixing them (its counter over-counts pages with indented sub-references — Kelly-Criterion reads 4 where the page has 2), and it ignores links inside code.
- **Final state:** dead refs **90 → 9** (all 9 now genuinely-missing targets, not convention breaks) · unindexed **12 → 0** · frontmatter gaps **25 → 16** · orphans **13 → 11** · every page now carries one of the five valid categories.
- **Still open, unchanged and deliberately so:** the 9 real dead targets (`entities/TRENT`, `entities/NIFTY`, `strategies/Sector-Momentum-Map` — create or delink?), 16 remaining frontmatter gaps (mostly `timeframe:` on older strategy/analysis pages), the 82 schema-section gaps (where the *schema* is likely wrong for roadmap-style analyses), and the 16 unverified `sources: N` counts.

## [2026-08-09] lint | 235 pages checked — 115 dead links repaired, and the biggest defect was our own linking convention
- Full scripted health check against the LINT spec in `CLAUDE.md`: **238 files (235 content + index/log/overview)** — 92 sources, 61 concepts, 33 analyses, 26 strategies, 17 entities. Index header verified accurate (`pages: 235`, `sources_ingested: 93` both match reality).
- ### Fixed: 115 dead wikilinks across 27 files (90 dead refs → 10)
  Almost all were **our own convention being broken**, not missing pages. `CLAUDE.md` says *"always use wiki-relative links"* (`[[concepts/RSI]]`), but 100 links used **bare names** — `[[LuxAlgo]]` ×13, `[[True-Strength-Index-TSI]]` ×10, `[[SuperTrend]]` ×9, `[[DGT]]` ×7 — which resolve to nothing. Plus 11 with a stray `.md` inside the brackets and 4 using a `wiki/` prefix. Repaired mechanically, **only where exactly one page matched the basename**; ambiguous or genuinely-absent targets were left alone, because guessing a target is worse than a dead link. Every resolution was printed and eyeballed before applying.
- **Side effect worth noting:** fixing bare-name links also cleared **2 orphans** (13 → 11). `entities/DGT` and `entities/Zeiierman` were never really orphaned — they were linked, just unresolvably.

  **[[analyses/Setup-Intelligence-Panel-2026-07-16]]** said *"volume profile is JS-only, no Python port, `stock_council.py` has no POC/VAH access"* — false since `volume_profile.py` shipped **2026-07-25, nine days after that page was written**, and HVN/LVN was wired 2026-08-09. **[[analyses/Market-Brief-Card]]** said *"FA-2 still needs an intraday store"* — the store was built and FA-2 has since run (null). Both annotated rather than silently rewritten, so the correction and its date travel with the claim.

  [[concepts/Fair-Value-Gap]] frontmatter said `sources: 7` while listing 6 — the discrepancy was real: **[[sources/FVG-FBG-Fibonacci-Retail-Framework]] is cited in the body but was missing from the Sources list.** Added as entry 7. [[concepts/Candlestick-Patterns]] said 3 while listing 5 (my own omission from this week's Bulkowski and infographic additions) — corrected to 5.

  > My automated counter over-counts: on [[concepts/Kelly-Criterion]] it read 4 where the page has 2 sources plus indented sub-references. Spot-checking three pages found the counter wrong on one, so a bulk edit would have introduced errors into metadata that is currently merely inconsistent. **Left for per-page review — a wrong count written confidently is worse than a known-inconsistent one.**
- ### Reported, not fixed — these need judgement, not a script
  **12 pages exist on disk but are absent from `index.md`** (incl. `concepts/Market-Sentiment-Index`, `concepts/SuperTrend`, `concepts/Stochastic-Oscillator`, `strategies/Minervini-SEPA`, `entities/LuxAlgo`) — the index is the master catalog, so these are effectively invisible. Each needs a real summary row, not a stub.
  **25 pages with frontmatter gaps**, clustered: 3 pages have **no `category` at all** (`entities/Kaushik-Akkiwakar`, `entities/MarkUp-Platform`, `sources/YT-Kaushik-Akkiwakar-TSI-Momentum` — all from one ingest, all also missing `updated`/`sources`/`related`); 3 entity pages lack the required `bias:`; 11 strategy/analysis pages lack the required `timeframe:`.
  **76 pages missing ≥1 schema-required section** — mostly `Conclusion` on analyses (roadmaps legitimately use *Action Taken / Plan* instead, so the schema may be what's wrong) and `Examples in This Wiki` on concepts. Needs a decision about whether the schema or the pages should move.
  **145 pages ≥30 days old, oldest 56d** — but the wiki is only ~56 days old, so this is mostly "written once, still true". Age alone is not staleness; the *claims* check below is the one that matters.

Two were false and are fixed; the rest were spot-checked as still true. **This class is the wiki's main decay risk** — it is what made both fixed claims wrong — and it is worth re-running this grep after every shipping session rather than at lint time.
