## [2026-08-09] measure | Hora tested — also null, not identified at 1h, and weekday beats it in every configuration
- The second pre-registered astro claim from the roadmap: *"do Mercury/Jupiter hora blocks differ from Saturn/Mars?"* Run as [hora_study.py](tools/hora_study.py) on the same 5,050 `^NSEI` 1h bars. Folded into [[analyses/FA-2-Rahu-Kaal-Measured]].
- ### Hora looked genuinely more promising than Rahu Kaal, and for a good reason
  Horas are **seasonal** — 12 equal divisions of sunrise→sunset — so their boundaries drift through the year. A fixed clock hour on a fixed weekday could therefore fall under **different lords in summer and winter**, which would let weekday *and* time-of-day be held fixed simultaneously. Rahu Kaal could not support that. So this was the one astro test with a possible clean identification.
- > [!warning] Measured, the hope dies: of 35 (weekday, clock) cells, only 5 hold more than one hora lord — and all five are the 15:15 stub
  > A ~10-minute boundary drift cannot reclassify a **60-minute** bar; it only bites at the session edge, on the very stub bar that manufactured the Rahu Kaal volatility artifact. So within a clock hour the 5 weekdays map **1:1** onto 5 lords, making **hora lord a deterministic function of (weekday, clock hour)** — no information beyond those 35 cells, so a hora effect cannot be told apart from a weekday×hour pattern. **Not a sample-size problem.**
- **I had to correct my own framing here.** I first wrote that lord and weekday were the *same partition* so the statistics would be identical. They are not: **7 lords against 5 weekdays**, because a weekday maps to a different lord at each hour, so pooled across hours a lord draws from several cells. The right question is therefore not "is hora significant" but **"which grouping explains more?"**
- **Result (stub excluded, 4,328 bars):** hora lord F=2.002, ANOVA p=0.062, perm p=0.042, range p=0.784 · **weekday F=3.725, ANOVA p=0.0050, perm p=0.0065**, range p=0.228. 8 tests run → Bonferroni **p<0.00625**. **Hora clears nothing; weekday sits right at the threshold.** Since hora is a deterministic function of weekday×hour and plain weekday accounts for *more*, the astrological relabelling is doing no work — and day-of-week effects in equity returns are long documented and need no astrology.
- **The per-lord ordering contradicts the tradition's own polarity:** Mars −1.48 bp · Venus −1.38 · Moon −0.27 · Sun +0.01 · Mercury +0.25 · Jupiter +1.18 · **Saturn +1.67 bp**. **Saturn, classically the most malefic hora, is the best**, and Venus — a benefic — is second worst. Insignificant, so not a counter-finding; but the *sign pattern* offers the tradition no support either.
- **What would fix identification: 15-minute bars.** At 15m a ~10-min seasonal drift *does* reclassify bars, breaking the collinearity. Third distinct reason now to accumulate fine intervals, after the 80% value-area rule and the 4H setup.
- **Both halves of FA-2 are now done and both are null.** Per the roadmap's own sequencing, **FA-3 (planet→sector map) should not be built** — the windows it would rest on have been tested and do nothing.

## [2026-08-09] measure | FA-2 run — Rahu Kaal shows nothing, and the one "significant" result is a 15-minute bar pretending to be an hour
- **The first astro claim in this repo measured rather than asserted.** [fa2_rahu_kaal_study.py](tools/fa2_rahu_kaal_study.py) on `^NSEI` 1h from the new store: **723 sessions, 5,050 bars, 2023-08-28 → 2026-08-07**, 827 inside Rahu Kaal. Full write-up: [[analyses/FA-2-Rahu-Kaal-Measured]].
- ### The confound dictated the design, and it would have wrecked a naive test
  Rahu Kaal sits at a **fixed 1/8 day-part per weekday** — Wednesday always midday, Monday always near the open. Intraday returns are strongly time-of-day structured (opening-hour median range **47bp** vs **23bp** at midday), so "inside RK vs all other bars" mostly measures **time of day** and reports it as Rahu Kaal. Escape: because the window's position is set by weekday, **the same clock hour is inside RK on one weekday and outside on another** — so stratify by clock hour, contrast within stratum, pool by inverse variance. Time of day then held exactly fixed. Both tests reported; the naive one only to show how far off it is.
- **Result — nothing clears the bar (4 tests → Bonferroni p<0.0125):** returns **+0.66 bp, p=0.39**; range **+1.41 bp, p=0.021**. Returns are not merely insignificant, they are **unstable in sign** across robustness configurations (+0.66, +0.23, +0.58, −0.53, −0.56 bp) — the signature of noise. Note the headline sign is *positive*: marginally better inside Rahu Kaal, opposite to the tradition, and equally meaningless.
- > [!key-insight] The one apparently-significant result is an artifact, and the robustness pass is now inside the script so nobody reads the headline alone
  > **NSE closes 15:30, so the "1h" bar opening at 15:15 is a 15-MINUTE STUB** — median range **11.9bp** against 22–47bp for real hours. The entire volatility signal lives in it: drop that single bar and **p=0.021 → p=0.5070**, and it stays dead in every further configuration (0.26, 0.53). Dropping Tuesday *alone* looks stronger (p=0.0025) — that is the artifact getting cleaner, not a finding emerging, since it removes Tuesday's 17 noisy in-window sessions at the same stub bar.
- > [!warning] A limit more data cannot fix: within a clock hour, Rahu-Kaal status IS weekday
  > Test B holds time of day fixed by contrasting weekdays — but within a stratum, "inside Rahu Kaal" is decided **entirely** by which weekday it is. **Any weekday-specific effect at that hour is indistinguishable from a Rahu Kaal effect.** The 15:15 stratum's in-window weekdays are Thu and Tue, so its "effect" is really *Thursday's last 15 minutes are more volatile than Mon/Wed/Fri's* — a day-of-week close effect in astrological clothing. **Structural, not statistical.** The one real identification strategy: Rahu Kaal's start **drifts ~50 min across the year** with sunrise while the weekday stays fixed, so early-window vs late-window sessions *within the same weekday* would vary RK while holding weekday constant — and that needs 15m bars to have any resolution, so it waits on accumulation.
- **The roadmap's ordering is vindicated and should now be honoured.** FA-2 was specified to precede **FA-3** (the planet→sector map) exactly so that mapping planets onto named stocks would not be built on untested windows. **FA-2 found nothing, so FA-3 should not be built.** Per FA-6, if a card is ever produced, this null belongs on its face.
- **Second measured claim this week to survive its headline and die on a control**, after the [[concepts/Fair-Value-Gap]] "magnet" (~2pp over a width-and-distance-matched placebo, gone at longer horizons). The pattern is consistent and worth stating plainly: **these claims do not fail because the data is thin — they fail because nobody had built the control.**

## [2026-08-09] ops | Daily intraday update scheduled — and the machine is not on IST, which decided the run time
- Registered Windows scheduled task **`TradingBramha-IntradayUpdate`**, daily **07:00 local**, running new wrapper `tools/intraday_update.bat` → `intraday_store.py update`, logging to `tools/intraday_update.log` (gitignored). **Verified end-to-end**: task `Ready`, manual trigger returned **Last Result 0**, log written, coverage unchanged (idempotent). This is what makes the perishable 15m/5m half accumulate — see [[analyses/Intraday-Store]].
- ### The timezone nearly produced a mid-session run
  **This machine runs US Eastern time, not IST.** A naive "16:30" schedule — the obvious choice for an hour after the 15:30 IST close — would have fired **during the trading session**. IST is +9h30 (EDT) / +10h30 (EST), so:
  | Machine regime | 07:00 local = | vs the 15:30 IST close |
  |---|---|---|
  | EDT (summer, UTC−4) | 16:30 IST | 1h after ✅ |
  | EST (winter, UTC−5) | 17:30 IST | 2h after ✅ |
  **And because India does not observe DST while the US does, the IST time of any fixed local run shifts by an hour twice a year.** 07:00 local was chosen because it lands post-close in *both* regimes, so the seasonal drift is harmless and no DST handling is needed. A time chosen without checking this would have silently broken every March and November.
- **Two deliberate properties of the wrapper.** It **hard-codes the interpreter path** — a bare `python` here resolves to `hermes-agent\venv` with no pandas and would fail every run ([[analyses/Python-Environments]], the same trap as yesterday's ephem bug). And **every run appends to a log**, because a scheduled task that quietly stops working is exactly the failure mode this repo keeps meeting (frozen NSE sector feeds, the 23-day-stale CSI 300). With a log, `coverage()` disagreeing with expectations is diagnosable rather than mysterious.
- > [!warning] Runs only while logged on — not fixable without storing a password
  > Created without `/RU`, so it runs as `Owner` in an interactive session; **if the machine is off or logged out at 07:00 that day is skipped.** No credential was supplied, since handling one is out of scope. **The mitigation is already in the design:** `update()` fetches a **7-day window**, so a missed day is recovered by the next run, and the overlap also self-heals partially-settled bars. **The real exposure is a gap beyond 7 days** — a holiday away from the machine — after which that stretch of 15m/5m is permanently unrecoverable, and beyond ~60 days the fine intervals are simply gone.

## [2026-08-09] fix | Morning Market Brief crashed on first real use — I verified it in an interpreter the app never runs
- **User report:** `Brief build failed: ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ephem'`. Root cause: **this machine has two Python 3.14.4 installs with different packages, and the app runs the one I did not test with.** Full page: [[analyses/Python-Environments]].
  | | `pythoncore-3.14-64` (runs the app) | `Programs\Python\Python314` (what I tested) |
  |---|---|---|
  | streamlit | ✅ 1.57.0 | ❌ broken (starlette ImportError) |
  | ephem | ❌ **missing** | ✅ 4.2.1 |
  | yfinance | 1.2.1 | 1.4.1 |
  A bare `python` resolves to a *third* (`hermes-agent\venv`) with no pandas at all. `streamlit` worked only in one and `ephem` only in the other, so no single interpreter could run both the app and the astro verification — exactly the configuration that invites testing in the wrong place.
- ### Two defects, and only one of them was the missing package
  **(1) Undeclared dependency.** `ephem` was pip-installed ad-hoc on 2026-08-07 and **B-14 was closed on that basis** — but it was never added to `tools/requirements_pipeline.txt`. So "ephem is installed" described a machine state, not the project; **any fresh environment would have failed identically.** Now installed into the dashboard's interpreter *and* declared, with a comment recording why.
  **(2) An optional dependency could erase the whole card.** `build_brief` hard-imported `panchang` at function top, so a missing `ephem` took down the **global quote strip and the sector breadth too** — neither of which touches astronomy. That is a design flaw independent of whether ephem is present: any future panchang problem would have done the same. Astro layer is now wrapped; the market half renders and the astro block shows a warning that names the fix. Note `astro_trading.py` **already modelled this correctly** with its `HAS_EPHEM` flag — the convention existed and the new consumer did not follow it.
- **Side finding, caught only because I tested the degradation rather than the happy path:** `overall_tone()` returned **UNKNOWN whenever breadth was absent** — including when the user simply unticks the "Include sector breadth" checkbox — while holding a perfectly usable Nifty change. It now degrades to a price-only read, with the caveat documented that price-only STRONG is the more generous call since breadth cannot veto it. **11/11 checks**, including a simulated missing `ephem` proving quotes, tone and disclaimer all survive.
- **The working rule now recorded:** verify anything dashboard-facing with the interpreter from `.claude/launch.json` (`pythoncore-3.14-64`), and **declare any new third-party import in the same change** — an ad-hoc `pip install` records machine state, not project state, which is precisely the mistake B-14 made.
- **Open:** reconcile or retire one of the two 3.14.4 installs — two environments each holding something the other lacks is the actual root cause and will keep generating this class of bug. Also consider pinning `yfinance`: the 1.2.1-vs-1.4.1 skew is harmless today and won't stay that way, and [[analyses/Intraday-Store]]'s measured `period` ceilings were measured on the *other* version.

## [2026-08-09] build | Intraday store — and it immediately proved Rahu Kaal barely overlaps NSE hours
- Built [intraday_store.py](tools/intraday_store.py) — the blocker recorded three times in three days (FA-2, the [[concepts/Volume-Profile]] 80% rule, the [[strategies/4H-Wick-Zone-FVG-Setup]]). SQLite in the house pattern (`tools/*.db`, gitignored like `reversal_calendar.db`). Backfill / update / coverage / `session_slice`, **21/21 behavioural checks** (idempotency, timezone round-trip, naive-input handling, failure capture, MultiIndex flattening). Full page: [[analyses/Intraday-Store]].
- ### The finding that justifies building it *before* it was needed: intraday data is perishable
  Yahoo serves intraday history as a **rolling window**, measured against RELIANCE.NS: **1m ~8 days · 5m/15m/30m ~81 days · 1h ~1,075 days (~3 years)**. And **over-asking returns an EMPTY frame with an error, not a truncated one** — so an optimistic "just request 5y" loses everything, which is why `MAX_PERIOD` is a measured lookup table. **Every day this does not run is a day of 15m data that can never be recovered**, unlike daily bars which Yahoo will still hand over in five years. That asymmetry, not any single study, is the argument for a schedule.
- **Backfilled:** 6 symbols × 3 intervals = **65,056 bars, 11.7 MB**. `^NSEI` 1h = **5,057 bars over 724 sessions, 2023-08-28 → 2026-08-07**. 15m/5m sit at 58 sessions — the ceiling, and the number that only grows by running.
- ### Then the join to `panchang.day_parts()` produced a constraint nobody had noticed
  Of 724 stored `^NSEI` sessions only **592** contain any 1h bar inside Rahu Kaal, and the weekday split is lopsided: **Mon 142 · Tue 17 · Wed 144 · Thu 145 · Fri 143**. The cause is structural, not missing data — Rahu Kaal is a fixed 1/8 day-part per weekday and the NSE session is 09:15–15:30:
  | Weekday | RK part | Window range | Overlaps session |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Monday | 2nd | 07:39–10:02 | 52/52 but **starts pre-open** |
  | **Tuesday** | 7th | 15:11–17:41 | **14/52 — post-close** |
  | Wednesday | 5th | 12:21–14:23 | 53/53 **fully inside** |
  | Thursday | 6th | 13:47–16:01 | 52/52, tail **past close** |
  | Friday | 4th | 10:55–12:52 | 52/52 **fully inside** |
- **So FA-2 is cleanly askable on Wednesday and Friday only** (~144 sessions each), and **Tuesday's Rahu Kaal is untestable on Indian equities** — it is a post-market window. **Pooling all five weekdays would dilute any real effect toward zero for a purely mechanical reason and read as a null** — precisely the kind of false negative that would then have been recorded as "measured, no edge". Caught before a study was designed on it, which is the whole value of building the plumbing first.
- **What is still blocked, now precisely rather than vaguely:** the **80% value-area rule** is specified on two consecutive **30-minute** bars and 30m depth is 58 sessions (1h is too coarse for a 30-minute hold condition); the **4H setup**'s 15m trigger leg likewise. Both become testable **through accumulation rather than more work** — if the update runs. Its Weekly→Daily translation needs none of this and is testable today.
- **Open / deliberately not done:** a **daily scheduled `update`** is what makes the perishable half accumulate, and standing configuration is the user's call — not created here. Without it the fine intervals stop growing and those two studies stay blocked indefinitely. Also worth naming: `*.db` is gitignored, so accumulated 15m/5m history has **no versioned backup** — it survives only because the repo happens to sit in a OneDrive-synced path, which is real protection nobody chose deliberately.
- **Next:** FA-2 proper — Wed/Fri, 1h bars inside vs outside Rahu Kaal, **against a matched-window baseline with a significance test**, following the [fvg_fill_study](tools/fvg_fill_study.py) template of testing a claim against a control rather than against nothing.

## [2026-08-09] fix | The "doji star is stronger" line corrected — a readability claim had been promoted to a performance claim
- Acted on the contradiction logged earlier today. From **2026-06-19 to 2026-08-09** the Three-Bar table on [[concepts/Candlestick-Patterns]] read simply **"Doji Star — stronger than regular star"**. Bulkowski's per-pattern figures invert that on both measures: **71% vs 72%** reversal (a wash) and **30th vs 4th** overall performance (not a wash), plus rarer (freq 81st vs 71st). Row now reads *"clearer indecision, not better performance"* with the numbers inline.
- **The old reasoning was not wrong — it was answering a different question.** Litchfield/Morris argued a doji makes the indecision unambiguous, which is true and worth keeping. What is not supported is the leap from *easier to read* to *performs better*: the doji filter cuts to a rarer subset whose average move is measured on fewer, noisier cases. **The bare word "stronger" collapsed those two claims into one** — the same slippage as FVG "magnet" language before it was measured, and as "simple & powerful" on the 4H card.
- **Left alone deliberately:** the Two-Bar row *"Harami Cross — stronger indecision signal"*. That one is scoped to *indecision*, i.e. signal clarity, which is exactly the defensible half. Fixed what was wrong, not everything adjacent.
- The earlier contradiction callout quoted the line it was about, so it would have gone stale on the fix; rewritten as a dated correction note that records what the page used to say and why. **Log entries above are left as authored** — the log is history, not a page to retro-edit.

## [2026-08-09] research | Bulkowski primary source checked — the gap requirement is confirmed, and the 72% belongs to the other pattern
- **Closes the open item logged 2026-08-08.** The provenance warning on [[concepts/Candlestick-Patterns]] said we could not tell whether Bulkowski's 72% Evening Star was measured on the **gapped** or **gapless** definition, so the figure could not be attached to a detector. Went to the author's own pattern pages ([Evening Star](https://thepatternsite.com/EveningStar.html), [Evening Doji Star](https://thepatternsite.com/EveningDojiStar.html)). Three findings, and only the first was the question asked.
- ### 1. The gap IS required — body-to-body, which is exactly what we shipped
  Verbatim: *"a small bodied candle of any color should **gap above the bodies of the two adjacent candles** (ignore the shadows)."* The doji page mirrors it. So the **sourced strict reading is Bulkowski's reading**, and specifically the **body-vs-body** test — *not* `pmax.pine`'s stricter wick-clear (`low[1] > high[2]`). `scan_bearish_reversal_candle`'s `require_gap=True` default **was a judgement call yesterday and is a citation today**. The retail infographic that omits the gap describes a looser, different pattern.
- ### 2. Evening Star and Evening Doji Star are separate patterns, and the doji variant is the WORSE one
  | | Bearish reversal | Reversal rank | Overall perf rank | Frequency rank |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **Evening Star** | 72% | 10th | **4th of 103** | 71st |
  | **Evening Doji Star** | 71% | 12th | **30th of 103** | 81st |

  **The 72% we have been quoting belongs to Evening Star**, not to the doji variant the infographic and our deep-dive are about. The doji version is 71% and — far more starkly — **30th vs 4th on overall performance**. Table row corrected.
- > [!contradiction] Our own page says the doji star is *stronger*; his data says the opposite
  > The Three-Bar table has recorded *"Doji Star — stronger than regular star"* since 2026-06-19, from the Litchfield/Morris side, reasoning that a doji makes the indecision unambiguous. Bulkowski inverts it on both metrics. Both can be internally consistent — "cleaner signal" and "better subsequent move" are different claims, and the doji filter cuts to a rarer subset measured on noisier cases — but **the plain word "stronger" is unsupported by the only per-pattern performance data this wiki holds.**
- ### 3. Our rarity measurement is independently corroborated
  We measured Evening Doji Star firing **twice in 11,628 bar-observations** (strict rule, 17 NSE large-caps, 3y). Bulkowski ranks it **81st of 103 on frequency**. Two unrelated methods, same conclusion: too rare to build a routine around. Pleasant to have a measurement agree with a source for once rather than contradict it.
- **Deliberately NOT acted on:** the scanner scores Evening Doji Star (68) above Evening Star (62). That ordering is explicitly *geometric strictness, not accuracy*, so it is not wrong by its own stated basis — but an accuracy ordering would invert them. **Not re-based**, because the numbers that would justify it rest on cells of **28 and 199 patterns**, and re-tuning a detector on that is the over-fitting-to-a-third-party-statistic this page keeps warning against. Recorded in the docstring so the next person meets the tension rather than rediscovering it.
- **Still unresolved:** the free pages publish ranks and selected cells but **no total sample or date range** for the headline rates. The Encyclopedia itself remains un-ingested. So the ranks are directional and the cell-level averages are anecdote — which is a better position than yesterday's *"cannot be attached to anything"*, and still not a foundation.

## [2026-08-09] ingest | Demand & Supply four formations — the content was already here, so the audit it prompted is the finding
- **Source:** unattributed retail "Demand & Supply" 2×2 infographic — RBR / RBD / DBR / DBD with the zone drawn at the base. Filed as [[sources/Demand-Supply-Four-Formations-Infographic]]. **[[strategies/Supply-Demand-Zone-Trading]] has carried all four since 2026-06-18**, with the same continuation/reversal split and the same zone-at-the-base construction, so this is **pure corroboration and contributes no mechanics.** Kept anyway on the precedent of [[sources/Fingrad-OI-Analysis-Infographic]] — independent corroboration of a framework is worth recording. `overview.md` untouched.
- **What our page has that the graphic does not**, for the record: zone-strength ranking (freshness → exit-move speed → time at base → excursion distance), **flip zones**, the HTF-mark→TTF-entry split, entry triggers (pin bar / engulfing / two-bar reversal inside the zone), stops with a 1–2 ATR buffer and the no-round-numbers rule, partials at 1:1, the Smart-Money accumulation→distribution phase table, and the DBR-flip-zone mapping of the sweep-reverse scalp. The graphic has four pictures and no rules.
- ### The finding is the coverage audit, not the source
  Re-reading the four formations against `tools/` produced a map **nobody had drawn in the eight weeks since the page was written**: | RBR ⚠️ partial · DBD ❌ · RBD ❌ · DBR ❌ |
- > [!contradiction] The one formation we detect, we trade backwards
  > S&D says the base **is the zone** — price leaves, returns, and the trade is the **retest**. [`scan_base_formation`](tools/scanner_templates.py:863) finds the RBR shape but is **Minervini-framed**: full Stage-2 trend template (price > SMA50 > SMA150 > SMA200, within 25% of the 52-week high, ≥25% above the 52-week low), `detect_vcp_geometry` for a contracting base, and it buys the **breakout above the pivot** while *explicitly excluding* names that already broke out. The base is a launchpad there, not a zone to return to. **Same shape, opposite trade, different bar** — so a Base Formation hit is **not** an RBR demand-zone setup, and wiring one to the other's output would buy the wrong event. Same class as wick-zone-vs-order-block and the Evening Star's gap: **a shape match is not a method match.**
- **Two of the three missing formations are supply-side — the fourth long-only gap this week**, after candlesticks (no bearish patterns until 2026-08-09), `scan_order_block` (long-only) and `scan_fvg_pullback` (long-only). At this point the bias is a repo-wide property, not four coincidences: **the scanner suite is structurally long.** Worth deciding whether that is intentional (it is a long-only book) or accidental (nobody wrote the mirrors), because the answer changes whether the gaps are bugs.
- **DBR is the most interesting absence.** Early Reversal Detector, [[concepts/Rounding-Bottom]], [[concepts/Falling-Wedge]] and the Drop-Recovery screen all have the DBR *shape* (drop → base → rally) but **none is framed as a base with a tradeable zone**, so none emits the zone a retest is measured against. Converting one is mostly marking the base and its retest — not new geometry.
- **Keepable mnemonic** from the graphic, the one thing it says better than us: *the middle leg names the zone, the two outer legs name the trade.* Same-direction outer legs → continuation; opposite → reversal.

## [2026-08-09] ingest | The 4H Setup — filed for reuse, and every primitive in it was already here
- **Source:** unattributed retail infographic, "THE 4H SETUP — A Simple & Powerful Intraday Strategy". Ingested on explicit instruction — *"I am not interested in intraday, however worth keeping it for reuse."* Filed accordingly: [[sources/4H-Wick-Zone-FVG-Infographic]] + [[strategies/4H-Wick-Zone-FVG-Setup]], the strategy marked **PARKED — reference only**, `overview.md` deliberately untouched since it shifts no thesis.
- **The strategy in one line:** HTF **wick zone** (a 4H candle's body→wick span) → LTF **liquidity sweep** (15m close past the 4H body) → LTF **BOS** → enter on the **FVG retest**. Mirrored for bearish. Each leg removes a different failure mode — zone gives location, sweep proves liquidity was taken, BOS proves the reversal is structural rather than a bounce, FVG gives a definable stop — **so the claimed power has to come from the sequencing, because no individual leg has an independent measured edge in this repo.**
- **Every primitive was already implemented.** `detect_bos_choch`, `detect_fvg_pullback`/`scan_fvg_pullback`, and the HTF-mark→TTF-entry discipline on [[strategies/Supply-Demand-Zone-Trading]] and [[concepts/Order-Blocks]]. **The contribution is ordering and a name, not a detector.**
- ### Contradiction 1: "wick zone" is a fourth zone definition, and the loosest of the four
  The wiki already carries three mechanically distinct HTF zones — an **order block** (needs a structure-breaking displacement), an **S&D base** (needs consolidation), an **FVG** (needs a 3-candle non-overlap). The wick zone needs **none of them**: any large candle's body-to-wick span qualifies. Not automatically wrong, but it means the zone contributes little selectivity and the sweep+BOS+FVG legs do the actual filtering. **Recorded explicitly so "wick zone" never gets used as a synonym for order block** — the graphic's own diagram shows a zone a BOS-based OB detector would not mark. Same definitional discipline as the order-block merge and the Evening Doji Star.
- ### Contradiction 2: its entry is the one our own page argues against
  Step 4's "enter at the FVG retest" reads as a resting limit *inside* the gap — which fills on the way through in exactly the cases where the gap fails, so the fill is guaranteed when least wanted. The strategy page **deviates from the source deliberately** and requires our rule: a close back above the gap with a dominant rejection wick. Separately, **yesterday's fill study is not a refutation of this use** — here the FVG is an entry *location after a confirmed BOS*, not a prediction that price returns — but it does mean the FVG leg carries **no independent edge**.
- **A real omission, not a simplification:** the graphic has **no HTF trend filter anywhere.** It collapses this wiki's three-rung template ([[concepts/Multi-Timeframe-Analysis]]: weekly regime → daily setup → intraday trigger) to two rungs and drops the regime layer, so the sequence fires against a deteriorating higher timeframe just as readily.
- **Closest sibling already on file:** [[strategies/False-Break-Buildup-Trading]] — same HTF-zone-plus-LTF-trigger shape, but the zone is a consolidation rather than one candle's wick, and the trigger **fades** the false break instead of **joining** the reversal after a BOS. Two spellings of one idea; neither measured.
- ### The keepable part, since intraday is not the interest: shift it up two rungs
  **Weekly zone → Daily sweep → Daily BOS → Daily FVG retest** needs **no intraday store** and is testable against [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] exactly as the FVG fill study was. **Two of the four legs already exist** (`detect_bos_choch`, `scan_fvg_pullback`); the other two are a few lines of candle geometry. That translation is the actual reason this page is worth keeping, and it is written into the strategy page as a table.
- > [!warning] Third claim in a row this repo cannot test, all blocked on one missing thing
  > As specified the strategy is intraday, and `chart_server` fetches intraday bars while **nothing persists them**. That now blocks, in order: **FA-2** (Rahu Kaal / hora windows), the **[[concepts/Volume-Profile]] 80% value-area rule**, and this. **One intraday store unblocks all three** — on current evidence it is the highest-leverage unbuilt piece of infrastructure in the repo, and the pattern is only visible because three separate ingests hit it in three days.

## [2026-08-09] measure | FVG "price magnet" tested against a matched control — the fill statistic is real, the gap's share of it is ~2pp
- Two things shipped: **`scan_fvg_pullback`** (the detector made screenable) and **[fvg_fill_study.py](tools/fvg_fill_study.py)** (the claim measured). Prompted by an "How to Identify a Fair Value Gap" infographic whose Step 3 is *"the market seeks balance and will eventually pull back to fill this gap — place your entry orders inside the zone."* Both halves of that sentence turned out to be worth testing.
- ### The measurement, and why the control is the entire point
  **2,014 size-gated gaps**, 20 NSE large-caps, 5y daily. A bare *"89% of gaps get filled"* is not evidence: whether price returns to a band is driven mostly by **how wide the band is and how far it sits from price**, neither of which has anything to do with imbalance. So every real gap is paired with a **matched placebo** — identical width, identical distance from the reference price, anchored at a random unrelated bar. The question becomes *"do gaps fill more, or sooner, than an equivalent stretch of ordinary price?"*
- **Result (horizon 40 bars):** Any Touch **89.2% vs 87.0%** placebo (+2.1pp, p=0.036) · Midpoint 79.8% vs 78.0% (p=0.14) · Wick Sweep 71.7% vs 69.6% (p=0.13) · Body Beyond 65.8% vs 63.3% (p=0.087). **The gap contributes about two percentage points.**
- **Three robustness checks, each weakening it further.** (1) **Extending the horizon to 120 bars — which is what "eventually" asks for — collapses the deltas to +0.3…+1.4pp, every p ≥ 0.32.** The claim is weakest on exactly the timescale it invokes, because given long enough almost everything gets touched. (2) **No speed effect anywhere** — median bars-to-fill identical in every cell, and for Wick Sweep the **placebo is faster** (6 vs 7). "Magnet" implies pull; nothing pulls. (3) **Wider gaps are not more magnetic** — stratified by width (n≈665 per class) no class is significant (p 0.20–0.50), and wide gaps fill *less* often in absolute terms (56.9% vs 72.6% narrow) which is pure geometry. The lone p=0.036 is the loosest definition, fails Bonferroni (4 tests → 0.0125), and does not replicate at 120 bars (p=0.65). Noise.
- **What it does NOT say, stated on the page so it cannot be over-read:** this tests the *mechanism* claim, not the trade. A zone can still be a useful place to look for a rejection even if its fill rate is unremarkable — that is a forward-return question for [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] and has not been run. Keeping the two apart matters in both directions.
- ### The second half of the infographic's sentence is worse than the first
  *"Place your entry orders inside the zone"* means a resting limit order — which **fills you on the way through in exactly the cases where the gap fails**. The fill is guaranteed precisely when it is unwanted. Our port instead requires price to **close back above** the zone with a dominant lower wick: fewer trades, worse average price, only gaps that held. That difference was already in the code; it is now written down as the reason.
- **Orphaned detector, fourth instance.** `detect_fvg_pullback` existed only as one boolean inside the Early Reversal validation layer (`checks["FVG"]`) — a gap setup could be *confirmed* on a name you were already looking at but never *found*. Now registered as **"FVG Pullback"** (Entry, Entry Trigger family), firing on 8/12 large-caps over 2y with entry/stop/target. Emitting a stop required `detect_fvg_pullback` to stop discarding the zone when the signal consumes it — additive `buy_signal_zone`/`sell_signal_zone` keys, existing consumer reads only `*_bars_ago`, verified unaffected. 10/10 invariant checks (registry, category, family, in neither measured list).
- **Note on where this leaves the FVG page:** it now carries a *measured* null on its central mechanism alongside 5 ingested sources and 4 compared implementations — the best-sourced concept in the wiki and the first one whose headline claim has been tested against a control rather than a baseline of nothing. **Unlike FA-2 this needed no intraday store**, which is why it was cheap enough to actually do.

## [2026-08-09] build | Morning Market Brief shipped — FA-1 verified to the minute, and the staleness check caught a dead index on its first run
- **Ask:** a published "Morning Market Brief" infographic reproduced on the Market Sentiment page, *including the finest astro detail of the day*. Delivered as [panchang.py](tools/panchang.py) (the astro engine, roadmap **FA-1**), [market_brief.py](tools/market_brief.py) (quotes + breadth + composition) and a section on the **Market Sentiment** page. New pages: [[concepts/Panchang]], [[analyses/Market-Brief-Card]].
- ### FA-1: the almanac layer, offline and verified against the published card
  Rahu Kaal · Yamaganda · Gulika · the 24-block hora sequence · nakshatra + pada with transit times · KP sub-lord · tithi + paksha · retrogrades. **No API, no login, no rate limit, no vendor** — the property the FA roadmap established was available. Verified for **Tue 2026-07-28 Mumbai: Rahu Kaal 16:00–17:38** (card: 04:00–05:38 PM) and **Purva Ashadha pada 3 at 05:30 rolling to pada 4 at 06:31** (same card). Both exact. Those references live in `verify()` inside the module — run `python tools/panchang.py`.
- **Two bugs the published check caught, and both returned *plausible wrong answers* rather than raising** — which is exactly why a published reference was used instead of eyeballing: (1) **`ephem.Ecliptic` defaults to J2000 while an ayanamsa is defined against the ecliptic of date**, leaving 26 years of precession (~0.36° ≈ **40 minutes of lunar motion**) in the longitude and putting the pada transition at 07:15 instead of 06:31. **Tithi passed throughout while nakshatra was wrong, which identified the cause** — tithi is a *difference* of two longitudes, so precession cancels. (2) Hora blocks built by repeated `start + length` accumulated microsecond drift and left gaps at the day/night seam; boundaries are now computed once and paired, so contiguity holds by construction.
- **Moon phase deliberately not computed here** — `astro_trading` owns it and `panchang()` delegates, because two surfaces disagreeing about the moon invalidates anything built on either. That delegation is **asserted, not assumed**: an over-broad `except Exception` initially swallowed a wrong class name (`AstroTrading` vs `AstroIndicators`) and made a plain bug look like an empty data field. Narrowed to DB/coverage errors, import made hard.
- > [!warning] The roadmap's FA-0 "non-negotiable blocker" was already fixed and nobody had noticed
  > [[analyses/Financial-Astrology-Sector-Week-Roadmap]] lists FA-0 (`get_lunar_phase` broken three ways, live on a shipped page) as the prerequisite for everything. **It was fixed 2026-08-02 — five days before that roadmap was written.** `_lunar_phase_approximate` is gone; both paths route through `_lunar_phase_from_calendar`, and it returns `new / 14.41%` for 2026-08-09 and `full / 99.95%` for the 2026-07-29 full moon. **FA-1 was never blocked.** That roadmap is still uncommitted and should carry the correction when it lands.
- ### Departure 1 — breadth from constituents, because the sector indices are dead
  The cheap version (count red sector indices) would be **fiction**: Yahoo froze **8 of 11** NSE sector feeds on 2026-07-17 and they return stale bars rather than erroring — which already fed a fake **25/25** into the composite on 08-03. So breadth comes from **Nifty 500 constituents** grouped by NSE's own macro-industry tag (20 of them, exchange CSV via `nse_universe`, cached 7 days, no per-symbol `.info` calls). **Median not mean** per sector, so one collapsing name cannot paint a sector; **thin sectors (<5 scored) reported, not counted**. Live: **500/500 constituents scored in ~6s, zero stale, zero missing → 12/19 sectors red.** The expensive path turned out to be the reliable one.
- ### The staleness check paid for itself on run one — same 2026-07-17 date
  Every quote is age-checked. First live run flagged **China CSI 300 (`000300.SS`) at 23 days stale**, last bar **2026-07-17**, while every live China ticker printed 08-07. **Without the check the card would have shown −3.60% as today's move.** Two unrelated index families frozen on one date reads as a **single batched Yahoo index-feed drop, not flakiness** — so the working assumption is now that *any* `^`-style index ticker can go quietly stale and only an age check will say so. Row switched to `510300.SS` (CSI 300 ETF), labelled `*` to mark it a proxy rather than the index. AAXJ carries the same asterisk for MSCI AP ex-Japan.
- ### Departure 2 — the astro layer makes no call, and that absence is the feature
  The source card pairs readings with directional language; the equivalent here would be *"avoid fresh buying during Rahu Kaal"*. **That line is not printed.** Rahu Kaal's inauspiciousness is a traditional assertion and **FA-2 has never run**, so the panchang block prints windows, lords, padas and transit times with an explicit note that no window carries a measured market effect. **`overall_tone()` takes no astro input at all** — WEAK/CAUTIOUS/STRONG comes only from Nifty's change and breadth. The roadmap's own line: holding the context-not-signal position "is not caution in the caption, it is a measurement."
- **Open:** FA-2 needs an **intraday store** — `chart_server` fetches intraday bars, nothing persists them. That is the same constraint blocking [[concepts/Volume-Profile]]'s 80% rule, so one store unblocks both. Also: a PNG export would need the disclaimer rendered **into** the image, since a brief card travels without context by design.

## [2026-08-09] build | HVN/LVN wired into Setup Intelligence — the pockets were drawn on the chart all along, only the read-back was missing
- Prompted by a user-supplied "Market Profile & Volume Distribution" infographic. **The audit's verdict: we were already ahead of it.** VAH/POC/VAL, the ~68–70% value area, HVN-as-magnet and LVN-as-fast-traverse were all documented on [[concepts/Volume-Profile]]; our implementation goes further with **delta** (signed volume per row — *who* built each node), **MTF profile stacking**, proportional-overlap allocation and volume-greedy VA expansion. The card's "price rotates in value, breaks beyond VAH/VAL" is our **80% rule**, already recorded *with* the note that it is an unverified vendor claim and measurable. The one substantive difference: the card says **~68%** value area, we use **70%** — the 1-SD framing vs the Market Profile convention. Ours is right; documented rather than changed.
- ### Two real gaps, both plumbing rather than knowledge
  **(1) `describe_position()` computed HVN/LVN counts but [stock_council.py](tools/stock_council.py:302) copied only the POC/VAH/VAL fields into the evidence.** So the panel could say *"above value"* but not *"sitting on an HVN"* or *"air pocket overhead"* — which is the actionable half of the whole concept. The pockets were computed by `volume_profile.py` and drawn on the chart from the start; nothing was missing except the read-back. Fixed: `describe_position()` now also returns `at_hvn`/`at_lvn` plus distance to the nearest pocket **above** (to its low) and **below** (to its high), via a new `_nearest_nodes()` helper, and the council surfaces **five factors** — HVN shelf ≤4% below = bullish stop reference · HVN ≤4% overhead = first real ceiling · LVN ≤4% overhead *with no HVN in front of it* = thin air · inside an HVN = magnet/chop warning · inside an LVN = no support underfoot, stops unreliable, fast both ways.
  **(2) The page had never mentioned `tools/volume_profile.py` at all** — it credited HVN/LVN to `volume_profile.js` (chart-side) and described the Python side as POC/VAH/VAL + delta only. But that module shipped **2026-07-25** with the full expanding-pocket HVN/LVN implementation and was already wired into the council. The page understated our own capability for two weeks, **including through its own 2026-08-02 update**. Now carries an implementation table (JS / PY / `compute_volume_profile_levels`) with a warning callout naming the omission.
- **Worth recording about the node maths**, since it is the part retail material flattens: a node **seeds** at a local-extremum bin clearing `mean_vol × 1.5` (HVN) or `× 0.5` (LVN), then **expands outward** while neighbours stay within tolerance, and overlapping pockets merge — so a node is a contiguous price *pocket*, not the "thick bar / thin bar" of the infographic.
- **Verified live (1y daily):** ITC inside an HVN with another 5.0% overhead · TITAN above value but **inside an LVN** with its nearest HVN 13.6% below (the no-shelf-underneath case) · TCS with an HVN 0.7% beneath · **SBIN firing the shelf-below and air-above branches together** (HVN 3.7% below, LVN 1.7% above with nothing in front) — the exact combination the ordering guard was written for. Plus 9/9 geometry checks on `_nearest_nodes` including edge-touch and empty-zone cases. `describe_position` has a single consumer, so extending its return was safe.
- **Standing caveat, unchanged:** these are structural-context reads, **not signals, and unmeasured** — same status as the value-area factors and the 80% rule. **Open:** harness the value-area and node reads; the 80% rule remains the cleanest testable claim on the page and still has not been run.

## [2026-08-09] build | Bearish candle risk flag shipped — and the pattern that prompted it fires twice in three years
- **`scan_bearish_reversal_candle`** in [scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py:3043), registered **"Bearish Reversal Candle"** (category **Risk**) and added to `RISK_SCANNERS` ([opportunity_hub.py](tools/opportunity_hub.py:228)). Detects **Evening Doji Star · Evening Star · Three Black Crows · Dark Cloud Cover · Bearish Engulfing · Shooting Star**, reporting the strictest that fires. Closes the asymmetry logged yesterday: the repo had four bullish patterns and **zero bearish ones**, while by our own top-10 the best-rated patterns are bearish.
- **Deliberately not a short signal.** `RISK_SCANNERS` already treats a bearish hit as evidence *against* a shortlisted long, barred from adding confluence — so this lands as a risk flag with **no change to the confluence maths**. Invariants verified: in the registry, category Risk, in `RISK_SCANNERS`, in **no** family, and in **neither** measured-edge nor measured-weak list (it is unmeasured, and says so in its own `reasons`).
- **Three properties, each tracing to a decision recorded yesterday:** `require_gap` is a **parameter** (default = the sourced Morris reading) rather than a hard-coded rule; **trend context is evaluated at the bar the pattern started forming**, not the current bar (the Forthius anti-lookback rule); and the **resistance location gate is mandatory**. Base scores are ordered by **geometric strictness, deliberately not by the Bulkowski percentages** — those carry no definitions and cannot be attached to an implementation, per yesterday's provenance correction.
- **A real bug the tests caught, worth recording because it would have been invisible in production:** the first location gate scanned the full 60-bar window for a swing-high pivot — but **a star is itself a local high**, so every pattern trivially qualified as "near a pivot" and the gate rejected *nothing*. It would have looked like a working filter while filtering nothing. Pivot search now excludes the pattern's own bars. 10/10 behavioural checks pass (fires/rejects on gap, location, trend-at-formation, volume, history).
- ### The measured base rate answers the gap question, and deflates the pattern that started this
  17 NSE large-caps, 3y daily, **11,628 bar-observations**. Strict **303 hits (2.61% of bars)**, loose **320 (2.75%)**. **The gap switch changes the total by 5.6% — and quadruples the stars**: Evening Star 5→23, Evening Doji Star 2→8. The aggregate hides it because Shooting Star (158) and Bearish Engulfing (99) dominate either way.
- **The deflating number: Evening Doji Star fired 2 times in 11,628 bar-observations under the sourced rule** — roughly one per 8.5 stock-years. The infographic presents it as a workhorse; on this universe it is a curiosity, and the flag earns its keep entirely through the commoner shapes. Worth knowing *before* building a watch routine around it.
- **Explicitly frequency, not edge.** Nothing here says a hit predicts anything; that needs [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] and has not been run. **Open:** harness the flag; and `is_morning_star()` still uses the loose no-gap/no-doji reading, now inconsistent with the bearish mirror's strict default — reconciling it changes a shipped detector's hit rate, so measure rather than flip.

## [2026-08-08] lint | Evening Doji Star pinned down — three implementations, three definitions, and a statistic that can't be transferred
- Prompted by a user-supplied "Evening Doji Star" infographic. **The wiki already had the pattern** — [[concepts/Candlestick-Patterns]] line 86 (Evening Star: large white → small body **gap higher** → large black >50% into white) and line 87 (Doji Star variant, "stronger than regular star") — plus the trade recipe in [[strategies/Candlestick-Filtering]] ("price at supply zone + RSI 55–65 + Shooting Star / Dark Cloud / **Evening Star** → short"). The infographic's "sniper entry at a Resistance/Supply zone" **is** this wiki's Litchfield thesis: candlesticks are the timing trigger inside a pre-identified structural setup, never the setup. So nothing conceptual was missing; what was missing was precision about *which* geometry we mean.
- ### The pattern has three implementations in this repo and no two agree
  **Sourced (Morris):** the star must **gap** away — "3-bar; middle bar gaps away (small body/doji), third bar confirms". **Retail infographic:** no gap, just "a tiny Doji at the top" — materially looser, many more and weaker signals. **[`pmax.pine:282`](tools/pmax.pine:282):** requires the gap ✓ but also requires the star to be **green**, applies **no small-body test at all** (a large candle can be the "star"), and demands `close < open[2]` — stricter than the 50%-midpoint rule. **[`is_morning_star()`](tools/scanner_templates.py:2988)**, the bullish mirror and the only star detector we ship, checks **no gap** and doesn't require a doji. Recorded as a contradiction callout with the resolution that gap-vs-no-gap belongs as a *parameter*, same as the [[concepts/Fair-Value-Gap]] fill-mode spectrum.
- ### Provenance correction: the Bulkowski numbers are weaker than the page claimed
  The page headed them "Encyclopedia of Candlestick Charts". **They are not from the book** — the actual source is a screenshot of a FINGRAD infographic *crediting* Bulkowski ([[sources/Fingrad-Trading-Infographics-Collection]], image 16); the Encyclopedia has never been ingested. No definitions, sample size, universe, window, or meaning of "resolves" accompanies the percentages. **Consequence that matters:** we cannot tell whether the **72% Evening Star** was measured on the gapped or gapless definition, so **that figure cannot be used to justify a detector** — quoting it against a loose implementation would be measuring one pattern and citing another. Logged as an open item: ingest a primary source before these numbers weight anything.
- **The code asymmetry, now written down:** [`scan_candlestick`](tools/scanner_templates.py:2948) is **bullish-only** (Morning Star · Bullish Engulfing · Piercing · Hammer). **No bearish candle detection exists anywhere in `tools/`.** And by our own top-10, the best-rated patterns are the bearish ones (Three Black Crows 78%, Evening Star 72%) while **not one of the four implemented patterns appears in that top-10 at all** — the selection is inverted relative to our own recorded evidence.
- **Design decision for the port (b):** the bearish patterns belong as a **risk flag**, not a short signal. `RISK_SCANNERS` ([opportunity_hub.py:228](tools/opportunity_hub.py:228)) already treats a bearish hit as evidence *against* a shortlisted long, barred from adding confluence — an Evening Doji Star at resistance on a name about to be bought is exactly that shape, and it needs no change to the confluence maths.
- **Also fixed:** page had **no "Examples in This Wiki" section** despite the schema requiring one (now added, covering the Python/Pine/port-target reality), and frontmatter claimed `sources: 1` while citing three. Port target recorded: [[sources/Forthius-Reversal-Scanner-Pine]] — ~15 patterns with per-pattern *quality scores*, trend context evaluated as of when the pattern **started forming** (`downCtx[N]`, avoiding lookback bias), and one-geometry-two-labels gating.

## [2026-08-08] merge | Order-block pages unified — and the two definitions disagreed on which candle is the block
- **Two pages existed for one concept:** `concepts/Order-Block.md` (2026-07-25, **0 sources**, greenfield — written to describe the shipped scanner) and [[concepts/Order-Blocks]] (2026-07-16, sourced from [[sources/LuxAlgo-Smart-Money-Concepts-Pine]]). Both were indexed separately, so nothing ever forced them into the same view. Merged into the **plural** page: it holds the sourced mechanics, had ~10 inbound links vs ~4, and plural is well-precedented in `concepts/` (Equal-Highs-Lows, Premium-Discount-Zones). Singular deleted, its 5 references repointed (Falling-Wedge ×2, Dashboard-UI-Roadmap ×2, `scan_order_block` docstring), index row 124 removed.
- ### The finding is not the duplication, it's that the definitions differ
  Singular: the bullish OB is **the last down-close candle before the break bar**. Plural (LuxAlgo): **the bar with the lowest low between the broken pivot and the break bar, after volatility parsing** — where a bar of range ≥ 2×ATR(200) gets its high/low *swapped* so a violent spike can't be mis-picked. In a multi-bar pullback **these are frequently different candles**, and only the second is spike-protected. [`scan_order_block`](tools/scanner_templates.py:3299) implements the **last-down-close shortcut with no volatility parse** — which is exactly the failure the plural page's own Common Mistakes list warns about. The greenfield page documented the shortcut *as the definition*, which is how a code simplification got laundered into wiki doctrine. Resolution recorded on the page: volatility-parsed extreme is the reference rule; last-down-close is an unvalidated simplification that happens to be what ships.
- **Stale claim killed:** the plural page still read *"No Python port yet — nothing in `tools/` computes order blocks"* and the index called OB the "highest-value un-ported SMC primitive". Both stale since 2026-07-25. Replaced with the shipped detector **and its measured result** — [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]]: 734 signals, **−0.74% @20d**, +0.11R, `MEASURED_WEAK_SCANNERS`, confluence weight **0.25**.
- ### New section from a user-supplied infographic: the OB is a base, not a candle
  The image states it twice — *the order block is "a small consolidation area on lower timeframe"*, forming from a sharp rally/decline away from a **base**, and identical in appearance to ordinary supply/demand zones. Neither page had this: both treated the OB as one candle's high–low on a single timeframe. So the rule added is detect on the working TF → **drop one or two TFs** → use the consolidation's extremes as the zone. **Tighter stop, better R:R at the same target — and missed more often, because price can turn from the unrefined parent range without ever reaching the base.** That trade-off is recorded as untested, not as an improvement.
- **The wiki already owned both halves of that machinery**, which is what makes the omission notable rather than merely absent: [[strategies/Supply-Demand-Zone-Trading]] is built on HTF-mark → TTF-entry ("zone must include the entire consolidation area"), and [[concepts/Fair-Value-Gap]] documents three-source detection (current TF + HTF + **LTF/intrabar**) from the DGT source. Order blocks never got the equivalent.
- **Documented-but-not-coded, now tabulated on the page:** volatility parse · LTF refinement · internal/swing tiers · Close-vs-High/Low as a choice · premium/discount grading · HTF-regime gate on CHoCH blocks · bearish OBs (scanner is long-only) · OB+FVG confluence — the last of which is nearly free, since [`detect_fvg_pullback`](tools/scanner_templates.py:2570) already exists.
- **Open:** the concept is well-sourced and the implementation is the thin part, so the doc↔code gap is the leading explanation for −0.74%. Cheapest candidates to move it: the **volatility parse** (a correctness fix, not tuning) and a **premium/discount or HTF-regime gate** (the page's own rank-1 quality filter). These are hypotheses to *measure* — the harness exists, which is the only reason they're worth stating. Infographic provenance is weak (unattributed retail graphic, no author/data/track record); it is credited for naming a real gap, not for evidence.

## [2026-08-07] plan | Financial-astrology weekly sector card — field assessment, and the source card reproduced to the minute
- New [[analyses/Financial-Astrology-Sector-Week-Roadmap]] (**FA-0…FA-6**), from a user-supplied "Bullion Market Outlook — Astro Trading Strategy" infographic. Goal: the same shape but **week-forward, for impacted sectors and their top stocks**.
- ### Feasibility was settled by reproducing the source rather than arguing about it
  Computing **Rahu Kaal for Tuesday 28 July 2026, Mumbai** from `ephem` locally gives **16:00–17:38 IST**; the published card prints **04:00 PM – 05:38 PM IST** — **exact to the minute**. Moon nakshatra agrees too (Purva Ashadha pada 3 at 05:30 IST rolling to pada 4 by 06:31, exactly the transition the card shows). **So the entire panchang layer is fetch-free** — no API, no login, no rate limit, no vendor. Sunrise/sunset, hora sequence, Rahu Kaal, nakshatra+pada, tithi, sub-lord are all `ephem` plus pure arithmetic. The precise opposite of the PCR/bhavcopy dependency story, and only possible because `ephem 4.2.1` was installed today (B-14).
- **Absent, each verified as zero occurrences repo-wide:** Hora, Rahu Kaal, KP sub-lord, nakshatra pada, and any planet→sector map. Worth noting `astrohora_loader.py` is a **false friend** — a CSV parser for the AstroHora desktop app (birth charts, Vimshottari dasha), not a planetary-hour calculator. **Present and reusable:** the 278-event reversal calendar to 2027-12-28, `yoga_dates`, `financial_astrology_methods`, and **the entire market half** — `opportunity_hub` already turns a sector into ranked constituents, so FA-4 needs no new market code at all.
- ### The hard part is not the astronomy, and the ordering follows from that
  Planet→sector assignments are **traditional, unprovenanced and mutually inconsistent** across sources, and they would be mapped onto *our* yfinance taxonomy — a second layer of judgement on an unmeasured first one. But **every claim on that card is precise and pre-registered**, which makes it *more* falsifiable than anything shipped this week: Rahu Kaal is an exact ~90-minute window, testable against years of intraday returns; hora partitions the session; nakshatra is daily. **So FA-2 (measure) precedes FA-3 (map).** Mapping planets to named stocks before testing whether the windows do anything is how this becomes astrology-flavoured tipping instead of a context layer — and the honest way to hold the repo's *"context modifier, never a signal"* line is a measurement, not a caption.
- **Sharp asymmetry worth recording:** PCR, RSI divergence and the OI quadrant are all unmeasured (B-19/B-25) because measuring them is real work. **Rahu Kaal is easier to test than any of them.** The real cost of FA-2 is an intraday store — `chart_server` fetches intraday bars, nothing persists them.
- **FA-0 = AW-0** and comes first regardless: `astro_trading.get_lunar_phase()` is broken in three ways and is a live path on the Astro Timing page. Two astro surfaces disagreeing about the moon would repeat the B-24 split. This roadmap supersedes [[analyses/Astro-Weekly-Forecast-Card-Fit]] in scope — same renderer and DB, plus the intraday panchang layer and the sector/stock join.
- **FA-6 renders the honesty layer INTO the PNG**, since the image travels alone: which elements are measured (with numbers), which are traditional assertions, the opening regime, and — **if FA-2 finds no edge, the card says so on its face**. A card that hides a null result is a tip sheet.

## [2026-08-07] ops | B-18 closed — remote added and pushed, and the item had overstated its own case
- `origin` → **github.com/kalyandhara/TradingBramhaTool** (private). All **88 commits** pushed; `master` tracks `origin/master` at **0 ahead / 0 behind**; remote carries exactly one branch. The two stale `claude/*` worktree branches were deliberately left local. Auth went through an existing credential helper — no credentials were seen or handled in-session.
- ### The item was right to fix and wrong about why
  B-18 said *"no off-site copy exists"*. **That was wrong**: the working tree sits under `C:\Users\Owner\OneDrive\…`, so OneDrive has been replicating everything including `.git` all along. **The real risk was close to the opposite** — a live `.git` directory inside a continuously-syncing folder is a known corruption hazard, and a proper remote fixes that by giving history a home the sync engine never touches. Corrected on [[analyses/Open-Backlog]] rather than quietly closed, because a backlog item that argued from a false premise is worth keeping visible.
- **Pre-push audit, recorded so it is not repeated:** `.gitignore` excludes `*.db`, `*.csv`, `*.xlsx`, `*.json`, `.env`, `raw/` and `tools/cache/` (captured screener.in session cookies). A credential scan across all **345 tracked files** returned exactly one hit — `first_token = bare.split()[0]` in `chart_server.py`, a variable name. No personal positions in the vault. 40 MB total.
- **Open and unverifiable from here:** that the repo is actually set to **Private**. No `gh` CLI and no API auth in-session, so visibility was taken on trust and needs a manual check. The vault holds market views and research, not credentials — but it is not meant to be public.
- **B-2 is now the only P0 left**: the chart server still runs pre-`/rsi_divergence.js` code.

B-19, B-25 and B-20a/b are one coherent piece of work.
