## [2026-08-07] build | Level linkage in OI Momentum — and the audit's own plan measured at 7%
- **Shipped Gap 4** from [[analyses/OI-Momentum-Gap-Audit]] as [price_levels.py](tools/price_levels.py) + a **Level / Range % / Lvl** column group and a per-name line. But **not** as the "composition of shipped scanners" that audit recommended, because measuring the recommendation killed it.
- ### The planned composition delivers a 7% column
  Composing `scan_structural_breakout` against a live OI scan annotates **4 of 57 readings**. Two reasons, neither visible until run: it is **bullish-only** — a resistance cluster with *no support-breakdown counterpart anywhere in this repo*, verified zero occurrences — so on a session of 35 down-moves against 22 up-moves it reaches at most 39% of rows before any quality gate; and it is a **scanner, not a level reporter**, gated on score floors (65/72), so *"no qualifying breakout"* and *"nowhere near a level"* collapse into the same blank cell. The per-symbol OHLCV fetch costs the same either way, **so it should buy a reading for every row rather than 7% of them.**
- **What shipped instead:** a symmetric location measure — where the close sits in its 60-bar range (`NEW HIGH / AT RESISTANCE / MID-RANGE / AT SUPPORT / NEW LOW` + position 0-100) — and `tag_against_oi()` turning that into the infographic's actual rule: longs built at resistance CONFIRM, longs at support DIVERGE, mirrored for shorts, and **mid-range names get no tag at all** because "built nowhere near a level" is neither confirmation nor contradiction. Nothing is scored, and the state names describe **position, never direction**.
- **Coverage 55 of 57 (96%)** against the planned 7%. States `MID-RANGE 34 / AT RESISTANCE 13 / NEW HIGH 4 / AT SUPPORT 3 / NEW LOW 1`; tags `CONFIRMS 10 / DIVERGES 4 / CONTEXT 7 / quiet 36`. The 2% band and 60-bar window were picked so that split stays informative — about a third at a level; a band flagging 90% or 5% would be arithmetic. Behind a button, since this is the only thing on the page costing a fetch **per symbol**.
- **The reads are legible**, which is the test that matters: HAL long build-up at resistance → CONFIRMS (and its PCR and 7.6× volume agree, three independent columns landing together); TCS, BRITANNIA, JUBLFOOD and RADICO short build-ups **at resistance** → DIVERGES, sellers positioning into strength rather than breakdown.
- **The real gap this exposed:** the repo has **no support-breakdown detector at all**, so the bearish half of the infographic's confirmation rule currently has nothing behind it but range position. Logged as its own item rather than papered over. Deliberately did *not* layer the bullish breakout-quality tag on top — it would be a second opinion about the same fact, over a location column that already covers everything.

## [2026-08-07] build | Index OI table — and a STRONG grade I had made unreachable
- **Shipped Gap 2** from [[analyses/OI-Momentum-Gap-Audit]]: `scan_index()` runs the same four quadrants over `IDF` rows on a second floor set, `INDEX_FLOORS`, threaded through `classify` / `summarize_symbol` / `scan` with `STOCK_FLOORS` as the default. Stock path verified byte-identical (57 readings, STRONG 7 / MODERATE 29 / UNSUPPORTED 21). Rendered in its own expander next to index PCR — index readings never appear as rows in the stock table, the same separation `pcr.py` enforces.
- ### Inheriting the stock floors would have been wrong in both directions at once
  Measured over 12 sessions: median |price %| **STOCK 0.90 vs INDEX 0.52**, median |OI %| **STOCK 1.43 vs INDEX 3.08**. An index is a diversified average so it moves ~58% as much on price, while its OI swings roughly twice as hard because index futures carry far fewer contracts and one large roll moves the percentage further. One floor set cannot serve both.
- ### The bug I put in and then caught: a threshold that could never be earned
  I first took the STRONG cuts from percentiles pooled across **all five** indices — |OI| p75 4.86 / p90 7.83. Those are inflated by FINNIFTY and NIFTYNXT50, which swing 20%+ on twenty-odd thousand contracts. The resulting cut (6.0) sat **above the liquid p90 of 4.70** and fired on **0 of 36 liquid symbol-days across 12 sessions** — unreachable by construction, and invisible on any single quiet session, which is exactly what the current one was. The fix is a rule rather than a number: **an illiquid index is forced to UNRELIABLE by `min_oi` anyway, so the only population a STRONG threshold can ever apply to is the liquid one — and that is the population it must be measured on.** Recalibrated liquid-only to min 0.5/2.5 and strong 0.9/4.5.
- **The cross-check that the calibration is honest:** matched selectivity against a scan calibrated independently. STRONG lands on **3% of liquid index symbol-days vs 3.4% of stock symbol-days**; a reading on 19% vs 27%. Two separately-derived floor sets arriving at the same strictness is the evidence neither was fitted to taste.
- **Verified** across 12 sessions: 18 index readings spanning **all four quadrants** (8 short build-up, 5 long build-up, 4 short covering, 1 long unwinding), 12 correctly UNRELIABLE on the liquidity floor, one STRONG (BANKNIFTY 2026-07-23, −0.93% on +5.48% OI). Today's session reads NO CLEAR READ on four of five, which is the floors doing their job on a quiet tape rather than a fault.
- **Recorded in passing:** the stock floor comment claims its values "sit just above those medians" — true of `MIN_OI_PCT` (1.5 vs 1.43) but **not** of `MIN_PRICE_PCT` (0.5 against a 0.90 median). Left unchanged because altering it would silently re-grade the entire stock universe, but the discrepancy is now noted in the module so the stated rationale stops misleading.

## [2026-08-07] build | Relative volume in OI Momentum — a veto, not a vote
- **Shipped Gap 1** from [[analyses/OI-Momentum-Gap-Audit]]: `fetch_volume_baseline()` + an `rvol` column + a STRONG downgrade. The column that had been aggregated and never read since the module was written is now the participation axis the source said it should be.
- **Optional by construction.** `build_oi_table` and `scan` take `volume_baseline` as a keyword; omitted, `rvol` is None everywhere and no name is downgraded for participation nobody measured. Every existing caller is untouched — verified identical strengths with and without.
- ### Three rules, each pinned by a test
  **Volume is a veto, never a vote** — it holds a would-be STRONG at MODERATE when rvol < 1.0, and can never promote MODERATE upward. **The disqualifiers still win** — near-expiry and thin-turnover names stay UNRELIABLE against rvol 5.0. **An absent baseline is absent, not normal** — no baseline, an empty one, or a symbol missing from it all give `rvol = None` and no downgrade; filling it with 1.0 would assert normal participation on no evidence, the same fault as scoring a missing factor as zero (backlog B-1).
- **The baseline excludes the scan date**, or every symbol is partly compared against itself and a real spike is diluted by its own presence in the denominator. `before=trade_date` enforces it; verified 2026-08-06 is absent from its own baseline.
- ### It downgraded nothing, exactly as the audit predicted
  The seven STRONG names on 2026-08-06 ran **1.07×–13.78×** normal volume. That is the honest outcome and was forecast before building — the audit measured STRONG at a median 3.98× with none below 1.0×, so the existing price-and-OI gates were already selecting high-participation names implicitly. **The value is that the weak-confirmation case is now detectable and named rather than silently graded STRONG**, plus a positive caveat that fires daily: 17 of 57 readings now carry a "volume ran Nx normal" note that previously existed nowhere. Because the downgrade path is unreachable from live data, it was exercised **synthetically** across rvol None/3.5/1.0/0.99/0.4 rather than left unverified.
- **Open:** the 1.0× cut and the 5-session window came from a single session's distribution and want re-measuring; and `rvol` refines a grade that [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] has never validated in the first place.

## [2026-08-07] ingest | Fingrad OI-analysis infographic — it matches our quadrants, and points at the one column we throw away
- **Ingested** [[sources/Fingrad-OI-Analysis-Infographic]]; audited `nse_oi_momentum.py` against it in [[analyses/OI-Momentum-Gap-Audit]]. The sheet's four-quadrant price×OI grid **reproduces ours exactly**, sign convention on the down-move pair included — independent corroboration of a framework we derived from NSE's own page, and worth recording as such rather than as news.
- ### The real hole: we aggregate volume and never read it
  The source makes volume the confirmation axis in so many words — *High Volume + OI↑ = **Strong Signal**, Low Volume + OI↑ = **Weak Confirmation***. `build_oi_table` computes `volume=("TtlTradgVol","sum")` at [nse_oi_momentum.py:161](tools/nse_oi_momentum.py:161) and **the column is referenced nowhere else in the module or the dashboard** — verified by grep, one occurrence, the definition. `summarize_symbol` grades STRONG/MODERATE on price and OI magnitude alone, and turnover appears only as a *disqualifying floor*, never as *confirmation*. So a name can be graded STRONG on a big move, a big OI build and **below-average participation**, and nothing in the output says so.
- ### But the honest version is that the hole is smaller than the source implies
  "High volume" is relative and the bhavcopy is one session, so I built a 5-session baseline to measure it. Relative volume across 208 symbols: **p50 0.80×, p75 1.45×, p90 2.39×**. Against the day's 57 readings: **STRONG median 3.98×, MODERATE 1.22×, UNSUPPORTED 1.48×** — and **zero STRONG names had rvol below 1**. The existing price-and-OI magnitude gates are already selecting high-participation names *implicitly*, so volume would corroborate the current tiers far more often than overturn them; on 2026-08-06 it would not have changed a single verdict. Still worth shipping — as a reported column plus a **downgrade** condition, to make the weak-confirmation case *detectable* rather than merely improbable — but expecting it to move many names would be wrong, and one session is not a distribution.
- **Three scope gaps:** **index OI is absent** (IDF, 5 underlyings, same file, zero extra fetch — NIFTY sat at −3.46% OI that session; the stock/index separation `pcr.py` just established applies unchanged), **strike-level option OI** is absent (PCR closed part of it same-day), and **no S/R-breakout linkage** although we own `scan_structural_breakout` and friends. Ordered volume → index → breakout → strike-level in the audit.
- **Noticed while updating the index: both page counters had drifted and disagreed with each other.** Frontmatter read `pages: 204 / sources_ingested: 78`, the body line read `Pages: 198 | Sources ingested: 81` (dated 2026-07-30), and the vault actually holds **226 content pages and 91 source pages**. Two counters, two different wrong numbers, neither checked against the filesystem — corrected to the real counts rather than incremented onward. A lint pass should compute these rather than trust them.
- **Two places we are already ahead of the source, worth stating:** it never mentions **rollover** — its examples read OI at face value, which near expiry turns ordinary contract-rolling into a confident fictitious covering signal, the exact trap our series-total sum exists to avoid — and it has **no liquidity floor or refusal-to-classify**, so every name always gets a label where we return `NO_READ`. *A teaching sheet omitting rollover is how that trap propagates.*

## [2026-08-07] build | pcr.py — stock-first, and the stock/index split turned out to be forced
- **Shipped** [pcr.py](tools/pcr.py) and its OI-Momentum column. Priority was stock-specific per instruction, with index kept scalable but **distinct**. Added `nse_oi_momentum.fetch_fo_bhavcopy_raw()` so one download feeds both consumers; the existing `fetch_fo_bhavcopy()` contract is unchanged (re-verified: same 622 STF-only rows). Full record: [[analyses/PCR-in-OI-Momentum]], concept at [[concepts/Put-Call-Ratio]].
- ### The reason for separating stock from index got stronger while building
  The expected reason was **level**: index puts are the standard portfolio hedge, so index PCR sits near 1.0 (NIFTY 1.045) while the stock median is **0.675** — which is why the textbook <0.70/>1.30 bands label 59% of stocks bearish. The stronger reason only appeared on contact with the data: **the two classes cannot share a calibration *method*.** Stock PCR self-calibrates against **208 same-day peers** and needs no stored history. Index PCR has **5 observations a day**, one of which (NIFTYNXT50) showed PCR 2.014 off **5,200 contracts** against NIFTY's 453,484,425 — a cross-sectional percentile over that is arithmetic without meaning. Index can only be ranked against its own past, and nothing here stores one.
- **So `index_reading()` ships deliberately unbanded**, returning level and one-day slope with `band=None` and a `band_reason` explaining the absence. Borrowing the stock cuts would have produced a confident label from an invalid comparison. *An uncalibrated number is reportable; an uncalibrated number wearing a calibrated label is not.*
- **Bands name a position, never a direction** — PUT-HEAVY / PUT-LEANING / MIDDLE / CALL-LEANING / CALL-HEAVY at the 20/40/60/80th percentiles of the day's liquid cross-section. Calling a band "bullish" would bake in the trend-following reading and hide the contrarian one, and [[concepts/Put-Call-Ratio]] records that PCR has no fixed sign. Direction stays the consumer's call.
- **In the UI it sits beside the verdict, never inside it:** a `PCR / PCR %ile / ΔPCR / Options` column group plus a per-name line that says outright "this does not change the reading above", and the index table in its own expander so the two scales are never presented side by side. The futures quadrant is untouched and still traces to two bhavcopy fields.
- **Verified** on 2026-08-06: 208 stock underlyings (186 liquid, median 0.675, quintile cuts 0.577/0.639/0.701/0.800), bands splitting 37/37/37/37/38 as percentile cuts must; FINNIFTY (786,600 OI) and NIFTYNXT50 (5,200) correctly excluded by the index floor with a stated reason; join into OI Momentum covered **52 of 57 readings (91%)** with the other 5 blank rather than fabricated; confirm/diverge fired 13 times and stayed silent 44 — it speaks only above ±0.09 ΔPCR (≈ the outer decile). Dashboard rendered clean in an isolated boot, no exceptions.
- **Open:** floors and cuts came from **one session** and need re-measuring; an index PCR history is the single thing blocking an index band; and PCR remains **unmeasured by [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]]**, so it annotates a verdict and never moves one.

## [2026-08-07] ingest | Fingrad PCR infographic — and the option data was in a file we already download
- **Ingested** [[sources/Fingrad-PCR-Infographic]] (nine-panel Hindi/English sheet, same publisher as the 2026-07-19 collection). Created [[concepts/Put-Call-Ratio]] and [[analyses/PCR-in-OI-Momentum]], which answers the question the ingest was requested alongside: can PCR go into the OI-Momentum verdict.
- ### The feed blocker was stale, and it cost nothing to find out
  PCR has been carried as *planned but unbuildable* since inception — `sentiment_index.py` factor 5, weight ~20, "requires NSE option-chain feed". **The F&O bhavcopy `nse_oi_momentum.py` already downloads carries 27,277 stock-option (STO) and 5,026 index-option (IDO) rows** with `OptnTp`, `OpnIntrst` and `ChngInOpnIntrst`. It throws them away with `FinInstrmTp == "STF"`. Index and per-stock PCR are computable at **zero additional network cost**. Third time this pattern has appeared here after the PEAD "no consensus estimate for NSE" episode: **a data-source limitation is a measurement with a date on it, not a property of the world** — and nobody had re-checked this one.
- ### The finding: the textbook PCR bands are index-calibrated and wrong one level down
  Measured on the 2026-08-06 bhavcopy. Index PCR sits near 1.0 (**NIFTY 1.045, BANKNIFTY 1.019**) — but across **208 single stocks the median is 0.675**, p95 only 1.007. Applying the sheet's <0.70/0.70–1.00/1.00–1.30/>1.30 scale to that cross-section labels **59% of the F&O universe "bearish" and 5% "bullish" on an ordinary session, with the median stock reading bearish**. A scale on which the typical name is bearish every day is measuring its own miscalibration, not sentiment. Cause is structural: index puts carry portfolio-hedging demand that single-stock options do not. Thresholds must be **per-instrument-class percentiles from the observed cross-section** — the rule `nse_oi_momentum.py` already states for its own materiality floors.
- ### The advice: compute it, but keep it out of `classify()`
  **PCR has no fixed sign** — trend-following reads >1 as bullish, contrarian reads extremes as crowding, both standard, and nothing inside PCR selects the regime. The source asserts both and never reconciles them. Folding a directionally ambiguous input into a verdict that is currently unambiguous and fully inspectable makes it worse; it would also blend *futures* OI with *options* OI into a label that can no longer be inverted back to its inputs. Add that PCR is **unmeasured by the harness** — the week after a commit removing unmeasured correlated inputs from a score — and that it **already has an owner** in Sentiment Index factor 5, so building it inside OI Momentum would be a second implementation of the same quantity. **Recommended: one `pcr.py` primitive, rendered in OI Momentum as a second column with a confirm/diverge tag, consumed by Sentiment Index as the weighted factor, slope preferred over level, backtested before it is ever allowed to move a verdict.**
- **Source errors recorded:** the defining formula is printed as `18,00,000 + 12,00,000 = 1.50` — the operator must be **÷** (the answer is right, the symbol is not) — plus the unreconciled trend/contrarian split and the band miscalibration above. **Max Pain** is cleanly separated by the sheet (probable expiry zone from total option OI, vs PCR's sentiment from the split) and has neither a page nor a tool here.

## [2026-08-07] build | RSI divergence on the chart — all four variants, and the repaint said out loud
- **Shipped** `rsi_divergence.js` + its integration layer in [chart_dashboard.html](tools/chart_dashboard.html): regular and hidden divergence, both directions, drawn across the price swings **and** the matching RSI swings. Prompted by a FinGrad infographic, but the spec was already ours — [[concepts/RSI]] has carried the four-variant table since 2026-06-22, so this is that page made executable, not a new claim.
- **Almost entirely composition.** The two hard parts already existed: `rsiSeries` (Wilder, already hardened against the LIQUIDCASE phantom-plunge bug) and `EW.computeZigzag` for swings. Reusing the zigzag means Elliott, Fibonacci and divergence all agree about where the swings are — one structure detector, three consumers. The module takes the dashboard's own RSI series as an argument, so the line you SEE and the values the divergence is measured from cannot drift.
- ### The judgement call: a coarser swing filter, chosen by measurement
  At Elliott's `{atrMult:1.2, minPct:0.5}` a 300-bar daily chart yields **~132 pivots** — a swing every other bar — and the divergences falling out have a **median span of 8 bars and ±0.4% price legs**. Divergence between two 2-bar wiggles is noise with a label on it. At `{atrMult:2.0, minPct:1.5}`: **~43 pivots, 27-bar median span**, which is the multi-week scale the concept actually means. Elliott solves its own clutter downstream by scoring structures; this has no such filter, so it had to be solved at the pivot level.
- ### Repainting, stated rather than hidden
  A zigzag's last pivot is provisional, so any divergence anchored to it can move or vanish as bars arrive. That is the standard failure of divergence indicators on retail platforms — the line silently disappears and the screenshot no longer matches the chart. Every divergence carries `provisional`; those render faded with a `?` on the marker. **Repainting is acceptable; silent repainting is not.**
- **A UX flaw caught in verification:** selecting divergence auto-enables RSI (half the signal lives in that pane). Re-asserting that every render pass meant unticking RSI silently re-added it — the box bounced back and the click appeared broken. Now it fires only on the transition, so turning RSI off deliberately holds and divergence keeps drawing its price-side lines alone.
- **Verified**: synthetic case with a known regular-bullish divergence detected exactly; six real NSE names produced 35 divergences across **all four types** with every label independently re-checked against the definition table (0 misclassified); `lineOnGrid` holds the full-bar-grid invariant `padToBars` exists to protect (interpolated, not a 2-point series, which would both break logical-range sync and refuse to draw across whitespace); all four on/off state transitions correct in a live isolated chart-server boot; colour/dash/fade encoding confirmed on the rendered series.
- **Open:** divergence is *a tell, not a clock* and [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] has never measured it — fine for a chart overlay, not yet earned for the scanner pipeline. `detect_bullish_rsi_divergence` in `scanner_templates.py` remains a cruder, disagreeing implementation (rolling-mean RSI, no pivots, regular-bullish only); reconciling it is open.

## [2026-08-07] build | The day's funnel leaves the machine — share card, chat text, detailed PDF
- **Shipped** [opportunity_report.py](tools/opportunity_report.py): one normalised model rendered three ways — paste-able text, a 1080px PNG share card, and a multi-page **vector** PDF — wired into both the stored Daily Shortlist and the live manual pipeline. Full record: [[analyses/Opportunity-Hub-Roadmap]] Phase 6.
- **matplotlib for the PDF**, chosen over installing reportlab and over a Pillow raster page: a detailed report is read by *searching* it, so Ctrl+F for a symbol has to work, and it has to print sharp. `Figure`/`PdfPages` used directly rather than `pyplot` so no global figure state is touched on Streamlit's worker threads. Follows [sector_card.py](tools/sector_card.py)'s doctrine otherwise — no Streamlit import, palette from `report_style`, Pillow for the card because it is a layout and not a plot.
- ### The constraint: this is the first thing here built to leave the machine
  Until now the honesty discipline could lean on the reader's context — you knew what tape the list came from because you were sitting in front of it. A file that travels has no such backstop, so each format states, unprompted, the **regime it was produced in**, that confluence is **agreement and not measured evidence**, which scanners have measured edge and which were **measured and failed**, and that it is an automated research output, not advice and not a buy list. The PDF labels every scanner inline with its tier and groups a name's hits **by family**, so "four of these six were one family talking to itself" is visible rather than inferred. A card without those is a tip sheet.
- ### The bug: the card clipped the warning it exists to carry
  A fixed banner height truncated the regime verdict mid-sentence at *"Treat this list as a"* — on the one artifact designed to be shared, cutting exactly the sentence that keeps it honest. Now measured on a probe canvas before the real one is allocated; the same fault in row height drew the bearish-flag line straight through the next row's rule and got the same fix. `sector_card` hit this identical class on its footer and it is worth naming: **a hard-coded height silently eats the text you most need**.
- **One correctness call:** the live-funnel export first borrowed its regime from the newest *stored* shortlist — which can be days old, so a fresh export would have carried a stale regime, precisely the misread the stored `market_regime` column exists to prevent. It now reads the benchmark frame the run actually scanned against, through the same `_market_context` the headless job uses.
- **Verified live**: three formats off a real 78-name shortlist (PNG 122 KB, PDF 89 KB / 14 pages); PDF text confirmed extractable and searchable; the **bearish-flag and empty-day paths, neither reachable from stored data**, exercised synthetically and confirmed to print the WARNING line and a "no name was caught" statement rather than an empty page; both buttons driven in an isolated boot.

## [2026-08-07] build | Opportunity Hub step 4 becomes a pipeline — and the confluence count stops lying
- **Shipped** `run_scanner_pipeline` + `SCANNER_FAMILIES` + the three weight tiers in [opportunity_hub.py](tools/opportunity_hub.py), now shared by the interactive funnel and the headless daily job. Manual step 4 gained an **All scanners (pipeline)** mode: 23 scanners in one pass over the shortlisted sector/industry leaders, each card showing how many caught the name. Full record: [[analyses/Opportunity-Hub-Roadmap]] Phase 5.
- ### The pipeline already existed — it was in the wrong place, and it counted the wrong thing
  `opportunity_daily` had been accumulating multi-scanner confluence since Phase 1.5, but over **9 of 24** scanners and on a universe it rebuilt itself — so it could not consume the funnel's own sector/industry shortlist, which is exactly what was asked for. The work was routing, plus one correction that turned out to be the substance.
- ### A raw "N scanners caught it" is a count of correlated votes
  A stock in a clean uptrend near its highs mechanically fires 52-Week High + Momentum Continuation + Stage 2 + Near Pivot + ATH Breakout. **That is one fact reported five times**, and ranking on it promotes precisely the momentum cohort [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] measured at or below a random-entry baseline — the metric would have systematically surfaced the weakest evidence. Scanners now sit in **6 families**, each contributing only its best member's weight. Both numbers show: the raw count because it was asked for, the family count because that is what the score spends. Measured effect: five correlated trend hits score **50%** confluence, while Falling Wedge + Order Block (2 scanners, 2 families) scores **62%**.
  Three more counting faults fixed in passing: **Downside Risk** is bearish and was worth +1 confluence (now runs, warns, never votes); **Zero-Debt Quality PEAD** declares `fn = scan_pead`, identical to PEAD Drift, so every PEAD hit became two votes (excluded); and **Order Block Retest** held a full-weight `MEASURED_EDGE` seat on a **−0.74%** measured forward edge (demoted to the new 0.25 tier for scanners measured at or below baseline).
- ### The bug it surfaced — the headless job was faking all-time highs
  `opportunity_daily` built one `_FrameCache(days=400)` and handed it to every scanner including **ATH Breakout**, which carries `needs_full_history`. `scan_ath_breakout` only requires 250 bars, so it fired and called a **~1.6-year high an all-time high** — the interactive Hub had routed those through `days=6000` since Phase 3, but the headless job never got the fix. `run_scanner_pipeline` now takes a `fetch_for(scanner_name)` **factory**, making the rule structural rather than something each caller must remember. Third instance of the house pattern: *a data fault wearing a market result's clothes*.
- **Verified live**: 23/23 scanners family-mapped against the registry, no typos or duplicates; confluence math checked on five hand-built cases; the fetch router proven to split full-history from the cheap window; a real 8-leader run caught 7 in **16 fetches, not 184**; DB migration additive with **188 existing rows preserved**, and pre-migration rows re-deriving their family count from the stored scanner list; funnel driven end-to-end in an isolated boot with engine dump and rendered DOM matching symbol-for-symbol. **The running dashboard predates the change and needs a restart** (backlog B-2 again).
- **Open, and stated on the card:** no backtest shows that confluence *itself* predicts anything — `backtest.py` measures scanners one at a time. Until co-firing is compared against single-firing, the Hub says *agreement, not evidence*.

## [2026-08-04] build | PEAD Phases 2+3 — the constraint the roadmap was built on had expired
- **Shipped** [earnings_calendar.py](tools/earnings_calendar.py): real NSE result dates, a **true SUE**, and the symbol-level enrichment join. Wired into `run_scanner` via a new `SCANNERS[…]["enrich"]` hook and into the Phase-5.1 composite. `scan_pead` now returns `event_date` — it had been discarding the bar dates at `reset_index(drop=True)`, which was the one thing Phase 2 needed. Scanner renamed **"Proxy-PEAD Drift" → "PEAD Drift"**. Full record: [[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]].
- ### The headline: the "no consensus estimate for NSE" constraint was false
  The roadmap's central section, three `scan_pead` docstrings, and the whole of Phase 3 rested on it. **`yfinance.get_earnings_dates()` returns `EPS Estimate`, `Reported EPS` and `Surprise(%)` for `.NS` symbols** — probe over **15 names across the cap spectrum → 15/15 had estimates**, small-caps included. The roadmap's specific worry, that coverage would be sparse for Indian mid/small-caps, did not reproduce.
  **What that deleted:** the entire screener.in path — `fetch_quarterly_eps`, the Playwright `screener_login.py` session, the 4:05 PM enrichment batch. The most fragile dependency on the roadmap, dropped rather than deferred. `lxml`, the stated Phase-2 blocker, was installed all along; the check had been run against the wrong interpreter (bare `python` resolves to an unrelated hermes venv, not the project's `pythoncore-3.14`).
  **The lesson generalises:** a vendor limitation was recorded once, on 2026-07-23, then quoted as fact for twelve days and cost one abandoned session-capture build. **A data-source limit is a measurement with a date on it, not a property of the world** — the wiki's own "price levels decay, always timestamp them" rule applies to capability claims just as hard.
- ### The finding: a confirmed earnings date is not a confirmed *setup*
  **BAJFINANCE** produced a textbook bullish hit — +8.3%, 3×ATR, **8σ** volume, gapped +3.9% — one day after a **confirmed** result date. The first cut of the enrichment duly promoted it. **It had missed consensus by 37% (EPS 8.57 vs 13.70, SUE −10σ).**
  PEAD is drift *in the direction of the surprise*; a rally on a large miss is evidence the mechanism is **not** what is driving the price. So adding real data made the scanner **wrong in a new way** until a direction check went in — the upgrade that finds more must also reject more. Divergent hits now earn no bonus and are relabelled *"(surprise diverges)"*, but are **not** demoted below their Phase-1 score: Phase 1 didn't know, and inventing a penalty is the unmeasured-weight habit [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] exists to end. Whether divergence deserves a real penalty is now the sharpest question for Phase 4.
- **Verified live** on 25 NSE names: TCS confirmed (+1.17σ beat, 79.11 → 88.03), BAJFINANCE divergent (79.71 → unchanged). Both yfinance data traps handled and tested — **duplicate rows per result date** (HCLTECH's 2026-07-13 appears twice, one all-NaN; the empty twin silently overwrites the real one if keyed naively — the same shape `ownership_cache` hit with event-driven filings) and **US/Eastern timestamps** (`2026-07-08 20:00 −04:00` is **9 July** IST, otherwise reconciled against the wrong bar). Four negative paths verified; none of them alters a score. `py_compile` clean, both servers HTTP 200 — though the **running dashboard predates the change and needs a restart**, exactly backlog **B-2**.

## [2026-08-02] plan | Open backlog consolidated — 18 items, and the pattern behind six of them
- New [[analyses/Open-Backlog-2026-08-02]] gathers everything left open at the end of the session into one index (B-1…B-18). Dedicated roadmaps stay authoritative; the backlog points rather than copies.
- **B-1 is the one that matters and it is live.** Today's sentiment refresh scored sector participation over **3 sector indices where the reading it replaced used 11** — `_sector_perf` divides by whatever it managed to fetch, so a quarter of the evidence produced a full-confidence factor, and that factor fed an **AGGRESSIVE verdict at 100% position size**. [market_health.py](tools/market_health.py) already solved exactly this (coverage reported, `NOT_CHECKED` below 50%); `sentiment_index` has no equivalent.
- **The four uncached ownership symbols are probably our bug.** `ABBOTINDIA`, `BAYERCROP` and `MCX` come back *"no filings"* from `corporate-share-holdings-master`, and `BSE` has no cache file at all. All four are large, liquid names that certainly file quarterly — first suspect is the `f["symbol"].upper() == symbol.upper()` filter in `get_shareholding_filings`. 496/500 cached.
- **Three claims parked as measurable rather than believed:** the five conflicting volume thresholds, the Connors stop-vs-no-stop contradiction, and the Volume-Profile 80% rule. Each is fully specified and can go straight through [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]].
- ### The thesis worth carrying out of this session
  **Six bugs found today. Not one threw an exception.** DS-2's regexes matched nothing · `build_dataset` returned an empty frame · a 403 sat behind it · a z-score divided by ~1e-14 · a full moon rendered as *"waning, 16.7%"* · sentiment scored on 3 of 11 indices. **Every one produced a confident-looking number with nothing behind it**, and every one was found by checking coverage rather than by an error surfacing.
  The defence is cheap and already proven three times in this repo — **carry coverage with the data and refuse to score below a floor**. `market_health` does it, `red_flags` does it via three-state NOT_CHECKED, `rrg` does it by omitting unmeasurable sectors. B-1 is that same fix applied to the last component still missing it.
- **Suggested order:** B-1 → B-2 (one restart) → B-3 (the advice/tooling contradiction) → B-7 (settles five open numbers at once).

## [2026-08-02] ingest | ForexTester — Breakout Trading, and the volume threshold that isn't one
- Source: `raw/Breakout Trading… _ FTO.html` (FTO Editorial, 26.05.2026) → [[sources/ForexTester-Breakout-Guide]]. Fourth ForexTester ingest; published as the explicit complement to [[sources/ForexTester-Mean-Reversion-Guide]].
- **Created [[concepts/Breakout]]** — the umbrella, mirroring [[concepts/Mean-Reversion]]. The wiki had ten breakout *strategies* and *patterns* (ORB, Darvas, VCP, High-Tight-Flag, Falling-Wedge…) and no page saying what a breakout **is** or how to confirm one.
- **Content captured:** the five-item confirmation stack (real level → **close not wick** → volume → trend filter agrees → ATR stop preset) · break-vs-**retest** entry as a throughput-vs-precision trade-off · three trade plans with `1–1.5×ATR` stops, `1.5R–2R` first targets, `1×ATR` trails, `0.5–1%` risk · nine setups · the false-breakout filter list.
- **Best single portable line:** *"Do not set the stop inside the range you just broke. That gets hit by noise."* The range is where price has already proven it oscillates — a stop there is a stop in the noisiest available location.
- ### The finding: there is no standard volume-confirmation threshold
  This article states **`≥120% of the 20-bar average`** as a hard rule, three times. **The same publisher's volume article, ingested hours ago, states `1.5×` the 20-period average** — also as the rule, also uncited. **1.2 vs 1.5, same vendor, same month.**
  With our own three (`1.5×`/20d in the breakout scanners, `1.5×`/50 in VCP, `1.4×`/50-DMA in structural breakout) that is **five numbers for one concept**, every one presented by its source as settled. The honest conclusion, now recorded on [[concepts/Volume-Analysis]] and [[concepts/Breakout]]: **the threshold is a tunable to be swept, not a constant to be adopted** — and the sweep is not free, since a lower bar admits more trades of lower quality. Settle it for our universe with [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]], not by adopting whichever article was read last.
- **Two repo asymmetries recorded.** ① **We detect breaks well and retests poorly** — the article's *preferred* entry is the retest, and `scan_order_block` is the only scanner that triggers on one rather than on the break itself. ② This is the **exact mirror** of the gap logged in the previous entry: ~24 scanners heavily weighted to the breakout regime, nothing for reversion, while `classify_regime` reports both.
- **One thing we already do better than the source.** Its false-breakout exit is impressionistic — *"a fast close back inside the range"*. Our `detect_structure_chain` time-boxes it (close back inside **within 3 bars**), which makes failed breaks countable rather than a judgement call.
- **A pleasing symmetry worth keeping:** expanding Bollinger Bandwidth means *stand aside* to a reversion trader and *stand ready* to a breakout trader. Same measurement, opposite instruction, resolved only by which regime you have classified — which is why `classify_regime` is upstream of both pages.

## [2026-08-02] ingest | ForexTester — Mean Reversion (a real contradiction, and a playbook with no tooling)
- Source: saved page `Mean Reversion Trading… _ FTO.html` (Denys R., 26.05.2026) → [[sources/ForexTester-Mean-Reversion-Guide]]. Third ForexTester ingest today; same prescriptive, results-free house style.
- **Created [[concepts/Mean-Reversion]]** — the general discipline. [[strategies/Quantified-MA-Mean-Reversion]] already existed but is one *specific* Connors system (dual-MA stretch + limit ladder + no stop), not the umbrella.
- **The framing worth keeping:** mean reversion is a **regime bet before it is a setup**. High win rate in ranges, large losses when trends run — the win rate is the seduction and the tail is the bill, which is exactly the payoff shape that flatters a short track record. Regime classification is therefore a prerequisite, not a refinement.
- **Content captured:** five-step skeleton · three interchangeable stretch measures (RSI/Stoch 30/70, **z-score ±2**, **1.5–2 ATR** from mean) · six setups with entry/stop/exit including pairs-trading statistics (z `>|2|` in, `0–0.5` out, `>|3|` or 10 bars stop) · stops **1–2 ATR beyond the extreme, never at the mean** · 0.5–1% risk · **~10-bar time stop** · failure modes each with a tell, the subtle one being a **volatility-regime shift that leaves the signal looking identical while its calibrating distribution has moved**.
- ### A genuine contradiction, and I have left it unresolved on purpose
  This source makes stops **mandatory** for mean reversion. [[strategies/Quantified-MA-Mean-Reversion]] uses **no stop at all**, because Connors' 2001–2013 equity backtests found stops *reduced or eliminated* the edge. Both describe mean reversion and they disagree on its most consequential rule.
  The reconciliation **neither source states**: a stopless fade is only survivable when something else bounds the loss. Connors carries three such bounds — a hard `Close > MA(200)` regime filter, a limit ladder buying progressively deeper, and multi-day holds. An FX intraday fade has none of them. Recorded as a contradiction callout on **both** pages, with the settling experiment named: run one reversion entry through [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] with and without a 2-ATR stop.
- ### The finding that matters more than the article
  `market_verdict.REGIME_PLAYBOOKS["SIDEWAYS"]` tells the user **"Range-bound. Mean-reversion works, breakouts fake out."** — and the `SCANNERS` registry holds ~24 scanners that are almost entirely **breakout, trend and continuation**. The reversion-adjacent few (Early Reversal Detector, Rounding Bottom, Harmonic Reversal) are **reversal-pattern** detectors rather than fade-the-stretch screens; even "RSI 60-40 Setup" is a trend-pullback entry. **On a SIDEWAYS day the dashboard prescribes a playbook and then offers two dozen tools for the opposite one.** Every input a `scan_mean_reversion` would need already exists — Wilder `_rsi_series`, ATR, and rolling z-scores in `rrg.py`.
- **Also absent from every scanner we ship: a time dimension.** Each structured plan carries entry/stop/target and nothing that closes a trade which simply never resolves.

## [2026-08-02] ingest | ForexTester — Volume Analysis (and three thresholds of our own that disagree)
- Source: `https://forextester.com/blog/volume-analysis-trading/` → [[sources/ForexTester-Volume-Analysis-Guide]]. Second ingest from this publisher today; same prescriptive-vendor character as [[sources/ForexTester-Anchored-VWAP-Guide]].
- **Created [[concepts/Volume-Analysis]]** — the umbrella page this wiki did not have. Volume-Profile, VWAP, Anchored-VWAP and Order-Flow all existed and all *assume* a shared foundation that was written down nowhere.
- **The headline is the caveat, not the technique.** Equities and futures report real exchange volume; **forex is OTC and reports tick volume** — a count of price *changes* per bar, correlating with real volume *"roughly 90%+ on liquid pairs"* and explicitly weaker on exotics. Crypto is real but venue-local. **Every volume-derived overlay inherits whatever its feed counts**, which matters here because `HyperliquidSource` feeds the same chart that draws Volume Profile, OBV and AVWAP.
- **Thresholds recorded:** `1.5×` the 20-period average = above-average / breakout confirmed · `2×` high conviction · **`500%+` at resistance = climactic, reversal risk not continuation** (more volume is not monotonically better) · value area `70%` · MFI `>80`/`<20`.
- **New to the wiki: the Volume Profile "80% rule"** — price entering the value area and holding **two consecutive 30-minute bars** implies ~80% odds of a full VAL→VAH traverse. Filed on [[concepts/Volume-Profile]] as *a claim with a number attached, from a vendor blog with no data behind it* — but fully specified (entry, target, probability), so [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] can measure it rather than anyone believing it.
- **It exposed an inconsistency in our own code, which is the real value of the ingest.** The article's standard — **≥1.5× the 20-period average** — **matches our breakout scanners exactly** (`scanner_templates.py` ~1191, ~1276), same multiple, same lookback, arrived at independently. But VCP (~755) uses `1.5×`/**50**-period and structural breakout (~1032) rejects below `1.4×`/50-DMA. **Three thresholds, two lookbacks, one concept, and nothing recording why.** Probably accidental rather than chosen.
- **Resolved an apparent contradiction nobody had written down.** The article says volume shrinking on an impulse leg means the trend is weakening; our VCP scanner treats `base_vol_ratio <= 0.7` as *constructive*. Not a conflict — **contraction on an impulse leg means demand is failing, contraction inside a base means supply is exhausted**. The same measurement inverts on context, and any rule testing volume contraction has to say which case it means.
- **Publishes no results at all** and recommends 50–100 trades before concluding — more honest than the same publisher's AVWAP piece, which led with a 2.02 profit factor over 30 trades.
- **Noted as absent from the repo:** **MFI** is implemented nowhere and is cheap — RSI weighted by volume, and Wilder `_rsi_series` already exists. **OBV** ships as a chart indicator but has no wiki page.

## [2026-08-02] ingest | ForexTester — Anchored VWAP (and a gap in the indicator we shipped this morning)
- Source: `https://forextester.com/blog/anchored-vwap/` → [[sources/ForexTester-Anchored-VWAP-Guide]].
- **Updated rather than created.** [[concepts/VWAP]] and [[concepts/Anchored-VWAP-Pyramiding]] already existed and already carried the behavioural framing, so this extends both instead of adding a third overlapping page.
- **What is genuinely new: the dual-anchor read.** Above the AVWAP anchored at a swing low, buyers from that low are in profit; below the AVWAP anchored at a swing high, sellers from that high are. **Between the two is "no man's land"** — neither cohort under pressure, edge dissolved. A single AVWAP can only say *above* or *below*; only a pair can say **"neither, stand aside"**. Same discipline as [[strategies/Crash-Proof-3-Filter-System]]'s STAND DOWN: a condition that tells you not to act is worth as much as one that tells you to.
- **Also new:** the explicit formula and its four price-input variants, **Brian Shannon** (alphatrends.net) as originator, an anchor-strength ranking (swing high/low → earnings → IPO → breakout → macro event), and the doctrine that **anchor selection is "the most subjective — and most critical — aspect"**.
- **Timing made this an audit.** We shipped Anchored VWAP hours earlier (`70601b3`, refined `f7570b0`), so the article reads as an independent spec to check against. Formula **identical** (`Σ(HLC/3 × V)/Σ(V)`, no reset); session-VWAP-intraday-only **matches**; on missing volume and on anchor significance **we are stricter** — the ≥2.0 ATR prominence filter is a mechanical form of the article's "avoid structurally insignificant bars", and ours is measured (median prominence ≈1.55 ATR, p90 ≈2.25) rather than asserted.
- **Two gaps recorded on the concept page.** ❌ We anchor **only to swing lows**, so the chart cannot express no-man's-land — closing that is the highest-value next change to the indicator. ⚠️ We **automated the anchor judgement away** rather than exposing it; the article calls that judgement the whole analysis, and we have no click-to-anchor, so earnings / breakout / IPO / macro anchors are unreachable.
- **The backtest in the article is not evidence and the article says so.** 53% win · 2.02 profit factor · $3,322 on $10K — over **30 trades on one instrument**, with its own text conceding that is "not enough to draw firm conclusions". Recorded with that caveat attached, per the standard [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] set when it found only 1 of 5 of our own scanners beating a random-entry baseline.
- **No contradictions.** The source independently confirms two claims this wiki already made: session VWAP must not be applied to swing trades, and AVWAP's power is behavioural rather than mathematical.

## [2026-08-02] build | AW-0 SHIPPED — the Astro page and the calendar now agree about the moon
- `_lunar_phase_approximate` is **deleted, not repaired**. `get_lunar_phase` in [astro_trading.py](tools/astro_trading.py) reads `reversal_calendar.db` and interpolates between real new-moon instants. Plan: [[analyses/Astro-Weekly-Forecast-Card-Fit]].
- **Live before/after on the Astro Timing page:** "**New · 56% illuminated · cycle day 0.6**" → "**Waning · 78.1% · cycle day 19.0 of 29.0**". 2026-08-02 is four days past the 29 Jul full moon and nineteen after the 14 Jul new moon, so the new reading is correct on all three fields where the old one was wrong on all three.
- **Unplaceable dates now read as unplaced.** Outside DB coverage the phase is `unknown` with `None` figures, the page shows `—` plus a warning naming the fix, and the recommendation text says the lunar read is *absent, not neutral*. The old code extrapolated a mean cycle forever and produced a confident number for **any** date — which is how a full moon came to render as "waning, 16.7%".
- **Two more bugs of the same family surfaced during verification**, both visible on the shipped page:
  - `_calculate_market_intensity` interpolated with `50 + 30·sin(…)`, which **peaks at first quarter and sits at 50 on new and full** — the exact inverse of its own docstring *and* of the hardcoded branches it interpolates between. Now `abs(cos)`: 80 new, 75 full, ~52 quarters.
  - `_solar_timing_approximate` called `.astimezone()` on a **naive** datetime, so 6 AM was interpreted in the host machine's zone and converted — the page displayed **sunrise 15:30, sunset 03:30 IST**. Now `localize`; 06:00/18:00.
- **The dormant ephem path was wrong too** and is fixed: it computed `cycle_day` as `(illumination/100)·29.53`, treating a cosine as a line, so day 7 and day 22 — both ~50% lit — collapsed onto one answer.
- **The subtlety worth keeping:** quadrant labels must be **centred** on the events they name, not started at them. Start-aligned bands labelled a full moon at fraction 0.483 as `waxing` while its own illumination read 99.7% — **11 of 44** DB events mislabelled. Centred bands: **0 of 44**. My first implementation had this wrong and the test caught it.
- **Verified:** 6-part self-check passes (four previously-wrong dates · all 44 DB lunar events · monotone illumination new→full · out-of-coverage without raising · intensity peaking at new/full · weekly calendar builds). Live page renders with zero exceptions.
- *Same theme, sixth entry: nothing here threw an exception. Every one of these produced a plausible-looking number that was simply wrong.*

## [2026-08-02] plan | Astro weekly forecast card — feasible, and the moon is currently wrong
- New **good-to-have** backlog page [[analyses/Astro-Weekly-Forecast-Card-Fit]] (AW-0…AW-4). Asked to assess whether a weekly astro forecast card could be built from existing sources.
- **Verdict: yes, ~85% composition.** `reversal_calendar.db` already holds **278 yoga + lunar events reaching 2027-12-28** (199 auspicious yoga · 50 lunar · 29 nakshatra), with `events_in_range` and `coincidence_scores` ready to slice a week — 2–4 events in a typical week, so a card has content without being noise. [sector_card.py](tools/sector_card.py) already proves the `build_model` → `render_png`/`render_text` Pillow pattern, and the data **points forward**, which is what makes a *forecast* card possible rather than a retrospective one.
- ### The assessment found a live bug, and it decides the design.
  `astro_trading.get_lunar_phase` is wrong in **three independent ways**, and it is the only lunar path that actually runs because **PyEphem is not installed** so every call falls through to `_lunar_phase_approximate`:
  - **Epoch is not a new moon.** It anchors on 2024-01-29; the real one is 2024-02-09. Measured as a constant **~10.8-day offset** — true new moons compute to `cycle_day` 11.10 and 10.57 instead of ~0.
  - **Illumination is a quarter-cycle out of phase.** `100·sin(2π·c/29.53)·0.5+50` gives **50% at new, 100% at first quarter, 50% at full, 0% at last quarter**. Should be `50·(1−cos(2π·c/29.53))` → 0/50/100/50. As written the new moon and the full moon are both reported half-lit.
  - **Residual drift** even once fixed: a fixed 29.53-day mean month leaves 2026-08-12 reading `waning` at cycle 29.1, ~0.4 days short of wrapping.
  - Net effect: it calls the **2026-07-29 full moon "waning, 16.7% illuminated"** and 2026-08-08 "**new** at 99.3%" — internally self-contradictory. Meanwhile the DB has the correct PyEphem events sitting right beside it. **Two astro surfaces in one dashboard would disagree about the moon**, so the card must read the DB and the approximation should be deleted rather than repaired (**AW-0**).
- **A prerequisite with a deadline:** `ephem` is absent, so `lunar_phases.py` cannot rebuild the calendar. The DB covers to 2027-12-28 — ~17 months of runway, then `pip install ephem`.
- **The framing risk is recorded, not waved at.** [[analyses/Dashboard-UI-Roadmap]] Phase 3 already rules that Astro is a regime/context modifier and **never** a standalone entry signal. A shareable card is precisely the format that invites being read as a prediction, and it travels to places where that context is absent — so AW-4 puts the disclaimer, the event count and the DB coverage date **inside the PNG**, not on the page around it.
- **Nothing built; every probe read-only.** AW-0 is the exception to "good-to-have" — it is a wrong number on a shipped page today.

## [2026-08-02] build | SR-1…SR-5 SHIPPED — orbit chart off screener.in, and three bugs in the DS-2 parser
- New [ownership_cache.py](tools/ownership_cache.py) closes the ownership spine of [[analyses/Sector-Rotation-Orbit-Data-Source-Roadmap]]: an immutable `(symbol, period)` cache over NSE's shareholding XBRL, a resumable backfill, the `FII_<quarter>` wide-frame emitter, and per-quarter historical cap weighting. A **🛰 Rebuild from NSE filings** button on the Sector Map runs the chain end to end. **The manual screener.in export is no longer required for anything** — the legacy path is kept working rather than deleted.
- **The cache is the design.** A filing is immutable once filed, so a cached quarter is valid forever: first pass 8 symbols in **2.3 min**, re-run **0.3 min fetching 0 quarters**. Each symbol is written as it lands, so an interrupted backfill resumes instead of restarting.
- **SR-5 is not a refinement.** `NumberOfFullyPaidUpEquityShares` × the quarter-end close gives each quarter the cap it actually had. Across the first eight names caps moved **−49% (TCS)** to **+92% (HDFCBANK**, which ran a ~2:1 share increase mid-window**)** — weighting eight quarters by today's cap, the trail's own documented caveat, silently over-weights whatever has since re-rated.
- ### Backfilling found three real bugs in DS-2's parser. All were invisible to DS-2's own verification.
  - **① A 100× error on every older filing.** Filings up to ~mid-2025 encode percentages as percent (total 100.0), newer ones as fractions (total 1.0), and the parser multiplied by 100 unconditionally — RELIANCE's Jun-2025 promoter stake read **5007%** against a filed 50.07%. Scale is now read from the filing's own total; a file matching neither shape returns None rather than a guess. **DS-2 verified five symbols' *latest* filings — one vintage — so only pulling history could have exposed it.**
  - **② A typo in SEBI's own taxonomy.** Older vintages spell it `...PortfolioInvestorCat**er**goryOneMember`. Matching only the correct spelling returned `fii: None` for every pre-2025 filing. Alias tuples now.
  - **③ A false negative on zero-promoter companies.** Filers omit zero rows, so HDFCBANK parsed `promoter: None` against a master `pr_and_prgrp: 0` and five good quarters were called unverifiable. None-vs-0 now agrees **in the check only** — the stored value stays None, because an unfiled category is still not a measured zero.
- **The `reconciles` cross-check earned its keep**, and this is the entry to point at next time someone asks whether a redundant check is worth the code. It is what surfaced ①; non-reconciling rows ran **35/64 → 5/64 → 0/64** as the fixes landed. Rows failing it are now excluded from the frame rather than charted.
- **A silent overwrite caught before it shipped.** NSE returns event-driven filings beside quarterly ones (RELIANCE 29-OCT-2024, HDFCBANK 28-AUG-2025, INFY 04-DEC-2025); `quarter_label` maps them onto the same label as the true quarter end, so one silently overwrote the other in a dict-keyed frame. Quarter-ends only now, filtered *before* the top-N slice so `--quarters 8` means eight quarters, not eight filings. *Fifth entry in the running theme — and the first one caught by inspection rather than by a symptom.*
- **Verified:** 8-symbol frame → 19 columns (8 `FII_`, 8 `CAP_`, no nulls) → trail → 7 sectors × 7 points, every quarter on `historical` cap basis, every sector resolving an index, orbit PNG/HTML/XLSX written; dashboard button exercised live with zero exceptions. **Nifty 500 backfill running** (~112 min); checked mid-run at 17 symbols / 135 quarters with **0 non-reconciling**.

## [2026-08-02] build | SR-6 SHIPPED — a real Relative Rotation Graph, on price alone
- New [rrg.py](tools/rrg.py) + a **Relative Rotation Graph** on the Sector Map: RS-Ratio vs RS-Momentum against NIFTY 50, fading tails, quadrant shading, weekly/daily toggle, tail + z-window sliders. Reads only frames `cached_fetch_sector_data` already holds plus one benchmark fetch — **zero gated data, ships without the SR-2 backfill**, as [[analyses/Sector-Rotation-Orbit-Data-Source-Roadmap]] scoped it.
- **It is not the quadrant chart already on that page.** That one plots 1Y vs 1M **absolute** return, so in a market that rises together every sector piles into the top-right and it stops discriminating. An RRG measures against the benchmark and adds the second derivative, which is what makes four corners a *cycle* instead of a ranking. **Live proof:** Realty is the strongest sector over 3 months (**+17.3% vs NIFTY**) and reads **`Weakening`** — outperformed hard, momentum now decaying. A ranking cannot express that.
- **The formula is documented rather than passed off as the real thing.** JdK's RS-Ratio/RS-Momentum are proprietary; the module says it uses the standard public approximation (z-score of RS centred on 100), and records the caveat the chart itself cannot: **each sector is standardised against its own history**, so read quadrant and direction of travel, not coordinates compared across sectors.
- **The self-check caught a robustness bug the market would not have.** The z-score guarded against an *exactly* zero rolling std; on a smooth series the std collapses to ~**1e-14** instead, and dividing by that turns floating-point noise into a **±40 standard score** — a violently confident number computed from nothing. Now masked relative to the price level so it holds at any scale. *Fourth entry in this log's running theme: the dangerous failure is never the exception, it is the confident-looking output with nothing behind it.*
- **A finding worth keeping:** a *linearly* outperforming sector correctly reads `Weakening`, because the ratio of two straight lines is concave — relative strength decelerates. RS-Momentum is the change in the **standardised** ratio, not in raw relative strength. My first test asserted otherwise and the test was wrong, not the code.
- **Unmeasurable sectors are omitted and counted, never plotted at the 100/100 origin** — a sector we could not place must not render as one sitting neutrally in the middle.
- **Verified:** self-check passes (4 quadrant corners · accelerating→Leading · accelerating-decline→Lagging · too-short→skipped · no-benchmark→empty). Live **11/11 sectors, 0 skipped**, all points inside a sane 50–150 band, weekly and daily. On-page quadrant counts (Leading 2 · Weakening 4 · Lagging 5 · Improving 0) match the offline computation exactly; 3 Plotly charts; zero exceptions.
- **Does not replace the ownership axis.** SR-1…SR-5 stay open; [[concepts/FPV-Flow-Price-Volume-Ownership]] is explicit that reading price momentum for ownership flow is the mistake.

- **The bug:** `api/quote-equity` 403s for every symbol, it was the sole source of `Industry` and `Mar Cap`, and `build_dataset` skips any symbol missing either — so **Other Screeners → Pipeline → Sector Rotation had been producing an empty frame**, silently. Fixed in [nse_shareholding_fetch.py](tools/nse_shareholding_fetch.py) + new [nse_universe.py](tools/nse_universe.py). Full plan: [[analyses/Sector-Rotation-Orbit-Data-Source-Roadmap]].
- **Industry now comes from NSE's index-constituent archive CSV** (`ind_nifty500list.csv` — not behind the API gateway), cached 7 days on disk with an in-process memo and a **stale-cache fallback**: a week-old constituent list beats no universe at all, and the log says which one you got. Market cap comes from **yfinance `fast_info`**, still in ₹ crore so the CSV column and its readers are unchanged.
- **The taxonomy map was pulled forward from SR-1 on purpose.** SR-0 as written would have produced rows whose `Industry` keyed into nothing, and `classify_bias` renders that as `"unscored (no index data)"` — a pipeline that runs and emits an unusable table is not fixed. New `NSE_SECTOR_INDEX_MAP` maps **16 of NSE's 20 macro-sectors** onto the 11 indices; new `sector_rotation.index_for_industry()` tries the screener.in map **then** the NSE one, so **both upstreams work** and the legacy export path is untouched. `sector_rotation_trail` shares the helper, so the orbit chart resolves either.
- **Four sectors stay unmapped by choice** — Services, Diversified, Textiles, Media Entertainment & Publication (27 of 500 names). Coverage **473/500 = 94.6%**, zero unrecognised labels. That is `SECTOR_INDEX_MAP`'s own stated rule — *unmapped rather than guessed, to avoid silently misclassifying bias* — applied to the new vocabulary.
- **The failure mode itself is closed, not just this instance.** `build_dataset` now **raises** when every symbol fails, naming which lookup died (shareholding / industry / cap) with counts, and the dashboard renders it as `st.error`. It used to return an empty frame under a "No data fetched" warning — *indistinguishable from a quiet market, which is precisely how a dead endpoint stayed invisible.* **Third occurrence of this shape in the log**: DS-2's regexes that matched nothing, `build_dataset`'s empty frame, and now the 403 behind it.
- **Recorded so nobody re-walks it:** the shareholding-master row carries its own `industry` key that looks like a free route around the 403 — it is **`'-'` for all seven symbols tested**.
- **Verified live:** industry + cap resolve for all five probe symbols; `build_dataset` returns **5 rows where it returned 0**; every row aggregates and resolves to an index with no `unscored`; the all-fail path raises with an accurate breakdown; dashboard boots clean, zero exceptions. Cross-check: RELIANCE FII change **−1.48 pp**, matching what DS-2 recorded two entries down from a separate code path.
- **Next: SR-6** (price-only RRG tail off yfinance — zero gating, ships standalone), then the SR-2 ownership cache.

## [2026-08-01] plan | Sector-rotation orbit off screener.in — and a 403 that broke the pipeline
- **New roadmap: [[analyses/Sector-Rotation-Orbit-Data-Source-Roadmap]]** (SR-0…SR-7). Asked to repoint the Historical Sector Rotation Orbit chart at yfinance or Trendlyne "unless the data is gated".
- **The premise needed correcting.** yfinance **already is** the chart's source — for the **X axis** (`fetch_index_history`, trailing-1Y sector-index return). It carries **no Indian ownership data whatsoever**, so it can never supply the **Y axis** (cap-weighted Δ FII holding by Industry), which is the only gated half. The right primary was already sitting in the repo: the **SEBI shareholding XBRL** from DS-2.
- **Probing found a live regression, not just a roadmap.** `https://www.nseindia.com/api/quote-equity` returns **403 for 6 of 6 symbols** tested. That is the sole supplier of `Industry` and `Mar Cap` to `build_dataset`, which **skips any symbol where either is None** — so **Other Screeners → Pipeline → Sector Rotation is returning an empty frame right now**. *Exactly the failure shape this log recorded for DS-2 three entries down: not an error, just an empty result that looks like an answer.* Filed as **SR-0**, jumping the queue as a bug.
- **What is verified working (2026-08-01, live):** XBRL filing master returns **22 filings for RELIANCE, 28 for TATASTEEL**, quarterly back past Dec 2023 — deeper than the trail's current window; `fetch_ownership` reconciles (`totals_to_100`, promoter+public match master); `fetch_ownership(quarters=N)` already exposes the history API. **Nifty 500 constituent CSV is open** (HTTP 200, 500 rows, carries an `Industry` column). **yfinance `fast_info["market_cap"]`** works (RELIANCE ₹17.7 lakh cr).
- **Two traps recorded so nobody re-walks them:** the filing-master row has its own `industry` key that looks like it routes around the 403 — it is **`'-'` for all 7 symbols tested**. And the taxonomies are **disjoint**: NSE's 20 macro-sectors overlap the 97-key screener.in `SECTOR_INDEX_MAP` at **0 of 20**. A new 20→11 map is needed — cheaper than the 97 it replaces, and coarse labels map onto 11 indices more honestly than a 130-way vocabulary did.

Still the wrong primary: **1-decimal values on a quarter-over-quarter *delta*** put the rounding error at the same order as the signal; it is an aggregator where we hold the filing itself; and a requested RELIANCE URL **redirected to a different company**. Their Terms were not read — flagged before any systematic fetch. Demoted to optional backfill (**SR-7**).

It is **not** a substitute for the ownership axis; [[concepts/FPV-Flow-Price-Volume-Ownership]] is explicit that reading price momentum for ownership flow is the mistake.
